# M1 Implementation Plan: the model, A0, and the A1 go/no-go

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Build the CfC hybrid on PyTorch kernels, together with the data, eval, export and verdict machinery that lets the Kaggle chain run A0 and A1 unattended. Then hand over the runs that make the go/no-go call.

**Architecture:** The model is `deluge/model/`. Its mixers share one interface, and A1 is a config change. The recurrent mixers run a head-batched chunked scan in plain torch (`deluge/kernels/head_scan.py`). Training reads long documents in 4,097-token pieces. `loop.py` gains an in-run eval cadence and a final eval whose `final_eval.json` marks a run complete. A separate hourly workflow commits each run's results to the repo and its checkpoint to Hugging Face. A pure-function verdict module applies the pre-registered rules to those committed results.

**Tech Stack:** Python ≥ 3.10 (the local `.venv` is 3.14 with CPU torch 2.14; GitHub runners use 3.12; Kaggle has its own image). PyTorch (SDPA, autocast fp16, DataParallel), numpy, PyYAML and pytest. Data prep uses `huggingface_hub`, `pyarrow` and `tokenizers`. The workflows use the `kaggle` CLI 2.2.4 and `huggingface_hub` 1.33.0. Runs on GitHub Actions and Kaggle T4 ×2.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-m1-design.md`, approved 2026-10-05 (commit 3eaecc1 plus the status line). Read it alongside this plan: the plan says how, and the spec says why. Where they disagree, the spec wins; stop and ask.

## How to read this plan

- **Code blocks are real.** Every "Create" block is the whole file. Every "Modify" block is a unified diff against the file as the previous task left it. All of them come from a replay of this plan in a scratch clone of the repo. There, each task's new tests were run before its implementation (the "Expected: FAIL" lines are that output), and each task's commit passed the full suite (the counts in each task's last run step). Apply a diff by hand, or save it to a file and run `git apply`.
- `$PY` means `.venv/bin/python`, run from `/home/LAIN/dev/Deluge`.
- Tasks 1–15 are code and need nobody. Tasks 16–17 use Kaggle, GitHub and the local GPU, and each says exactly where it needs the user. Task 18 is the final review and the handoff.

## Global Constraints

- **Branch and checkout.** All work is on `m1` in the main checkout, `/home/LAIN/dev/Deluge`, not a worktree. `.venv`'s editable install points at the main checkout, so tests in a worktree would import the wrong files.
- **Outward-facing actions need the user's yes in this session.** That covers git push, merge, creating or pushing Kaggle kernels or datasets, anything on Hugging Face, and GitHub secrets. Read-only Kaggle and Hugging Face calls are fine. The local credentials are `KAGGLE_CONFIG_DIR=~/Downloads`.
- **The local GPU is for Task 17's short runs only.** The house shares one power bill.
- **Torch boundary.** torch is imported only by `deluge/model/`, `deluge/kernels/`, `deluge/train/trainer.py` (lazily, inside functions) and `deluge/train/smoke.py`. `deluge/config.py`, `deluge/train/{config,loop,monitors,checkpoint}.py`, `deluge/data/`, `deluge/chain/` and `deluge/eval/` stay importable without torch.
- **No Triton and no kernel registry in M1** (design D2). The mixers import `head_chunked_scan` directly.
- **No hyperparameter in Python.** Shapes and budgets live in YAML. The spec's pre-registered constants are named constants, and nothing configures them: `SCAN_CHUNK = 64`, `GATE_BIAS = 1.278`, `STD = 0.02`, `PIECE_LEN = 4097`, `SUBSET_PIECES = 488`, `BUCKETS = ((0, 512), (512, 1536), (1536, 4096))`, noise floor `0.01`, late-divergence tolerance `0.02`, decay bounds `0.5` / `0.9999`, skip bound `1%`, export grace `3 h`.
- **Shapes.** `seq_len` is 4096, and `global_batch_tokens` is 262,144 (64 sequences). Training is fp16 on the T4. The scan runs in fp32 with autocast off. Seeds are 1234, 4321 and 7777.
- **Parameter counts** (design §4.5), exact: A0 110,017,644; conv 110,091,336; LRU 109,945,068; transformer 110,070,528.
- **The dataset** is `heylaine/deluge-long4k`, and runs reference it as `data: heylaine/deluge-long4k:train.bin`. Run names are as in design §8.2.
- **Commits.** Messages read `<area>: <summary>`, with the areas `git log` uses: kernels, config, train, data, model, chain, eval, scripts, docs. Every message ends with these two lines:
  ```
  Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
  Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_014aUsWk2mR3EEzk211iLJE2
  ```
- **Style.** Module docstrings say *why*. Comments go on non-obvious lines. Error messages name the fix. Match the surrounding code.

## Review Focus

1. **A truncated `train.bin` in piece mode** (an interrupted download). A reasonable person expects the stream to read only whole pieces and never a sequence that runs off the end. Pinned by `test_a_partial_final_piece_is_never_read` (Task 5).
2. **A session killed during the final eval.** A reasonable person expects the next session to run only the eval and exit 0, rather than retrain, exit 2 forever, or lose the run. Pinned by `test_a_resume_with_the_budget_spent_runs_only_the_final_eval` (Task 9).
3. **A held-out set whose last batch is ragged** (5,858 pieces at `micro_batch` 4, or one piece across two GPUs). A reasonable person expects every piece to be counted exactly once, and the final eval not to crash. Pinned by `test_the_evaluator_reads_every_piece_including_a_ragged_last_batch` (Task 10). On two GPUs, DataParallel copies keyword arguments to every replica, so a last batch of one piece would call the second replica with keywords and no inputs. The evaluator therefore passes `"none"` positionally, pinned by `test_the_evaluator_passes_nothing_by_keyword` (Task 10). No CPU test can run DataParallel's scatter.
4. **The Kaggle CLI reporting output paths nested (`runs/<name>/x`) or flat.** A reasonable person expects the exporter to find the files either way. Pinned by `test_results_are_written_from_the_last_sessions_kernel[True/False]` (Task 13), and Task 13 Step 1 checks the real CLI.
5. **A document that passes the GPT-2 pre-filter but tokenizes to fewer than 4,097 tokens.** A reasonable person expects it to be counted and dropped, never written as a short piece. Pinned by `test_documents_under_one_piece_are_counted_and_dropped` (Task 11).

## Choices the spec left open (made here; veto at review)

- **Sink K/V start at zero.** A zero sink has logit 0 and contributes nothing, so attention starts as softmax with `n_sink` extra units in the denominator. Section 4.4 lists every other init and is silent on sinks.
- **The final eval runs as soon as the budget is spent**, even past the session deadline. It takes minutes, and the deadline leaves an hour of margin.
- **An overdue export fails every hourly run until it is fixed**, not just once. The spec says only "fails once a run has been done for more than ~3 h".
- **`prepare_data.py` becomes three subcommands:** `measure`, `long`, and `stream`, which is the old behaviour unchanged. The Kaggle prep kernel is rendered by a committed script (`scripts/kaggle_prep.py`), so how the dataset was made stays in the repo.
- **The model refuses what M1 does not build.** It raises `NotImplementedError` on MoD, MTP, chunk summaries or untied embeddings rather than ignoring them. Mixers without Δ raise if given one, and attention raises if given a state.
- **The exporter's runner pins `huggingface_hub==1.33.0`**, the last 1.x release: `create_repo` and `upload_folder` are the 1.x API.
- **A rerun stands in for its role by flag.** The design reruns a broken run under a new name. `scripts/m1_verdict.py --run m1-a0-xfmr=m1-a0-xfmr-2` reads the rerun in that role's place. The tables name every run they read, and only `m1-*` runs may stand in.
- **The binding A1 verdict does not wait on the early read.** An early run (`m1-a1s-*`) that never exported is left out of the early table, so a skipped early run cannot block the verdict.

### Task 1: Mask the reference scan before the exp

The reference `chunked_scan` returns NaN at λ = 1.5 with chunk 64 (design §5): above the diagonal `exp(L_t − L_s)` overflows, and inf × 0 is NaN. Task 2's scan is held to this reference, so the reference is fixed first.

**Files:**
- Modify: `deluge/kernels/reference/scan.py` (`chunked_scan` and its docstring)
- Test: `tests/test_scan_parity.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing new.
- Produces: `chunked_scan(log_a, bv, h0, chunk)`, with its signature unchanged; now finite for any log a ≤ 0.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

**Modify `tests/test_scan_parity.py`:**

```diff
--- a/tests/test_scan_parity.py
+++ b/tests/test_scan_parity.py
@@ -167,3 +167,21 @@ def test_rejects_a_non_positive_chunk():
     log_a, bv, h0 = make()
     with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="chunk"):
         chunked_scan(log_a, bv, h0, 0)
+
+
+@pytest.mark.parametrize("lam", [1.5, 5.0])
+def test_fast_decay_stays_finite_forwards_and_backwards(lam):
+    # M1 review finding 1: above the diagonal exp(L_t - L_s) is positive.
+    # Masking it after the exp let it overflow to inf once a channel decayed
+    # faster than a ~ 0.24 across a 64-token chunk, and inf * 0 is NaN in both
+    # passes -- one short-memory head would have read as "CfC is unstable".
+    def run(scan, **kwargs):
+        log_a, bv, h0 = make(time=128, log_a_min=-lam, log_a_max=-lam,
+                             requires_grad=True)
+        states, last = scan(log_a, bv, h0, **kwargs)
+        (states.sum() + last.sum()).backward()
+        return states.detach(), log_a.grad, bv.grad, h0.grad
+
+    for reference, candidate in zip(run(sequential_scan), run(chunked_scan, chunk=64)):
+        assert torch.isfinite(candidate).all()
+        torch.testing.assert_close(candidate, reference, rtol=1e-4, atol=1e-5)
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it to see it fail**

Run: `$PY -m pytest tests/test_scan_parity.py -q`
Expected: `2 failed, 26 passed`, namely `test_fast_decay_stays_finite_forwards_and_backwards[1.5]` and `[5.0]` (NaN in the chunked scan).

- [ ] **Step 3: Fix the scan**

**Modify `deluge/kernels/reference/scan.py`:**

```diff
--- a/deluge/kernels/reference/scan.py
+++ b/deluge/kernels/reference/scan.py
@@ -69,7 +69,8 @@ def chunked_scan(log_a: torch.Tensor, bv: torch.Tensor, h0: torch.Tensor,
     as a ratio exp(L_t) / exp(L_s). They are algebraically identical; the ratio
     is not usable, because exp(L_s) underflows to zero a few hundred tokens into
     a chunk at a = 0.9 and the division then produces infinities. Since
-    log a <= 0, every exp(L_t - L_s) here is in (0, 1].
+    log a <= 0, every exp(L_t - L_s) with s <= t is in (0, 1]. The s > t
+    exponents are positive, and are masked to -inf before the exp (below).
 
     This materialises the chunk x chunk decay matrix, which the Triton kernel
     will hold in registers one tile at a time. Memory is O(batch * chunk^2 *
@@ -90,11 +91,16 @@ def chunked_scan(log_a: torch.Tensor, bv: torch.Tensor, h0: torch.Tensor,
         width = stop - start
 
         cumulative = torch.cumsum(log_a_c, dim=1)                  # [B, C, D]
-        # decay[b, t, s, d] = exp(L_t - L_s), the decay applied to bv_s by time t
-        decay = torch.exp(cumulative.unsqueeze(2) - cumulative.unsqueeze(1))
+        # decay[b, t, s, d] = exp(L_t - L_s), the decay applied to bv_s by time t.
+        # Above the diagonal (s > t) the exponent is positive, so it is set to
+        # -inf *before* the exp. Masking after it lets the exp overflow once a
+        # channel decays fast (a below ~0.24 across a 64-token chunk), and
+        # inf * 0 is NaN forwards and backwards.
+        exponent = cumulative.unsqueeze(2) - cumulative.unsqueeze(1)
         causal = torch.tril(torch.ones(width, width, dtype=torch.bool,
                                        device=log_a.device))
-        decay = decay * causal[None, :, :, None]
+        decay = torch.exp(exponent.masked_fill(~causal[None, :, :, None],
+                                               float("-inf")))
 
         carried = torch.exp(cumulative) * h_prev.unsqueeze(1)
         within = (decay * bv_c.unsqueeze(1)).sum(dim=2)
```

### Task 2: The head-batched scan

The training scan (design §5). λ is per head, so within a chunk one [C, C] decay matrix serves all of a head's channels. The intra-chunk work is one batched matmul, a 64-step loop carries state between chunks, and everything runs in fp32 with autocast off.

**Files:**
- Create: `deluge/kernels/head_scan.py`
- Test: `tests/test_head_scan.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `sequential_scan` (the reference, in tests only).
- Produces: `head_chunked_scan(log_a: [B, T, H], bv: [B, T, H*P], h0: [B, H*P], chunk: int) -> (states: [B, T, H*P] fp32, h_last: [B, H*P] fp32)`. Channels are head-major: head j owns channels j·P … (j+1)·P − 1. log a ≤ 0 is the caller's invariant, and the function does not check it.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

**Create `tests/test_head_scan.py`:**

```python
"""The head-batched scan must equal the sequential reference, forwards and backwards.

head_chunked_scan is the scan every CfC and LRU layer trains on in M1, so this
file is to it what test_scan_parity.py is to the reference chunked scan. The
reference takes log a per channel; per-head log a is expanded to it with
repeat_interleave, which is exactly the broadcast spec 3 describes.
"""

import pytest
import torch

from deluge.kernels.head_scan import head_chunked_scan
from deluge.kernels.reference.scan import sequential_scan

# Spec 3's init range: a in [0.9, 0.999] at Delta=1.
LOG_A_MIN, LOG_A_MAX = -0.10536, -0.001
HEADS, PER_HEAD = 3, 4


def make(batch=2, time=100, seed=0, log_a_min=LOG_A_MIN, log_a_max=LOG_A_MAX,
         requires_grad=False):
    generator = torch.Generator().manual_seed(seed)
    span = log_a_max - log_a_min
    log_a = log_a_min + span * torch.rand(batch, time, HEADS, generator=generator)
    bv = torch.randn(batch, time, HEADS * PER_HEAD, generator=generator)
    h0 = torch.randn(batch, HEADS * PER_HEAD, generator=generator)
    if requires_grad:
        for tensor in (log_a, bv, h0):
            tensor.requires_grad_(True)
    return log_a, bv, h0


def reference(log_a, bv, h0):
    return sequential_scan(log_a.repeat_interleave(PER_HEAD, dim=-1), bv, h0)


@pytest.mark.parametrize("chunk", [1, 16, 64])
@pytest.mark.parametrize("time", [1, 63, 64, 100, 128])
def test_matches_the_sequential_scan(chunk, time):
    # time 63, 100: a ragged final chunk, padded with log a = 0 and bv = 0.
    log_a, bv, h0 = make(time=time)
    expected, expected_last = reference(log_a, bv, h0)
    got, got_last = head_chunked_scan(log_a, bv, h0, chunk)
    torch.testing.assert_close(got, expected, rtol=1e-5, atol=1e-5)
    torch.testing.assert_close(got_last, expected_last, rtol=1e-5, atol=1e-5)


@pytest.mark.parametrize("chunk", [16, 64])
def test_gradients_match_the_sequential_scan(chunk):
    def grads(scan, **kwargs):
        log_a, bv, h0 = make(requires_grad=True)
        states, last = scan(log_a, bv, h0, **kwargs)
        weights = torch.arange(1, states.shape[1] + 1)[None, :, None]
        ((states * weights).sum() + last.sum()).backward()
        return log_a.grad, bv.grad, h0.grad

    for expected, got in zip(grads(reference), grads(head_chunked_scan, chunk=chunk)):
        torch.testing.assert_close(got, expected, rtol=1e-4, atol=1e-5)


def test_long_chunks_do_not_underflow():
    # exp(L_s) is below float32's smallest normal a few hundred tokens into a
    # chunk at a = 0.9; the difference-of-logs form never forms it.
    log_a, bv, h0 = make(time=512, log_a_min=-0.105, log_a_max=-0.105)
    states, last = head_chunked_scan(log_a, bv, h0, 512)
    assert torch.isfinite(states).all() and torch.isfinite(last).all()
    torch.testing.assert_close(states, reference(log_a, bv, h0)[0], rtol=1e-4, atol=1e-5)


def test_fast_decay_stays_finite_forwards_and_backwards():
    # lambda = 5 across a 64-token chunk: the above-diagonal exponent reaches
    # 5 * 63 = 315, far past exp's float32 overflow at ~88.7.
    def run(scan, **kwargs):
        log_a, bv, h0 = make(time=128, log_a_min=-5.0, log_a_max=-5.0, requires_grad=True)
        states, last = scan(log_a, bv, h0, **kwargs)
        (states.sum() + last.sum()).backward()
        return states.detach(), log_a.grad, bv.grad, h0.grad

    for expected, got in zip(run(reference), run(head_chunked_scan, chunk=64)):
        assert torch.isfinite(got).all()
        torch.testing.assert_close(got, expected, rtol=1e-4, atol=1e-5)


def test_the_last_state_is_the_last_row():
    log_a, bv, h0 = make(time=100)
    states, last = head_chunked_scan(log_a, bv, h0, 64)
    assert torch.equal(states[:, -1], last)


def test_runs_in_fp32_under_autocast():
    # Spec 3: a is materialised in fp32. A bf16 cumulative sum near a = 1
    # would lose the time constant the model is learning.
    log_a, bv, h0 = make()
    expected, _ = head_chunked_scan(log_a, bv, h0, 16)
    with torch.autocast("cpu", dtype=torch.bfloat16):
        got, _ = head_chunked_scan(log_a, bv, h0, 16)
    assert got.dtype == torch.float32
    torch.testing.assert_close(got, expected)


def test_rejects_a_width_that_does_not_split_into_heads():
    log_a, bv, h0 = make()
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="channels per head"):
        head_chunked_scan(log_a, bv[..., :-1], h0[..., :-1], 16)


def test_rejects_a_non_positive_chunk():
    log_a, bv, h0 = make()
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="chunk"):
        head_chunked_scan(log_a, bv, h0, 0)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the scan**

**Create `deluge/kernels/head_scan.py`:**

```python
"""Head-batched chunked scan: the training scan of M1, in plain PyTorch.

    h_t = a_t * h_{t-1} + bv_t,   a_t = exp(log_a_t), one log_a per decay head

kernels/reference/scan.py takes log a per channel and materialises a
[B, C, C, D] decay tensor per chunk. That is right for a reference and unfit
for training at 4096 tokens. Spec 3 makes lambda per head, so within a chunk
one [C, C] decay matrix serves all P channels of a head, and the intra-chunk
work for the whole sequence is one batched matmul (SSD-style):

  1. intra-chunk, every chunk at once: decay[b, h, n, t, s] = exp(L_t - L_s)
     for s <= t, with L the within-chunk cumulative sum of log a, and
     within = decay @ bv
  2. inter-chunk: a loop over the N chunks carries the chunk-end state forward
  3. combine: states = within + exp(L_t) * h_chunk_start

Same numerics as the reference: fp32 with autocast off, and decay formed as a
difference of logs, never a ratio. tests/test_head_scan.py holds it to
sequential_scan forwards and backwards. A Triton kernel replaces it at M3
(M1 design D2); until then it is the only training scan, and the mixers import
it directly -- the kernel registry arrives with a second implementation.
"""

import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F

__all__ = ["head_chunked_scan"]


def head_chunked_scan(log_a: torch.Tensor, bv: torch.Tensor, h0: torch.Tensor,
                      chunk: int) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    """The recurrence with per-head decay, parallel within chunks.

    log_a: [batch, time, heads], <= 0. bv: [batch, time, width] with width a
    whole number of channels per head, laid out head-major (head j owns
    channels j*P .. (j+1)*P - 1). h0: [batch, width].
    Returns (states [batch, time, width], h after the last t [batch, width]),
    both fp32.

    log_a <= 0 is the caller's invariant (log_a = -Delta * softplus(...)) and
    is not checked here: the check is a GPU sync per layer per step.
    """
    _check(log_a, bv, h0, chunk)
    batch, time, heads = log_a.shape
    width = bv.shape[-1]
    per_head = width // heads

    with torch.autocast(device_type=log_a.device.type, enabled=False):
        log_a, bv, h = log_a.float(), bv.float(), h0.float()
        pad = (-time) % chunk
        if pad:
            # log a = 0 and bv = 0 leave the state where it is, so padding the
            # ragged final chunk cannot move any real position.
            log_a = F.pad(log_a, (0, 0, 0, pad))
            bv = F.pad(bv, (0, 0, 0, pad))
        n = (time + pad) // chunk

        la = log_a.reshape(batch, n, chunk, heads).permute(0, 3, 1, 2)            # [B,H,N,C]
        x = bv.reshape(batch, n, chunk, heads, per_head).permute(0, 3, 1, 2, 4)   # [B,H,N,C,P]
        cumulative = torch.cumsum(la, dim=-1)                                     # L_t
        exponent = cumulative.unsqueeze(-1) - cumulative.unsqueeze(-2)           # [B,H,N,C,C]
        causal = torch.tril(torch.ones(chunk, chunk, dtype=torch.bool, device=la.device))
        # -inf before the exp, never a mask after it: above the diagonal the
        # exponent is positive and overflows (the reference scan's old NaN).
        decay = torch.exp(exponent.masked_fill(~causal, float("-inf")))
        within = decay @ x                                                        # [B,H,N,C,P]

        carry = torch.exp(cumulative[..., -1])                                    # [B,H,N]
        state = h.reshape(batch, heads, per_head)
        starts = []
        for j in range(n):
            starts.append(state)
            state = carry[:, :, j, None] * state + within[:, :, j, -1]
        start = torch.stack(starts, dim=2)                                        # [B,H,N,P]

        states = within + torch.exp(cumulative).unsqueeze(-1) * start.unsqueeze(3)
        states = states.permute(0, 2, 3, 1, 4).reshape(batch, n * chunk, width)[:, :time]
    return states, states[:, -1]


def _check(log_a: torch.Tensor, bv: torch.Tensor, h0: torch.Tensor, chunk: int) -> None:
    if log_a.ndim != 3 or bv.ndim != 3:
        raise ValueError(f"expected log_a [batch, time, heads] and bv [batch, time, "
                         f"width], got {tuple(log_a.shape)} and {tuple(bv.shape)}")
    if log_a.shape[:2] != bv.shape[:2]:
        raise ValueError(f"log_a {tuple(log_a.shape)} and bv {tuple(bv.shape)} must "
                         f"agree on batch and time")
    heads, width = log_a.shape[2], bv.shape[2]
    if width % heads:
        raise ValueError(f"bv width ({width}) must be a whole number of channels per "
                         f"head ({heads} heads)")
    if h0.shape != (bv.shape[0], width):
        raise ValueError(f"h0 {tuple(h0.shape)} must be [batch, width] = "
                         f"{(bv.shape[0], width)}")
    if chunk < 1:
        raise ValueError(f"chunk must be at least 1, got {chunk}")
```

```bash

git commit -F - <<'EOF'
kernels: head-batched chunked scan, the M1 training scan

Per-head decay shares one [C, C] matrix across a head's channels, so the
intra-chunk work is one batched matmul. fp32 with autocast off, decay as
a difference of logs masked before the exp; held to sequential_scan
forwards and backwards.
```

### Task 3: Config: `b_g`, the final norm, the attention mixer, the M1 arms

Design §4.5 and §8.1. Three changes reach the instantiated model: the gate bias `b_g` (D7) is costed in CfC and the LRU, the final RMSNorm is costed, and `mixer.type: attention` exists for the transformer. The A1 arms are re-based on A0, so every M1 arm is window-only and matched to A0's 110.0M.

**Files:**
- Modify: `deluge/config.py`
- Modify: `configs/ablations/A0_scan_only.yaml`, `configs/ablations/A1_conv_baseline.yaml`, `configs/ablations/A1_lru_baseline.yaml`
- Create: `configs/ablations/A0_transformer_baseline.yaml`
- Test: `tests/test_config_invariants.py`. Two tests are replaced: `test_ablation_configs_differ_from_dev_only_in_the_mixer` and `test_a1_arms_are_parameter_matched_to_the_cfc_arm` compare against `dev.yaml` and would fail once the arms are re-based.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing new.
- Produces:
  - `AttentionMixer`: a dataclass with no fields; `state_width == 0` and `uses_delta is False`; registered as `MIXER_TYPES["attention"]`.
  - `ParamCount.final_norm: int`, which both `total` and `active_total` include.
  - `ModelConfig._mixer_layer_params() -> int`, which costs one C-slot layer for `params()` and `_mtp_params()` alike.
  - `ModelConfig` raises `ConfigError` mentioning `summary_sources` for an attention mixer with non-empty `summary_sources`.

```diff
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class AttentionMixer:
+    """Attention in the C slots: A0's matched transformer (M1 design D3).
+
+    No fields. The heads, window and sinks are the model's, shared with the A
+    slots, so an all-attention stack is one layer repeated. There is no
+    params(): a mixer is costed from d_model alone, and attention needs the
+    head config, so ModelConfig._mixer_layer_params costs it instead.

+    @property

+        return 0
+
+    @property

+        return False
+
+
+MIXER_TYPES = {"cfc": CfCMixer, "gated_conv": GatedConvMixer, "lru": LRUMixer,
+               "attention": AttentionMixer}
```

**Create `configs/ablations/A0_transformer_baseline.yaml`:**

```yaml
# A0's matched transformer (M1 design D3): this stack with attention in every
# slot. Read against A0_scan_only.yaml; at M3 the same config becomes B1.
#
# Three things differ from A0, and the suite asserts nothing else does:
#   - mixer: attention, so all 12 layers are the hybrid's own attention layer
#     (GQA 12/4, RoPE theta 1M, 4 learned sinks) -- not a tuned transformer's;
#   - window: 4096, the whole training sequence, so every layer is full causal
#     attention. The hybrid's A slots keep W = 512;
#   - d_ff: 2408, the matching knob. Each of the nine C slots becomes a 1.58M
#     attention layer in place of a 2.68M CfC layer, 9.90M in all; widening
#     every FFN by 360 puts 9.95M back. The total is 110.07M, +0.048% against
#     A0's 110.02M -- the transformer is slightly the larger, so any mismatch
#     works against CfC. 2408 is a multiple of 8, for fp16 tensor cores.
extends: A0_scan_only.yaml

mixer:
  type: attention

window: 4096

ffn:
  type: dense
  d_ff: 2408
```

```diff
-# projections against CfC's two plus a tiny W_f), and A1 would be comparing size
-# as much as architecture -- in the direction that favours the baseline.
+# d_inner is 872, not 1152, so that total parameters match the CfC arm to
+# within 0.07% (110.09M against 110.02M). At equal width the conv's three input
+# projections outweigh CfC's two plus a tiny W_f, and A1 would compare size as
+# much as architecture -- in the direction that favours the baseline. 872 is a

+# larger, so any mismatch works against CfC.
 #

-# mixer width. Two things follow:

-extends: ../model/dev.yaml
```

```diff
-# A1 / B4: plain LRU -- decay and state, but no Delta argument and no conv.
-# Isolates what the Delta-aware decay and the short conv are each worth.
-extends: ../model/dev.yaml
+# A1 / B4: plain LRU -- CfC-mix without the short conv and without the Delta
+# argument. Everything else is A0_scan_only.yaml -- the suite asserts that.
+#
+# At A1 Delta is always 1 (no MoD), so CfC's Delta does nothing and this arm
+# measures the conv alone: CfC against LRU is what DWConv_k4 is worth. Whether
+# Delta does work is A3's question, asked with MoD on.
+#
+# Same d_inner as CfC: dropping the conv and its buffer leaves the arm 0.07%
+# smaller, inside the 0.5% match the suite asserts.
+extends: A0_scan_only.yaml
 
 mixer:
   type: lru
```

### Task 4: Train budgets: 4096 tokens, the eval cadence, three seeds, the rehearsal

Design §7.1 and §8.1. `eval_every_tokens` is a new required `TrainConfig` field. `_build` rejects missing keys, so `base.yaml` sets it to `null` and every fixture gains it. It stays out of the fingerprint, like `checkpoint_every_tokens`.

**Files:**
- Modify: `deluge/train/config.py`
- Modify: `configs/train/base.yaml`, `configs/train/screen.yaml`, `configs/train/dev.yaml`
- Create: `configs/train/screen_seed2.yaml`, `configs/train/dev_seed2.yaml`, `configs/train/dev_seed3.yaml`, `configs/train/rehearse.yaml`
- Test: `tests/test_train_config.py`; fixtures in `tests/test_torch_backend.py` and `tests/test_resume.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing new.
- Produces:
  - `TrainConfig.eval_every_tokens: Optional[int]` (positive, or None).
  - `TrainConfig.eval_every_steps -> Optional[int]`: None when evals are off, else `max(1, eval_every_tokens // global_batch_tokens)`.
  - `base.yaml` sets `micro_batch: 4` provisionally; Task 17 measures the real value.

```diff
+# ---- M1: 4096-token sequences, held-out evals, seeds ------------------------ #
+
+def test_a_step_is_sixty_four_sequences_of_4096():
+    # M1 design D5 and 8.1: the step count and the LR schedule are unchanged
+    # from 1024, because a step is still 262,144 tokens.
+    cfg = load_train_config(REPO / "configs/train/base.yaml")
+    assert cfg.seq_len == 4096 and cfg.sequences_per_step == 64
+    # micro_batch splits over two T4s and must divide the step's 64 sequences:
+    # a power of two, not merely even.
+    assert cfg.micro_batch & (cfg.micro_batch - 1) == 0 and 64 % cfg.micro_batch == 0
+
+
+def test_rejects_a_non_positive_eval_cadence():
+    with pytest.raises(ConfigError, match="eval_every_tokens"):
+        make(eval_every_tokens=0)
+
+
+def test_no_eval_cadence_means_no_in_run_evals():
+    assert make(eval_every_tokens=None).eval_every_steps is None
+
+
+@pytest.mark.parametrize("name, every", [("screen", 190), ("dev", 381)])
+def test_evals_land_on_checkpoint_steps(name, every):
+    # When both are due the eval runs first, so a kill during it reruns the
+    # eval on resume rather than skipping past it (deluge/train/loop.py).
+    cfg = load_train_config(REPO / f"configs/train/{name}.yaml")
+    assert cfg.eval_every_steps == every
+    assert cfg.eval_every_steps % cfg.checkpoint_every_steps == 0
+
+
+def test_the_rehearsal_saves_evaluates_and_finishes():
+    # Batch 0 must exercise a save, an in-run eval and the final eval.
+    cfg = load_train_config(REPO / "configs/train/rehearse.yaml")
+    assert cfg.total_steps == 80
+    assert cfg.warmup_tokens == 10 * cfg.global_batch_tokens
+    assert cfg.checkpoint_every_steps == 20 and cfg.eval_every_steps == 40

+    ("screen_seed2", "screen", 4321), ("dev_seed2", "dev", 4321), ("dev_seed3", "dev", 7777),
+])
+def test_a_seed_config_changes_only_the_seed(seeded, budget, seed):
+    one = load_train_config(REPO / f"configs/train/{seeded}.yaml")
+    base = load_train_config(REPO / f"configs/train/{budget}.yaml")
+    assert one.seed == seed
+    assert dataclasses.replace(one, seed=base.seed) == base
+
+
+def test_the_three_seeds_share_no_epoch_ordering():
+    # TokenStream keys epoch e's order as seed + e. Seeds whose keys collide
+    # would see the same data order in some epoch, and their difference would
+    # understate the noise the verdicts are built on.
+    seeds = [load_train_config(REPO / f"configs/train/{name}.yaml").seed
+             for name in ("dev", "dev_seed2", "dev_seed3")]
+    assert seeds == [1234, 4321, 7777]
+    keys = [{seed + epoch for epoch in range(100)} for seed in seeds]
+    assert not (keys[0] & keys[1] or keys[0] & keys[2] or keys[1] & keys[2])
+
```

```diff
 # environment, not hyperparameters, and differ between Kaggle and local. They
 # are CLI arguments so that a config file is portable between the two.
 
-seq_len: 1024
+# 4096 because A1's claim is about long-range state (M1 design D5): at 1024 the
+# stacked 512-token windows of blocks 4, 8 and 12 already span the sequence,
+# and A1 would test only local mixing. The data comes in pieces of

+seq_len: 4096

 # and grad_accum is derived from the two. Moving between a T4 and a 4090 should

+global_batch_tokens: 262144    # 64 sequences of 4096

+micro_batch: 4                 # 16 accumulation steps
```

### Task 5: Piece-mode data reading

Design §6.2. A token file with a `provenance.json` beside it that names `piece_len` is read in pieces: sequence i starts at `i · piece_len`, so every target stays inside its document. The file layout is that of Task 11's prep. `piece_batches` is the held-out reader that Task 10 uses.

**Files:**
- Modify: `deluge/data/stream.py`, `deluge/data/__init__.py`
- Test: `tests/test_data.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing new.
- Produces:
  - `deluge.data.PROVENANCE == "provenance.json"`, and `load_provenance(data_path) -> Optional[dict]`, which reads the `provenance.json` beside a token file.
  - `TokenStream(tokens, seq_len, seed, piece_len=None)`, with attributes `piece_len` and `stride`. It raises `ValueError("the data is in pieces of N tokens ...")` unless `piece_len == seq_len + 1`.
  - `TokenStream.from_path(path, seq_len, seed)` takes `piece_len` from the provenance, when there is one.
  - `piece_batches(tokens, piece_len, n_pieces: Optional[int], batch: int)` yields `(inputs, targets)`, int64 `[≤ batch, piece_len − 1]`, for pieces 0 … n − 1 in file order.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

**Modify `tests/test_data.py`:**

```diff
--- a/tests/test_data.py
+++ b/tests/test_data.py
@@ -7,7 +7,7 @@ so a resumed run can jump straight to step N without replaying anything.
 import numpy as np
 import pytest
 
-from deluge.data import TokenStream, permute
+from deluge.data import TokenStream, permute, piece_batches
 
 CORPUS = np.arange(10_000, dtype=np.uint16)
 
@@ -97,3 +97,68 @@ def test_reads_a_memmapped_file(tmp_path):
     assert s.n_sequences == stream().n_sequences
     assert np.array_equal(s.batch(s.indices_for_step(0, 4))[0],
                           stream().batch(stream().indices_for_step(0, 4))[0])
+
+
+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
+# Piece mode (M1 design 6.2): whole documents cut into pieces of seq_len + 1
+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
+
+PIECE = 65                                   # seq_len 64 + the shifted target
+
+
+def pieces(n=10):
+    return np.arange(n * PIECE, dtype=np.uint16)
+
+
+def test_piece_mode_keeps_every_target_inside_its_piece():
+    s = TokenStream(pieces(), seq_len=64, seed=3, piece_len=PIECE)
+    for i in range(s.n_sequences):
+        inputs, targets = s.batch(np.array([i]))
+        start = s.start_of(i)
+        assert start % PIECE == 0
+        assert inputs[0, 0] == start and targets[0, -1] == start + PIECE - 1
+
+
+def test_piece_mode_reads_every_piece_once_per_epoch():
+    s = TokenStream(pieces(), seq_len=64, seed=3, piece_len=PIECE)
+    assert s.n_sequences == 10
+    assert sorted(s.start_of(i) for i in range(10)) == [i * PIECE for i in range(10)]
+
+
+def test_a_partial_final_piece_is_never_read():
+    # A truncated train.bin must not yield a sequence that runs off its end.
+    s = TokenStream(pieces()[:-7], seq_len=64, seed=3, piece_len=PIECE)
+    assert s.n_sequences == 9
+    assert max(s.start_of(i) for i in range(9)) + PIECE <= len(s.tokens)
+
+
+def test_rejects_pieces_that_do_not_fit_the_sequence_length():
+    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="pieces of 65"):
+        TokenStream(pieces(), seq_len=32, seed=3, piece_len=PIECE)
+
+
+def test_from_path_reads_the_piece_length_from_provenance(tmp_path):
+    path = tmp_path / "train.bin"
+    pieces().tofile(path)
+    (tmp_path / "provenance.json").write_text('{"piece_len": 65}')
+    assert TokenStream.from_path(path, seq_len=64, seed=3).n_sequences == 10
+
+
+def test_without_provenance_the_stream_is_contiguous_as_before(tmp_path):
+    path = tmp_path / "tokens.bin"
+    pieces().tofile(path)
+    s = TokenStream.from_path(path, seq_len=64, seed=3)
+    assert s.piece_len is None and s.n_sequences == (650 - 1) // 64
+
+
+def test_piece_batches_reads_pieces_in_file_order():
+    batches = list(piece_batches(pieces(), PIECE, n_pieces=5, batch=2))
+    assert [len(inputs) for inputs, _ in batches] == [2, 2, 1]
+    inputs, targets = batches[2]
+    assert inputs[0, 0] == 4 * PIECE and targets[0, -1] == 5 * PIECE - 1
+    assert inputs.dtype == np.int64
+
+
+def test_piece_batches_stops_at_the_last_whole_piece():
+    assert sum(len(i) for i, _ in piece_batches(pieces()[:-1], PIECE, None, 4)) == 9
+    assert sum(len(i) for i, _ in piece_batches(pieces(), PIECE, 50, 4)) == 10
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Add piece mode**

**Modify `deluge/data/stream.py`:**

```diff
--- a/deluge/data/stream.py
+++ b/deluge/data/stream.py
@@ -9,10 +9,16 @@ trainer asks for step N's sequences and gets them without having touched steps
 
 numpy only, no torch: the ordering is arithmetic, and keeping it torch-free
 means the resume logic is testable without a GPU or a model.
+
+Two layouts. A token file with a provenance.json beside it that names a
+piece_len is in pieces: whole runs of seq_len + 1 tokens, each cut from one
+document (scripts/prepare_data.py long). Without one it is a single flat stream
+(synthetic data, the chain rehearsal) and sequences are cut every seq_len.
 """
 
+import json
 from pathlib import Path
-from typing import Union
+from typing import Any, Dict, Iterator, Optional, Tuple, Union
 
 import numpy as np
 
@@ -23,6 +29,16 @@ _M64 = (1 << 64) - 1
 # exceeds 65535.
 TOKEN_DTYPE = np.uint16
 
+PROVENANCE = "provenance.json"
+
+
+def load_provenance(data_path: Union[str, Path]) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
+    """The provenance.json next to a token file, or None if there is none."""
+    path = Path(data_path).parent / PROVENANCE
+    if not path.exists():
+        return None
+    return json.loads(path.read_text())
+
 
 def _mix(x: int) -> int:
     """splitmix64 finalizer: an integer hash with good avalanche."""
@@ -72,12 +88,25 @@ class TokenStream:

+def piece_batches(tokens: np.ndarray, piece_len: int, n_pieces: Optional[int],
+                  batch: int) -> Iterator[Tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]]:
+    """(inputs, targets) for pieces 0 .. n_pieces - 1, in file order, `batch`
+    pieces at a time; n_pieces None means every whole piece.
+
+    The held-out reader. File order, not shuffled: heldout.bin is written in
+    hash order, so its first pieces already come from a random sample of
+    documents, and the in-run evals read exactly those.

+    available = len(tokens) // piece_len
+    count = available if n_pieces is None else min(n_pieces, available)
+    for first in range(0, count, batch):
+        stop = min(first + batch, count)
+        window = np.asarray(tokens[first * piece_len: stop * piece_len],
+                            dtype=np.int64).reshape(stop - first, piece_len)
+        yield window[:, :-1], window[:, 1:]
```

### Task 6: Init and the CfC, LRU and gated-conv mixers

Design §4.2 and §4.4. Each module initialises itself from `reset_parameters()`, using the helpers in `init.py`, so a mixer built on its own is exactly the mixer inside the model. The tests check each mixer against a token-by-token version of its equations. They also split a sequence in two and require the state to carry across the split exactly.

**Files:**
- Create: `deluge/model/init.py`, `deluge/model/mixers/base.py`, `deluge/model/mixers/cfc.py`, `deluge/model/mixers/lru.py`, `deluge/model/mixers/gated_conv.py`
- Modify: `deluge/model/mixers/__init__.py` (currently empty)
- Test: `tests/test_mixers.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes:
  - `head_chunked_scan` (Task 2);
  - `CfCMixer`, `LRUMixer` and `GatedConvMixer` (config);
  - `input_scale` (`kernels/reference/scan.py`);
  - `RMSNorm` (`model/norm.py`) and `MixerState` (`model/state.py`).
- Produces:
  - `init.py`: `STD = 0.02`, `GATE_BIAS = 1.278`, `output_std(n_blocks)`, `decay_rates(a_min, a_max, n_heads)` (float64), `decay_bias(...)` (fp32 `b_f`), `normal_(linear, std)`, `identity_conv_(weight)`.
  - `mixers/base.py`: `causal_depthwise_conv(u [B,T,D], weight [D,1,k], buffer [B,k−1,D]) -> (out [B,T,D], new buffer)` and `no_gap(x)`.
  - `CfC(d_model, cfg: CfCMixer, out_std=STD)`, with parameters `W_v`, `conv`, `W_f` (bias is b_f), `W_g` (bias is b_g), `W_out`, `h0`, `conv_buf0` and `norm_h`, plus `.n_decay_heads` and `.decay(x) -> λ [B,T,H] fp32`.
  - `LRU(d_model, cfg: LRUMixer, out_std=STD)`: the same, without `conv` and `conv_buf0`.
  - `GatedConv(d_model, cfg: GatedConvMixer, out_std=STD)`, with `W_in`, `conv`, `W_out` and `conv_buf0`.
  - All three have `forward(x, state=None, delta=None) -> (y, MixerState)`. The LRU and the gated conv raise `ValueError` on any `delta`.
  - `cfc.SCAN_CHUNK = 64` and `cfc.gated_scan_readout(mixer, x, v, log_a, h0)`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

**Create `tests/test_mixers.py`:**

```python
"""The slot mixers: state continuity, the spec's equations, and the M1 init.

Each mixer is checked against a per-token implementation written straight from
its equations, so the scan (tested in test_head_scan.py) and the wiring around
it -- conv tap order, the b scaling, the gate -- are tested separately.
"""

import math

import pytest
import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F

from deluge.config import CfCMixer, GatedConvMixer, LRUMixer
from deluge.model.init import GATE_BIAS, decay_rates
from deluge.model.mixers import CfC, GatedConv, LRU
from deluge.model.mixers.base import causal_depthwise_conv
from deluge.model.state import MixerState

D = 16
TINY_CFC = CfCMixer(d_inner=24, n_decay_heads=3, conv_kernel=4, delta_aware=True,
                    a_min=0.9, a_max=0.999)
TINY_LRU = LRUMixer(d_inner=24, n_decay_heads=3, a_min=0.9, a_max=0.999)
TINY_CONV = GatedConvMixer(d_inner=24, conv_kernel=3)
DEV_CFC = CfCMixer(d_inner=1152, n_decay_heads=12, conv_kernel=4, delta_aware=True,
                   a_min=0.9, a_max=0.999)

MIXERS = {
    "cfc": lambda: CfC(D, TINY_CFC),
    "lru": lambda: LRU(D, TINY_LRU),
    "gated_conv": lambda: GatedConv(D, TINY_CONV),
}


def perturbed(make):
    """A mixer moved off its init, so zero h0, zero buffers and W_f = 0 cannot
    hide a state bug."""
    torch.manual_seed(0)
    module = make()
    with torch.no_grad():
        for p in module.parameters():
            p.add_(0.1 * torch.randn_like(p))
    return module


# ---- state ----------------------------------------------------------------- #

@pytest.mark.parametrize("kind", MIXERS)
@pytest.mark.parametrize("split", [1, 37, 64, 99])
def test_a_split_sequence_continues_exactly(kind, split):
    # 37 splits mid scan-chunk, 64 on a chunk edge.
    module = perturbed(MIXERS[kind])
    x = torch.randn(2, 100, D)
    whole, _ = module(x)
    first, state = module(x[:, :split])
    second, _ = module(x[:, split:], state)
    torch.testing.assert_close(torch.cat([first, second], dim=1), whole,
                               rtol=1e-5, atol=1e-5)


def test_a_fresh_sequence_starts_from_the_learned_state():
    # state=None means h0 and conv_buf0, broadcast over the batch -- not zeros.
    module = perturbed(MIXERS["cfc"])
    x = torch.randn(2, 5, D)
    explicit = MixerState(h=module.h0.detach().expand(2, -1).clone(),
                          conv_buf=module.conv_buf0.detach().expand(2, -1, -1).clone(),
                          gap=torch.zeros(2))
    torch.testing.assert_close(module(x)[0], module(x, explicit)[0])


def test_delta_of_one_is_the_default():
    module = perturbed(MIXERS["cfc"])
    x = torch.randn(2, 10, D)
    torch.testing.assert_close(module(x, delta=torch.ones(2, 10))[0], module(x)[0])


@pytest.mark.parametrize("kind", ["lru", "gated_conv"])
def test_the_baselines_refuse_a_delta(kind):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="Delta"):
        MIXERS[kind]()(torch.randn(1, 4, D), delta=torch.ones(1, 4))


# ---- the equations --------------------------------------------------------- #

def cfc_by_hand(m, x, conv=True):
    """Spec 3, one token at a time, from the module's weights. conv=False is
    the LRU."""
    batch, time, _ = x.shape
    per_head = m.W_v.out_features // m.n_decay_heads
    u = x @ m.W_v.weight.T
    if conv:
        k = m.conv.shape[-1]
        padded = torch.cat([m.conv_buf0.expand(batch, -1, -1), u], dim=1)
    h, ys = m.h0.expand(batch, -1), []
    for t in range(time):
        if conv:
            window = padded[:, t:t + k]                       # oldest input first
            v = F.silu((window * m.conv[:, 0, :].T).sum(dim=1))
        else:
            v = F.silu(u[:, t])
        lam = F.softplus(x[:, t] @ m.W_f.weight.T + m.W_f.bias)
        a = torch.exp(-lam).repeat_interleave(per_head, dim=-1)
        h = a * h + torch.sqrt(1 - a ** 2) * v
        gate = F.silu(x[:, t] @ m.W_g.weight.T + m.W_g.bias)
        ys.append((m.norm_h(h) * gate) @ m.W_out.weight.T)
    return torch.stack(ys, dim=1)


def gated_conv_by_hand(m, x):
    batch, time, _ = x.shape
    b_gate, c_gate, candidate = (x @ m.W_in.weight.T).chunk(3, dim=-1)
    k = m.conv.shape[-1]
    padded = torch.cat([m.conv_buf0.expand(batch, -1, -1), b_gate * candidate], dim=1)
    ys = [(c_gate[:, t] * (padded[:, t:t + k] * m.conv[:, 0, :].T).sum(dim=1))
          @ m.W_out.weight.T for t in range(time)]
    return torch.stack(ys, dim=1)


@pytest.mark.parametrize("kind, by_hand", [
    ("cfc", cfc_by_hand),
    ("lru", lambda m, x: cfc_by_hand(m, x, conv=False)),
    ("gated_conv", gated_conv_by_hand),
])
def test_matches_its_equations_token_by_token(kind, by_hand):
    module = perturbed(MIXERS[kind])
    x = torch.randn(2, 70, D)
    with torch.no_grad():
        torch.testing.assert_close(module(x)[0], by_hand(module, x), rtol=1e-4, atol=1e-5)


# ---- init (M1 design 4.4) ---------------------------------------------------- #

def test_the_decay_init_is_the_geometric_grid_exactly():
    module = CfC(768, DEV_CFC)
    lam = F.softplus(module.W_f.bias.double())
    slow, fast = -math.log(0.999), -math.log(0.9)
    grid = [slow * (fast / slow) ** (i / 11) for i in range(12)]
    torch.testing.assert_close(torch.exp(-lam), torch.exp(-torch.tensor(grid, dtype=torch.float64)),
                               rtol=1e-6, atol=0.0)
    # Design D6: memories of 999 ... 9 tokens, half of them past 100.
    assert [round(1 / value) for value in decay_rates(0.9, 0.999, 12).tolist()] == [
        999, 655, 429, 281, 184, 120, 79, 52, 34, 22, 14, 9]
    assert torch.equal(module.W_f.weight, torch.zeros_like(module.W_f.weight))


def test_the_lru_starts_on_the_same_grid():
    lru = LRU(768, LRUMixer(d_inner=1152, n_decay_heads=12, a_min=0.9, a_max=0.999))
    assert torch.equal(lru.W_f.bias, CfC(768, DEV_CFC).W_f.bias)


@pytest.mark.parametrize("kind", ["cfc", "lru"])
def test_the_gate_starts_at_silu_of_its_bias(kind):
    module = MIXERS[kind]()
    gate = F.silu(module.W_g(torch.randn(3, 5, D)))
    torch.testing.assert_close(gate, torch.full_like(gate, F.silu(torch.tensor(GATE_BIAS)).item()))
    assert F.silu(torch.tensor(GATE_BIAS)).item() == pytest.approx(0.9995, abs=1e-4)


@pytest.mark.parametrize("kind", ["cfc", "gated_conv"])
def test_both_convs_start_as_the_identity(kind):
    module = MIXERS[kind]()
    taps = module.conv[:, 0, :]
    assert torch.equal(taps[:, -1], torch.ones(24))
    assert torch.equal(taps[:, :-1], torch.zeros(24, taps.shape[1] - 1))
    u = torch.randn(2, 7, 24)
    buffer = torch.zeros(2, taps.shape[1] - 1, 24)
    torch.testing.assert_close(causal_depthwise_conv(u, module.conv, buffer)[0], u)


def test_the_conv_buffer_carries_the_last_inputs():
    u = torch.arange(2 * 5 * 3, dtype=torch.float32).reshape(2, 5, 3)
    weight = torch.randn(3, 1, 4)
    _, buffer = causal_depthwise_conv(u, weight, torch.zeros(2, 3, 3))
    assert torch.equal(buffer, u[:, -3:])
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write init and the mixers**

**Create `deluge/model/init.py`:**

```python
"""Initialisation (spec 3, M1 design 4.4).

Every module calls these from its own reset_parameters, so a mixer built on
its own is initialised exactly as it is inside the model.

  b_f     softplus^-1 of a geometric grid of lambda over heads, from -ln a_max
          to -ln a_min (design D6): log-uniform in the time constant. Dev's 12
          heads remember 999, 655, 429, 281, 184, 120, 79, 52, 34, 22, 14 and
          9 tokens.
  W_f     zero, so the designed decays are exact at step 0. W_f still gets
          gradient through x.
  W_g     zero, with b_g = GATE_BIAS: the gate is SiLU(1.278) = 0.9995 at step
          0, with gradient 1.000 (design D7).
  convs   identity on the current-token tap, the spec's "centre tap" read
          causally. CfC's k4 and the gated conv's k3 alike, so A1 does not also
          compare init schemes.
  zero    h0, the initial conv buffers, and the attention sinks' K and V. A
          zero sink has logit 0 and contributes nothing, so attention starts as
          softmax with n_sink extra units in the denominator (the spec leaves
          sink init open; this is the M1 choice).
  normal  std STD for linears and the embedding; output projections (W_out,
          W_o, the FFN's W_down) std STD / sqrt(2 * n_blocks).
  ones    RMSNorm gains (RMSNorm's own init).
"""

import math

import torch
from torch import nn

STD = 0.02
GATE_BIAS = 1.278


def output_std(n_blocks: int) -> float:
    """Residual-branch output projections, scaled so the stream's variance does
    not grow with depth."""
    return STD / math.sqrt(2 * n_blocks)


def decay_rates(a_min: float, a_max: float, n_heads: int) -> torch.Tensor:
    """lambda per head, float64: a geometric grid from -ln a_max (the longest
    memory, head 0) to -ln a_min."""
    slow, fast = -math.log(a_max), -math.log(a_min)
    if n_heads == 1:
        return torch.tensor([slow], dtype=torch.float64)
    steps = torch.arange(n_heads, dtype=torch.float64) / (n_heads - 1)
    return slow * (fast / slow) ** steps


def decay_bias(a_min: float, a_max: float, n_heads: int) -> torch.Tensor:
    """b_f = softplus^-1(lambda) = log(expm1(lambda)), so softplus(b_f) is the
    grid. expm1, because lambda is as small as 0.001."""
    return torch.log(torch.expm1(decay_rates(a_min, a_max, n_heads))).float()


def normal_(linear: nn.Linear, std: float) -> None:
    nn.init.normal_(linear.weight, std=std)
    if linear.bias is not None:
        nn.init.zeros_(linear.bias)


def identity_conv_(weight: torch.Tensor) -> None:
    """Depthwise conv weight [channels, 1, k]: 1 on the last tap, which
    F.conv1d applies to the current token, and 0 on the k - 1 before it."""
    with torch.no_grad():
        weight.zero_()
        weight[..., -1] = 1.0
```

**Create `deluge/model/mixers/base.py`:**

```python
"""The mixer interface: docs/project-structure.md calls this file "the whole
ablation strategy".

Every slot mixer -- CfC, the gated conv, the LRU, attention -- is

    forward(x, state=None, delta=None) -> (y, state)

with x the pre-normed residual stream [batch, time, d_model].

  state=None  a fresh sequence: a recurrent mixer starts from its learned h0
              and conv buffer, broadcast over the batch. The returned state
              (a MixerState, deluge/model/state.py) continues the sequence
              exactly: tests/test_mixers.py splits a sequence in two to check.
  delta=None  Delta = 1, the only value M1 uses. MoD's gaps are M2.

Recurrent mixers also have decay(x) -> lambda [batch, time, heads], which the
model reads for spec 7's stability monitors.
"""

import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F


def causal_depthwise_conv(u: torch.Tensor, weight: torch.Tensor,
                          buffer: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    """A depthwise causal conv that carries its last k - 1 inputs.

    u [batch, time, channels]; weight [channels, 1, k]; buffer
    [batch, k - 1, channels], the inputs before u. Returns (the conv output
    [batch, time, channels], the new buffer).
    """
    k = weight.shape[-1]
    padded = torch.cat([buffer.to(u.dtype), u], dim=1)
    out = F.conv1d(padded.transpose(1, 2), weight, groups=u.shape[-1])
    return out.transpose(1, 2), padded[:, padded.shape[1] - (k - 1):]


def no_gap(x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
    """MixerState.gap for a sequence MoD never skips: zero tokens skipped."""
    return torch.zeros(x.shape[0], device=x.device)
```

**Create `deluge/model/mixers/cfc.py`:**

```python
"""CfC-mix (spec 3), the layer under test.

    v     = SiLU(DWConv_k4(W_v x))
    lam   = softplus(W_f x + b_f)              per head, broadcast over its channels
    log a = -Delta * lam,   b = sqrt(1 - a^2)
    h     = scan(log a, b * v) from h0
    y     = W_out(RMSNorm(h) * SiLU(W_g x + b_g))

lambda, b and the scan run in fp32 (spec 3's numerics); the projections run
in whatever autocast says. b_g is M1 design D7: on zero-mean pre-normed input
SiLU(W_g x) averages 0.07 whatever W_g is, so only a bias can start the gate
near 1.
"""

import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

from ...config import CfCMixer
from ...kernels.head_scan import head_chunked_scan
from ...kernels.reference.scan import input_scale
from ..init import GATE_BIAS, STD, decay_bias, identity_conv_, normal_
from ..norm import RMSNorm
from ..state import MixerState
from .base import causal_depthwise_conv, no_gap

# The scan's tile, not architecture: it is not cfg.chunk (the summary chunk),
# and changing it changes only float rounding, memory and speed (design 5).
SCAN_CHUNK = 64


def gated_scan_readout(mixer: nn.Module, x: torch.Tensor, v: torch.Tensor,
                       log_a: torch.Tensor, h0: torch.Tensor):
    """The part CfC and the LRU share: scan b * v from h0, then the gated
    readout. Returns (y, h after the last token)."""
    b = input_scale(log_a)                                        # [B, T, H] fp32
    per_head = v.shape[-1] // log_a.shape[-1]
    bv = b.repeat_interleave(per_head, dim=-1) * v.float()
    states, h_last = head_chunked_scan(log_a, bv, h0, SCAN_CHUNK)
    y = mixer.W_out(mixer.norm_h(states) * F.silu(mixer.W_g(x)))
    return y, h_last


class CfC(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, d_model: int, cfg: CfCMixer, out_std: float = STD):
        super().__init__()
        self.cfg, self.out_std = cfg, out_std
        di, k = cfg.d_inner, cfg.conv_kernel
        self.n_decay_heads = cfg.n_decay_heads
        self.W_v = nn.Linear(d_model, di, bias=False)
        self.conv = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(di, 1, k))          # depthwise
        self.W_f = nn.Linear(d_model, cfg.n_decay_heads)          # bias is b_f
        self.W_g = nn.Linear(d_model, di)                         # bias is b_g
        self.W_out = nn.Linear(di, d_model, bias=False)
        self.h0 = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(di))
        self.conv_buf0 = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(k - 1, di))
        self.norm_h = RMSNorm(di)
        self.reset_parameters()

    def reset_parameters(self) -> None:
        normal_(self.W_v, STD)
        normal_(self.W_out, self.out_std)
        identity_conv_(self.conv)
        with torch.no_grad():
            self.W_f.weight.zero_()
            self.W_f.bias.copy_(decay_bias(self.cfg.a_min, self.cfg.a_max,
                                           self.cfg.n_decay_heads))
            self.W_g.weight.zero_()
            self.W_g.bias.fill_(GATE_BIAS)
            self.h0.zero_()
            self.conv_buf0.zero_()

    def decay(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        """lambda [batch, time, heads], fp32."""
        return F.softplus(self.W_f(x).float())

    def forward(self, x, state=None, delta=None):
        batch = x.shape[0]
        if state is None:
            h0 = self.h0.expand(batch, -1)
            buffer = self.conv_buf0.expand(batch, -1, -1)
        else:
            h0, buffer = state.h, state.conv_buf
        conv, buffer = causal_depthwise_conv(self.W_v(x), self.conv, buffer)
        lam = self.decay(x)
        log_a = -lam if delta is None else -delta.unsqueeze(-1).float() * lam
        y, h_last = gated_scan_readout(self, x, F.silu(conv), log_a, h0)
        return y, MixerState(h=h_last, conv_buf=buffer, gap=no_gap(x))
```

**Create `deluge/model/mixers/lru.py`:**

```python
"""B4: plain LRU -- CfC-mix without the short conv and without Delta.

    v = SiLU(W_v x), then CfC's decay, scan and gated readout, b_g included.

At A1 Delta is always 1, so CfC minus this arm is what the conv is worth
(M1 design 4.2); Delta's worth is A3's question.
"""

import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

from ...config import LRUMixer
from ..init import GATE_BIAS, STD, decay_bias, normal_
from ..norm import RMSNorm
from ..state import MixerState
from .base import no_gap
from .cfc import gated_scan_readout


class LRU(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, d_model: int, cfg: LRUMixer, out_std: float = STD):
        super().__init__()
        self.cfg, self.out_std = cfg, out_std
        di = cfg.d_inner
        self.n_decay_heads = cfg.n_decay_heads
        self.W_v = nn.Linear(d_model, di, bias=False)
        self.W_f = nn.Linear(d_model, cfg.n_decay_heads)
        self.W_g = nn.Linear(d_model, di)
        self.W_out = nn.Linear(di, d_model, bias=False)
        self.h0 = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(di))
        self.norm_h = RMSNorm(di)
        self.reset_parameters()

    def reset_parameters(self) -> None:
        normal_(self.W_v, STD)
        normal_(self.W_out, self.out_std)
        with torch.no_grad():
            self.W_f.weight.zero_()
            self.W_f.bias.copy_(decay_bias(self.cfg.a_min, self.cfg.a_max,
                                           self.cfg.n_decay_heads))
            self.W_g.weight.zero_()
            self.W_g.bias.fill_(GATE_BIAS)
            self.h0.zero_()

    def decay(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        return F.softplus(self.W_f(x).float())

    def forward(self, x, state=None, delta=None):
        if delta is not None:
            raise ValueError("the LRU baseline takes no Delta (spec 10, B4)")
        h0 = self.h0.expand(x.shape[0], -1) if state is None else state.h
        y, h_last = gated_scan_readout(self, x, F.silu(self.W_v(x)),
                                       -self.decay(x), h0)
        return y, MixerState(h=h_last, conv_buf=None, gap=no_gap(x))
```

**Create `deluge/model/mixers/gated_conv.py`:**

```python
"""B3: the LFM2-style double-gated short conv.

    B, C, x~ = W_in x                    three projections
    y        = W_out(C * DWConv_k3(B * x~))

It has a learned initial conv buffer and no recurrent state, so there is
nothing for a chunk summary to read (that asymmetry is A2's, not A1's).
"""

import torch
import torch.nn as nn

from ...config import GatedConvMixer
from ..init import STD, identity_conv_, normal_
from ..state import MixerState
from .base import causal_depthwise_conv, no_gap


class GatedConv(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, d_model: int, cfg: GatedConvMixer, out_std: float = STD):
        super().__init__()
        self.out_std = out_std
        di, k = cfg.d_inner, cfg.conv_kernel
        self.W_in = nn.Linear(d_model, 3 * di, bias=False)
        self.conv = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(di, 1, k))
        self.W_out = nn.Linear(di, d_model, bias=False)
        self.conv_buf0 = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(k - 1, di))
        self.reset_parameters()

    def reset_parameters(self) -> None:
        normal_(self.W_in, STD)
        normal_(self.W_out, self.out_std)
        identity_conv_(self.conv)
        with torch.no_grad():
            self.conv_buf0.zero_()

    def forward(self, x, state=None, delta=None):
        if delta is not None:
            raise ValueError("the gated conv baseline takes no Delta (spec 10, B3)")
        if state is None:
            buffer = self.conv_buf0.expand(x.shape[0], -1, -1)
        else:
            buffer = state.conv_buf
        b_gate, c_gate, candidate = self.W_in(x).chunk(3, dim=-1)
        conv, buffer = causal_depthwise_conv(b_gate * candidate, self.conv, buffer)
        return self.W_out(c_gate * conv), MixerState(h=None, conv_buf=buffer, gap=no_gap(x))
```

### Task 7: RoPE and attention

Design §4.2 and D3. GQA with RoPE θ = 1M, `n_sink` position-free sink K/V pairs, an explicit `[T, n_sink + T]` boolean mask, and SDPA pinned to the memory-efficient backend on CUDA. D3 rests on the brute-force test, so the reference shares no code with the module: it computes RoPE in complex form, expands KV heads by explicit indexing, and does the softmax one query at a time in float64.

**Files:**
- Create: `deluge/model/rope.py`, `deluge/model/mixers/attention.py`
- Modify: `deluge/model/mixers/__init__.py` (adds `Attention` and `make_mixer`)
- Test: `tests/test_attention.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `ModelConfig` (`n_heads`, `n_kv_heads`, `head_dim`, `window`, `n_sink`, `d_model`); `init.STD` and `init.normal_` (Task 6).
- Produces:
  - `rope.THETA`, `rope_angles(positions, head_dim, theta=THETA) -> (cos, sin)`, each `[T, head_dim/2]` fp32, and `apply_rope(x [..., T, head_dim], cos, sin)`, which returns x's dtype.
  - `attention_mask(time, n_sink, window, device) -> bool [time, n_sink + time]`.
  - `Attention(cfg: ModelConfig, out_std=STD)`, with `W_q`, `W_k`, `W_v`, `W_o`, `sink_k` and `sink_v` (`[n_kv_heads, n_sink, head_dim]`), and `forward(x, state=None, delta=None) -> (y, None)`. It raises `ValueError` mentioning M4 on any state.
  - `mixers.make_mixer(cfg, slot: "C" | "A", out_std) -> nn.Module`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

**Create `tests/test_attention.py`:**

```python
"""Attention against a brute-force reference that shares no code with it.

M1 design D3 rests on this file: A0's transformer is our own Block stack with
attention in every slot, so its forward pass is only independent of the hybrid
if this test is. The reference therefore re-derives everything: RoPE in complex
form, KV heads expanded by explicit indexing, the mask as a list of visible
positions, and softmax(q k^T / sqrt(d)) v one query at a time, in float64.
"""

import math

import pytest
import torch

from deluge.config import ModelConfig
from deluge.model.mixers.attention import Attention, attention_mask
from deluge.model.rope import apply_rope, rope_angles

TIME = 24
TINY = dict(d_model=64, mixer={"type": "attention"}, n_heads=4, n_kv_heads=2,
            head_dim=16, window=8, chunk=8, summary_sources=[], vocab_size=97,
            tie_embeddings=True, n_units=1, n_sink=2,
            ffn={"type": "dense", "d_ff": 128}, mod=None, mtp=None)


def config(window: int) -> ModelConfig:
    return ModelConfig.from_dict({**TINY, "window": window})


def complex_rope(x: torch.Tensor, theta: float = 1e6) -> torch.Tensor:
    """RoPE as the paper writes it: channel pair j at position t is the complex
    number x_2j + i x_2j+1, multiplied by e^(i t theta^(-2j/d)). x: [T, d]."""
    time, d = x.shape
    freqs = theta ** (-torch.arange(0, d, 2, dtype=torch.float64) / d)
    turn = torch.polar(torch.ones(time, d // 2, dtype=torch.float64),
                       torch.arange(time, dtype=torch.float64)[:, None] * freqs)
    z = torch.view_as_complex(x.double().reshape(time, d // 2, 2).contiguous())
    return torch.view_as_real(z * turn).reshape(time, d)


def brute_force(module: Attention, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
    batch, time, _ = x.shape
    heads, kv_heads, d = module.n_heads, module.n_kv_heads, module.head_dim
    q = (x.double() @ module.W_q.weight.double().T).reshape(batch, time, heads, d)
    k = (x.double() @ module.W_k.weight.double().T).reshape(batch, time, kv_heads, d)
    v = (x.double() @ module.W_v.weight.double().T).reshape(batch, time, kv_heads, d)
    out = torch.zeros(batch, time, heads, d, dtype=torch.float64)
    for b in range(batch):
        for h in range(heads):
            g = h // (heads // kv_heads)                 # this query head's KV head
            q_h, k_h = complex_rope(q[b, :, h]), complex_rope(k[b, :, g])
            keys = torch.cat([module.sink_k[g].double(), k_h])        # sinks: no RoPE
            values = torch.cat([module.sink_v[g].double(), v[b, :, g]])
            for t in range(time):
                visible = list(range(module.n_sink)) + [
                    module.n_sink + s for s in range(time) if t - module.window < s <= t]
                scores = keys[visible] @ q_h[t] / math.sqrt(d)
                out[b, t, h] = torch.softmax(scores, dim=0) @ values[visible]
    return out.reshape(batch, time, heads * d) @ module.W_o.weight.double().T


@pytest.mark.parametrize("window", [8, 16, 24, 32])
def test_matches_the_brute_force_reference(window):
    # 8 and 16 are windows inside the sequence, 24 is exactly T, 32 is past it.
    torch.manual_seed(0)
    module = Attention(config(window))
    with torch.no_grad():                    # zero sinks would hide a sink bug
        module.sink_k.normal_()
        module.sink_v.normal_()
    x = torch.randn(2, TIME, 64)
    y, state = module(x)
    assert state is None
    torch.testing.assert_close(y.double(), brute_force(module, x), rtol=1e-4, atol=1e-5)


def test_rope_matches_the_complex_form():
    x = torch.randn(4096, 64)
    cos, sin = rope_angles(torch.arange(4096), 64)
    torch.testing.assert_close(apply_rope(x, cos, sin).double(), complex_rope(x),
                               rtol=1e-4, atol=1e-4)


@pytest.mark.parametrize("t, s, shift", [(10, 3, 100), (4000, 3990, -3000), (7, 7, 2000)])
def test_rope_scores_depend_only_on_the_offset(t, s, shift):
    q, k = torch.randn(1, 64), torch.randn(1, 64)
    cos, sin = rope_angles(torch.arange(4096), 64)

    def score(t, s):
        return (apply_rope(q, cos[t:t + 1], sin[t:t + 1])
                @ apply_rope(k, cos[s:s + 1], sin[s:s + 1]).T)

    torch.testing.assert_close(score(t, s), score(t + shift, s + shift),
                               rtol=1e-4, atol=1e-4)


def test_the_mask_shows_the_sinks_always_and_the_window_band():
    mask = attention_mask(6, 2, 3, torch.device("cpu"))
    assert mask.shape == (6, 8) and mask[:, :2].all()
    assert mask[0, 2:].tolist() == [True, False, False, False, False, False]
    assert mask[5, 2:].tolist() == [False, False, False, True, True, True]


def test_attention_refuses_a_carried_state():
    module = Attention(config(8))
    _, state = module(torch.randn(1, 4, 64))
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="M4"):
        module(torch.randn(1, 4, 64), state=object())
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write RoPE, attention and the mixer factory**

**Create `deluge/model/rope.py`:**

```python
"""Rotary position embedding, theta = 1M (spec 5).

Channel pairs are adjacent, (2i, 2i + 1), as in the RoPE paper, and pair i
turns by angle t * theta^(-2i / head_dim) at position t. The angles are formed
in float64 and the rotation is done in fp32, then cast back: at t = 4095 an
fp16 angle would be off by whole radians. tests/test_attention.py checks this
against a complex-number form that shares no code with it.
"""

import torch

THETA = 1_000_000.0


def rope_angles(positions: torch.Tensor, head_dim: int,
                theta: float = THETA) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    """(cos, sin), each [len(positions), head_dim // 2], fp32."""
    if head_dim % 2:
        raise ValueError(f"RoPE needs an even head_dim, got {head_dim}")
    exponents = torch.arange(0, head_dim, 2, dtype=torch.float64,
                             device=positions.device) / head_dim
    angles = positions.to(torch.float64)[:, None] * theta ** -exponents[None, :]
    return torch.cos(angles).float(), torch.sin(angles).float()


def apply_rope(x: torch.Tensor, cos: torch.Tensor, sin: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
    """Rotate x [..., time, head_dim] by the angles for its positions."""
    pairs = x.float().unflatten(-1, (-1, 2))
    even, odd = pairs[..., 0], pairs[..., 1]
    rotated = torch.stack((even * cos - odd * sin, even * sin + odd * cos), dim=-1)
    return rotated.flatten(-2).to(x.dtype)
```

**Create `deluge/model/mixers/attention.py`:**

```python
"""Attention for the A slots, and for every slot of A0's matched transformer.

Spec 5 without the chunk summaries, which are M2: GQA with RoPE (theta 1M) on
Q and K, a sliding window W, and n_sink learned, position-free K/V pairs per
KV head that every query sees.

The mask is explicit, [T, n_sink + T] booleans: the sinks always, plus the
window band t - W < s <= t. SDPA's is_causal cannot express it -- with the
sinks prepended its top-left alignment is off by n_sink -- and an explicit
mask gives up causal block-skipping, so every layer costs the full T^2 (the
M1 design's throughput guesses assume that).

On CUDA, SDPA is pinned to the memory-efficient backend. The math fallback
would materialise [T, n_sink + T] scores for every head and sequence; pinned,
an unsupported case fails loudly instead. The pin is the global backend switch,
set once, not the sdpa_kernel context manager: DataParallel runs its replicas
in threads, and one thread leaving the context would restore the default
backends under the other mid-call.
"""

import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

from ...config import ModelConfig
from ..init import STD, normal_
from ..rope import apply_rope, rope_angles

_pinned = False


def _pin_memory_efficient_backend() -> None:
    global _pinned
    if _pinned:
        return
    torch.backends.cuda.enable_flash_sdp(False)
    torch.backends.cuda.enable_math_sdp(False)
    torch.backends.cuda.enable_cudnn_sdp(False)
    torch.backends.cuda.enable_mem_efficient_sdp(True)
    _pinned = True
    print("[model] attention: SDPA pinned to the memory-efficient backend")


def attention_mask(time: int, n_sink: int, window: int,
                   device: torch.device) -> torch.Tensor:
    """[time, n_sink + time] booleans, True where query t may attend."""
    t = torch.arange(time, device=device)[:, None]
    s = torch.arange(time, device=device)[None, :]
    band = (s <= t) & (s > t - window)
    sinks = torch.ones(time, n_sink, dtype=torch.bool, device=device)
    return torch.cat([sinks, band], dim=1)


class Attention(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, cfg: ModelConfig, out_std: float = STD):
        super().__init__()
        self.out_std = out_std
        self.n_heads, self.n_kv_heads = cfg.n_heads, cfg.n_kv_heads
        self.head_dim, self.window, self.n_sink = cfg.head_dim, cfg.window, cfg.n_sink
        q, kv = cfg.n_heads * cfg.head_dim, cfg.n_kv_heads * cfg.head_dim
        self.W_q = nn.Linear(cfg.d_model, q, bias=False)
        self.W_k = nn.Linear(cfg.d_model, kv, bias=False)
        self.W_v = nn.Linear(cfg.d_model, kv, bias=False)
        self.W_o = nn.Linear(q, cfg.d_model, bias=False)
        self.sink_k = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(cfg.n_kv_heads, cfg.n_sink, cfg.head_dim))
        self.sink_v = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(cfg.n_kv_heads, cfg.n_sink, cfg.head_dim))
        self.reset_parameters()

    def reset_parameters(self) -> None:
        for linear in (self.W_q, self.W_k, self.W_v):
            normal_(linear, STD)
        normal_(self.W_o, self.out_std)
        with torch.no_grad():
            self.sink_k.zero_()
            self.sink_v.zero_()

    def forward(self, x, state=None, delta=None):
        if state is not None:
            raise ValueError("attention carries no state in M1: its KV cache is "
                             "the decode engine's (M4)")
        if delta is not None:
            raise ValueError("attention takes no Delta")
        batch, time, _ = x.shape
        q = self.W_q(x).view(batch, time, self.n_heads, self.head_dim).transpose(1, 2)
        k = self.W_k(x).view(batch, time, self.n_kv_heads, self.head_dim).transpose(1, 2)
        v = self.W_v(x).view(batch, time, self.n_kv_heads, self.head_dim).transpose(1, 2)
        cos, sin = rope_angles(torch.arange(time, device=x.device), self.head_dim)
        q, k = apply_rope(q, cos, sin), apply_rope(k, cos, sin)
        # The sinks carry no RoPE: they are position-free (spec 5).
        k = torch.cat([self.sink_k.to(k.dtype).expand(batch, -1, -1, -1), k], dim=2)
        v = torch.cat([self.sink_v.to(v.dtype).expand(batch, -1, -1, -1), v], dim=2)
        group = self.n_heads // self.n_kv_heads
        k = k.repeat_interleave(group, dim=1)
        v = v.repeat_interleave(group, dim=1)
        if x.is_cuda:
            _pin_memory_efficient_backend()
        mask = attention_mask(time, self.n_sink, self.window, x.device)
        y = F.scaled_dot_product_attention(q, k, v, attn_mask=mask)
        return self.W_o(y.transpose(1, 2).reshape(batch, time, -1)), None
```

```diff
+++ b/deluge/model/mixers/__init__.py
@@ -1,7 +1,26 @@

+from torch import nn
+

+                       ModelConfig)
+from .attention import Attention
 from .cfc import CfC
 from .gated_conv import GatedConv
 from .lru import LRU
 
-__all__ = ["CfC", "GatedConv", "LRU"]

+def make_mixer(cfg: ModelConfig, slot: str, out_std: float) -> nn.Module:
+    """The mixer for a "C" slot (cfg.mixer) or an "A" slot (always attention)."""
+    mixer = cfg.mixer if slot == "C" else AttentionMixer()

+    if isinstance(mixer, AttentionMixer):
+        return Attention(cfg, out_std)
+    raise TypeError(f"no module for mixer config {mixer!r}")
```

### Task 8: The model, `build()`, and the trainer's no-decay hook

Design §4.1 and §4.3. The model is the embedding, `n_units` × (C, C, C, A) blocks, a final RMSNorm, the tied unembedding, and fp32 cross-entropy. `reduction="none"` is the eval contract. The trainer leaves the parameters that `model.no_weight_decay()` names undecayed, and a factory without that method still works.

**Files:**
- Create: `deluge/model/ffn/__init__.py`, `deluge/model/ffn/dense.py`, `deluge/model/block.py`, `deluge/model/model.py`
- Modify: `deluge/model/__init__.py` (currently empty), `deluge/train/trainer.py` (the weight-decay groups and the `resolve_factory` messages)
- Test: `tests/test_model.py`; `tests/test_train_cli.py` and `tests/test_torch_backend.py` change

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `make_mixer` (Task 7), `init.STD` and `init.output_std` (Task 6), `ModelConfig` and `CFC_PER_UNIT` (`deluge/config.py`), `RMSNorm`.
- Produces:
  - `SwiGLU(d_model, cfg: DenseFFN, out_std=STD)`, with `W_gate`, `W_up` and `W_down`.
  - `Block(d_model, mixer, ffn)`, with `.recurrent` and `forward(x, stats=None)`. A stats dict of lists collects `decay_lambda [B,H]` and `state_rms [B]` per recurrent block.
  - `deluge.model.build(cfg) -> Model` and `deluge.model.Model`.
  - `Model.forward(inputs, targets, reduction="mean")` returns `(scalar loss, {})`. With `reduction="none"` it returns `(token_losses [B,T] fp32, stats)`, where stats is `{"decay_lambda": [B, L, H], "state_rms": [B, L]}`, or `{}` with no recurrent layer.
  - `Model.no_weight_decay() -> List[str]`: every parameter name ending in `conv_buf0`, `sink_k` or `sink_v`.
  - `Model(cfg)` raises `NotImplementedError` mentioning M2 for MoD, MTP or summaries.
  - `build_backend` puts the names from `no_weight_decay()` in the undecayed group. It raises `ValueError` for a name that is not a trainable parameter.

```python
"""The assembled model: its size, its starting loss, that it learns, and the
contracts the trainer and the evaluator call."""

import math
from pathlib import Path

import pytest
import torch

from deluge.config import ModelConfig, load_model_config
from deluge.model import build
from deluge.model.init import STD

REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
ARMS = ["A0_scan_only", "A1_conv_baseline", "A1_lru_baseline", "A0_transformer_baseline"]

TINY = dict(d_model=64, n_heads=4, n_kv_heads=2, head_dim=16, window=16, chunk=8,
            summary_sources=[], vocab_size=128, tie_embeddings=True, n_units=1,
            n_sink=2, ffn={"type": "dense", "d_ff": 128}, mod=None, mtp=None)
TINY_MIXERS = {
    "cfc": {"type": "cfc", "d_inner": 48, "n_decay_heads": 4, "conv_kernel": 4,
            "delta_aware": True, "a_min": 0.9, "a_max": 0.999},
    "lru": {"type": "lru", "d_inner": 48, "n_decay_heads": 4, "a_min": 0.9, "a_max": 0.999},
    "gated_conv": {"type": "gated_conv", "d_inner": 48, "conv_kernel": 3},
    "attention": {"type": "attention"},
}


def tiny(kind: str) -> ModelConfig:
    return ModelConfig.from_dict({**TINY, "mixer": TINY_MIXERS[kind]})


def arm(name: str) -> ModelConfig:
    return load_model_config(REPO / f"configs/ablations/{name}.yaml")


@pytest.mark.parametrize("name", ARMS)
def test_the_built_model_has_exactly_the_configured_parameters(name):
    # The M1 param match is made in config.py; it only holds if the modules
    # are what config.py costs. On the meta device: no memory, no init time.
    cfg = arm(name)
    with torch.device("meta"):
        model = build(cfg)
    assert sum(p.numel() for p in model.parameters()) == cfg.params().total


@pytest.mark.parametrize("name", ARMS)
def test_a_fresh_model_starts_near_the_uniform_loss(name):
    # ln V + sigma^2 / 2, with sigma = STD * sqrt(d_model) ~ 0.55 the logit SD a
    # tied std-0.02 embedding gives through a unit-RMS final norm: ~10.53 nats.
    # Far off it means an init bug -- a gate that starts closed, a residual
    # branch that starts too loud -- not bad luck.
    cfg = arm(name)
    torch.manual_seed(0)
    model = build(cfg)
    tokens = torch.randint(0, cfg.vocab_size, (2, 129), generator=torch.Generator().manual_seed(0))
    with torch.no_grad():
        loss, _ = model(tokens[:, :-1], tokens[:, 1:])
    expected = math.log(cfg.vocab_size) + (STD * math.sqrt(cfg.d_model)) ** 2 / 2
    assert abs(loss.item() - expected) < 0.3, f"{loss.item():.3f} against {expected:.3f}"


@pytest.mark.parametrize("kind", TINY_MIXERS)
def test_a_tiny_model_overfits_one_batch(kind):
    torch.manual_seed(0)
    model = build(tiny(kind))
    tokens = torch.randint(0, 128, (2, 33), generator=torch.Generator().manual_seed(1))
    optimizer = torch.optim.Adam(model.parameters(), lr=3e-3)
    losses = []
    for _ in range(150):
        loss, _ = model(tokens[:, :-1], tokens[:, 1:])
        optimizer.zero_grad()
        loss.backward()
        optimizer.step()
        losses.append(loss.item())
    assert losses[-1] < 0.1 * losses[0], f"{losses[0]:.3f} -> {losses[-1]:.3f}"


def test_per_token_losses_average_to_the_training_loss():
    model = build(tiny("cfc"))
    tokens = torch.randint(0, 128, (3, 33))
    loss, metrics = model(tokens[:, :-1], tokens[:, 1:])
    losses, stats = model(tokens[:, :-1], tokens[:, 1:], reduction="none")
    assert loss.ndim == 0 and metrics == {}
    assert losses.shape == (3, 32)
    torch.testing.assert_close(losses.mean(), loss)
    # Leading batch dimension on everything, so DataParallel can gather it:
    # 3 recurrent layers (C, C, C, A) of 4 decay heads.
    assert stats["decay_lambda"].shape == (3, 3, 4)
    assert stats["state_rms"].shape == (3, 3)


def test_the_monitors_read_the_initial_decay_grid():
    # W_f = 0 at init, so the mean lambda is softplus(b_f) whatever the input.
    model = build(tiny("cfc"))
    tokens = torch.randint(0, 128, (2, 33))
    _, stats = model(tokens[:, :-1], tokens[:, 1:], reduction="none")
    expected = torch.nn.functional.softplus(model.blocks[0].mixer.W_f.bias.float())
    torch.testing.assert_close(stats["decay_lambda"][0, 0], expected)


@pytest.mark.parametrize("kind", ["gated_conv", "attention"])
def test_arms_without_recurrent_state_report_no_monitors(kind):
    model = build(tiny(kind))
    tokens = torch.randint(0, 128, (2, 17))
    _, stats = model(tokens[:, :-1], tokens[:, 1:], reduction="none")
    assert stats == {}


def test_rejects_an_unknown_reduction():
    model = build(tiny("cfc"))
    tokens = torch.randint(0, 128, (1, 9))
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="reduction"):
        model(tokens[:, :-1], tokens[:, 1:], reduction="sum")


def test_no_weight_decay_names_the_conv_buffers_and_the_sinks():
    names = build(tiny("cfc")).no_weight_decay()
    assert "blocks.0.mixer.conv_buf0" in names
    assert {"blocks.3.mixer.sink_k", "blocks.3.mixer.sink_v"} <= set(names)
    assert all(n.endswith(("conv_buf0", "sink_k", "sink_v")) for n in names)


def test_m2_features_are_refused_rather_than_ignored():
    # dev.yaml has chunk summaries on; building it would silently drop them.
    with pytest.raises(NotImplementedError, match="M2"):
        build(load_model_config(REPO / "configs/model/dev.yaml"))
```
