**Create `deluge/model/ffn/__init__.py`:**

```python
"""FFN blocks, one per config `ffn.type`. MoE (spec 8) is M5."""

from .dense import SwiGLU

__all__ = ["SwiGLU"]
```

**Create `deluge/model/ffn/dense.py`:**

```python
"""Dense SwiGLU FFN (spec 8, v1): W_down(SiLU(W_gate x) * W_up x)."""

import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

from ...config import DenseFFN
from ..init import STD, normal_


class SwiGLU(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, d_model: int, cfg: DenseFFN, out_std: float = STD):
        super().__init__()
        self.out_std = out_std
        self.W_gate = nn.Linear(d_model, cfg.d_ff, bias=False)
        self.W_up = nn.Linear(d_model, cfg.d_ff, bias=False)
        self.W_down = nn.Linear(cfg.d_ff, d_model, bias=False)
        self.reset_parameters()

    def reset_parameters(self) -> None:
        normal_(self.W_gate, STD)
        normal_(self.W_up, STD)
        normal_(self.W_down, self.out_std)

    def forward(self, x):
        return self.W_down(F.silu(self.W_gate(x)) * self.W_up(x))
```

**Create `deluge/model/block.py`:**

```python
"""One block: a pre-norm residual mixer, then a pre-norm residual FFN (spec 2).

    x = x + mixer(norm1(x))
    x = x + ffn(norm2(x))

The two norms are what config.py counts as each mixer's and FFN's "pre-norm".
"""

from typing import Dict, List, Optional

import torch
import torch.nn as nn

from .norm import RMSNorm


class Block(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, d_model: int, mixer: nn.Module, ffn: nn.Module):
        super().__init__()
        self.norm1, self.mixer = RMSNorm(d_model), mixer
        self.norm2, self.ffn = RMSNorm(d_model), ffn

    @property
    def recurrent(self) -> bool:
        return hasattr(self.mixer, "decay")

    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor,
                stats: Optional[Dict[str, List[torch.Tensor]]] = None) -> torch.Tensor:
        """stats, when given, collects the monitors of a recurrent mixer:
        decay_lambda [batch, heads], the mean lambda over the sequence, and
        state_rms [batch], the RMS of h at the last position."""
        normed = self.norm1(x)
        y, state = self.mixer(normed)
        if stats is not None and self.recurrent:
            # decay() again rather than a side channel out of forward(): one
            # [d_model, heads] matmul, and only on the eval path.
            stats["decay_lambda"].append(self.mixer.decay(normed).mean(dim=1))
            stats["state_rms"].append(state.h.float().pow(2).mean(dim=-1).sqrt())
        x = x + y
        return x + self.ffn(self.norm2(x))
```

**Create `deluge/model/model.py`:**

```python
"""The model: embedding, n_units x (C, C, C, A) blocks, RMSNorm, tied unembedding.

build() is the factory deluge.train calls by default (the contract is in
deluge/train/trainer.py's docstring). C slots use cfg.mixer; A slots are always
attention. Imports torch only (docs/project-structure.md principle 1).
"""

from typing import Dict, List, Tuple

import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

from ..config import CFC_PER_UNIT, ModelConfig
from .block import Block
from .ffn import SwiGLU
from .init import STD, output_std
from .mixers import make_mixer
from .norm import RMSNorm

# Learned state rather than weights (spec 15's baked-in prompt); 2-D or 3-D,
# so the trainer's ndim rule alone would decay them toward zero.
NO_DECAY_SUFFIXES = ("conv_buf0", "sink_k", "sink_v")


class Model(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, cfg: ModelConfig):
        super().__init__()
        if cfg.mod is not None or cfg.mtp is not None or cfg.uses_summaries:
            raise NotImplementedError(
                "MoD, MTP and chunk summaries are M2. M1 builds the A0/A1 "
                "configs (configs/ablations/A0_*.yaml, A1_*.yaml): mod: null, "
                "mtp: null, summary_sources: []")
        if not cfg.tie_embeddings:
            raise NotImplementedError("untied embeddings: spec 2 ties them, and "
                                      "M1 builds only that")
        self.cfg = cfg
        out_std = output_std(cfg.n_blocks)
        self.embed = nn.Embedding(cfg.vocab_size, cfg.d_model)
        slots = ("C",) * CFC_PER_UNIT + ("A",)
        self.blocks = nn.ModuleList(
            Block(cfg.d_model, make_mixer(cfg, slot, out_std),
                  SwiGLU(cfg.d_model, cfg.ffn, out_std))
            for _ in range(cfg.n_units) for slot in slots)
        self.norm = RMSNorm(cfg.d_model)
        nn.init.normal_(self.embed.weight, std=STD)

    def no_weight_decay(self) -> List[str]:
        """Parameter names the trainer leaves undecayed on top of its 1-D rule."""
        return [name for name, _ in self.named_parameters()
                if name.endswith(NO_DECAY_SUFFIXES)]

    def forward(self, inputs: torch.Tensor, targets: torch.Tensor,
                reduction: str = "mean"):
        """reduction="mean": (loss, metrics), the training contract.
        reduction="none": (token_losses [B, T], stats), the eval contract. stats
        has decay_lambda [B, n_recurrent, heads] and state_rms [B, n_recurrent],
        both absent when no layer is recurrent. A leading batch dimension on
        everything, so DataParallel's gather works."""
        if reduction not in ("mean", "none"):
            raise ValueError(f"reduction must be 'mean' or 'none', got {reduction!r}")
        stats: Dict[str, List[torch.Tensor]] = {"decay_lambda": [], "state_rms": []}
        x = self.embed(inputs)
        for block in self.blocks:
            x = block(x, stats if reduction == "none" else None)
        logits = F.linear(self.norm(x), self.embed.weight)
        # fp32 cross-entropy: an fp16 logsumexp over 32k logits loses the small
        # differences the loss is made of.
        losses = F.cross_entropy(logits.float().flatten(0, 1), targets.flatten(),
                                 reduction="none").view_as(targets)
        if reduction == "mean":
            return losses.mean(), {}
        if not stats["decay_lambda"]:
            return losses, {}
        return losses, {"decay_lambda": torch.stack(stats["decay_lambda"], dim=1),
                        "state_rms": torch.stack(stats["state_rms"], dim=1)}


def build(cfg: ModelConfig) -> Model:
    return Model(cfg)
```

### Task 9: Loop: in-run evals and the final-eval completion marker

Design §7.1. `loop.py` stays torch-free and gains an eval cadence, the way it already has a checkpoint cadence. When both are due, the eval runs first, so a kill during it reruns the eval on resume. Once the budget is spent and a `final_eval_fn` exists, the loop writes `final_eval.json` atomically, and the run is complete only once that file exists. With no `final_eval_fn` (synthetic data, the smoke rehearsal), completion stays as it is today.

**Files:**
- Modify: `deluge/train/loop.py`
- Test: `tests/test_resume.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `TrainConfig.eval_every_steps` (Task 4).
- Produces:
  - `TrainLoop(..., eval_fn: Optional[EvalFn] = None, final_eval_fn: Optional[EvalFn] = None)`, where `EvalFn = Callable[[], Dict[str, Any]]`.
  - In-run records are logged as `{"event": "eval", "step", "tokens_seen", **eval_fn()}`.
  - `TrainLoop.final_eval_path` is `out_dir / "final_eval.json"` (`loop.FINAL_EVAL`). It holds `{"step", "tokens_seen", **final_eval_fn()}`, which is also logged as `{"event": "final_eval", ...}`.
  - `write_json_atomic(path, record)`.
  - `Outcome.complete` means the budget is spent and, if there is a `final_eval_fn`, its file exists.

```diff
+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #

+    evaluated.run()
+
+    assert losses_a == losses_b and np.array_equal(model_a.w, model_b.w)
+    assert len(calls) == cfg.total_steps
+    evals = [r for r in records if r["event"] == "eval"]
+    assert [r["step"] for r in evals] == list(range(1, cfg.total_steps + 1))
+    assert evals[0]["subset_loss"] == 1.0 and evals[0]["tokens_seen"] == cfg.global_batch_tokens
+
+
+def test_an_eval_due_with_a_checkpoint_runs_before_the_save(tmp_path):
+    # A kill during the eval then resumes from the previous checkpoint and
+    # reruns the eval, rather than resuming past it.
+    cfg = dataclasses.replace(TrainConfig(**BUDGET),
+                              eval_every_tokens=BUDGET["checkpoint_every_tokens"])
+    events = []
+    loop, *_ = build(tmp_path / "order", cfg,
+                     eval_fn=lambda: events.append(("eval", loop.step)) or {})
+    save = loop.checkpointer.save
+    loop.checkpointer.save = lambda step, *a: events.append(("save", step)) or save(step, *a)
+    loop.run()
+    assert events.index(("eval", 4)) < events.index(("save", 4))
+
+
+def test_the_final_eval_is_the_completion_marker(tmp_path):
+    cfg = TrainConfig(**BUDGET)
+    loop, *_ = build(tmp_path / "final", cfg, final_eval_fn=lambda: {"full_loss": 2.5})
+    outcome = loop.run()
+    assert outcome.complete and outcome.exit_code == 0
+    record = json.loads(loop.final_eval_path.read_text())
+    assert record == {"step": 20, "tokens_seen": 20 * cfg.global_batch_tokens,
+                      "full_loss": 2.5}
+    assert not list(loop.final_eval_path.parent.glob(".*.tmp"))
+
+
+def test_a_resume_with_the_budget_spent_runs_only_the_final_eval(tmp_path):
+    # Review focus: a session killed during the final eval. The checkpoint at
+    # total_steps exists; final_eval.json does not.
+    cfg = TrainConfig(**BUDGET)
+
+    def killed():
+        raise KeyboardInterrupt("Kaggle stopped the session")
+
+    first, *_ = build(tmp_path / "k", cfg, final_eval_fn=killed)
+    with pytest.raises(KeyboardInterrupt):
+        first.run()
+    assert not first.final_eval_path.exists()
+
+    calls = []
+    second, _, _, losses, _ = build(tmp_path / "k", cfg,
+                                    final_eval_fn=lambda: calls.append(1) or {})
+    outcome = second.run()
+    assert losses == [] and calls == [1]
+    assert outcome.complete and outcome.exit_code == 0
+
+
+def test_an_interrupted_run_runs_no_final_eval(tmp_path):
+    cfg = dataclasses.replace(TrainConfig(**BUDGET), deadline_minutes=10 / 60)
+    calls = []
+    loop, *_ = build(tmp_path / "i", cfg, final_eval_fn=lambda: calls.append(1) or {})
+    assert loop.run().exit_code == 2
+    assert calls == [] and not loop.final_eval_path.exists()
+
+
+def test_without_a_final_eval_the_spent_budget_completes_the_run(tmp_path):
+    # Synthetic data and the chain's smoke runs have no heldout.bin: they must
+    # still finish, or the chain would resume them forever.
+    loop, *_ = build(tmp_path / "s", TrainConfig(**BUDGET))
+    assert loop.run().exit_code == 0
+    assert not loop.final_eval_path.exists()
+
+
+def test_checkpointed_counts_are_not_recounted_after_a_kill(tmp_path):
+    # The fp16 skip count is checkpointed (deluge/train/monitors.py): steps
+    # replayed after a kill must not be counted twice.
+    cfg = TrainConfig(**BUDGET)
+
+    def make(directory, die_at=None):
+        counter, calls = Counter(), []
+        loop, *_ = build(directory, cfg)
+        loop.stateful["counter"] = counter
+        inner = loop.step_fn
+
+        def step_fn(batches, lr):
+            calls.append(1)
+            if len(calls) == die_at:
+                raise KeyboardInterrupt("killed mid-step")
+            counter.count += 1
+            return inner(batches, lr)
+
+        loop.step_fn = step_fn
+        return loop, counter
+
+    killed, _ = make(tmp_path / "c", die_at=10)      # checkpoints at 4 and 8
+    with pytest.raises(KeyboardInterrupt):
+        killed.run()
+    resumed, counter = make(tmp_path / "c")
+    resumed.run()
+    assert counter.count == cfg.total_steps
```

```diff
 StepFn = Callable[[List[Batch], float], Union[float, Dict[str, float]]]

+            # session resumes at total_steps and comes straight back to it.
+            record = {"step": self.step, "tokens_seen": self.tokens_seen,
+                      **self.final_eval_fn()}

         return found[0][1] if found else None
 
 
+def write_json_atomic(path: Path, record: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:
+    """Temp file, then os.replace: final_eval.json marks the run complete, so a
+    kill mid-write must leave no file rather than half of one."""
+    tmp = path.with_name(f".{path.name}.tmp")
+    tmp.write_text(json.dumps(record, indent=2) + "\n")
+    os.replace(tmp, path)
+
+
 def jsonl_logger(path: Path, echo: bool = True) -> Callable[[Dict[str, Any]], None]:
     """Append-mode run log. Appends, so a resumed session extends one file."""
     path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
```

```bash
train: in-run evals and final_eval.json as the completion marker

eval_fn runs every eval_every_steps, before a checkpoint due at the same
step. With a final_eval_fn the run completes only once final_eval.json
is written, atomically, so a session killed during the final eval
```

---

### Task 10: Monitors, the evaluator, and the trainer's wiring

Design §7.1 and §7.2. `monitors.py` is torch-free: it holds the eval sums (loss, position buckets, the decay summary) and the checkpointed `SkipCounter`. In `trainer.py`:
- `build_evaluator` reads `heldout.bin` in pieces, under the training autocast, in eval mode with no grad;
- `step_fn` logs the loss scale, the skip count and peak memory;
- `run()` copies `provenance.json` into the run directory, builds the stream before the model (so a `piece_len` mismatch fails in a second), and wires the evals when `heldout.bin` sits beside `--data`.

**Files:**
- Create: `deluge/train/monitors.py`
- Modify: `deluge/train/trainer.py`
- Test: `tests/test_monitors.py`; `tests/test_torch_backend.py` changes

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes:
  - `piece_batches`, `load_provenance`, `PROVENANCE` and `TOKEN_DTYPE` (Task 5);
  - `TrainLoop(eval_fn=, final_eval_fn=)` (Task 9);
  - `Model.forward(..., reduction="none")` (Task 8).
- Produces:
  - `monitors.SUBSET_PIECES == 488` and `monitors.BUCKETS`.
  - `EvalAccumulator.add(losses [B,T], stats)` and `EvalAccumulator.result()`, which returns `{"loss", "tokens", "buckets": {"0-512", "512-1536", "1536-4096"}}`, plus `"decay": {"min", "median", "max"}` (per layer) and `"state_rms"` when the arm is recurrent.
  - `SkipCounter`, with `observe(before, after)`, `count`, `state_dict()` and `load_state_dict()`.
  - `trainer.HELDOUT == "heldout.bin"`.
  - `build_backend(...)`'s stateful dict gains `"skips"`. Step records gain `skipped_steps` always, `loss_scale` when the scaler is enabled, and `peak_alloc_gib` and `peak_reserved_gib` on CUDA.
  - `build_evaluator(model, cfg, device, data_parallel, heldout, piece_len) -> evaluate(n_pieces) -> (full, subset)`. It calls the model as `forward(x, y, "none")`, with nothing passed by keyword (Review Focus 3).
  - `in_run_eval(evaluate)` returns the eval-record fields `subset_loss`, `subset_tokens`, `buckets`, `decay` and `state_rms`.
  - `final_eval(evaluate, skips)` returns the `final_eval.json` fields `full_loss`, `full_tokens`, `subset_loss`, `subset_tokens`, `buckets`, `decay`, `state_rms` and `skipped_steps`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

**Create `tests/test_monitors.py`:**

```python
"""Eval accounting and the skip counter, without torch."""

import numpy as np
import pytest

from deluge.train.monitors import BUCKETS, EvalAccumulator, SkipCounter


def test_the_loss_is_the_mean_over_every_token():
    acc = EvalAccumulator()
    acc.add(np.full((2, 4096), 3.0), {})
    acc.add(np.full((1, 4096), 6.0), {})
    result = acc.result()
    assert result["loss"] == pytest.approx(4.0) and result["tokens"] == 3 * 4096


def test_position_buckets_split_the_sequence():
    losses = np.concatenate([np.full(512, 1.0), np.full(1024, 2.0), np.full(2560, 3.0)])[None]
    acc = EvalAccumulator()
    acc.add(losses, {})
    assert acc.result()["buckets"] == {"0-512": 1.0, "512-1536": 2.0, "1536-4096": 3.0}


def test_a_short_sequence_leaves_later_buckets_empty():
    acc = EvalAccumulator()
    acc.add(np.ones((2, 64)), {})
    assert acc.result()["buckets"] == {"0-512": 1.0, "512-1536": None, "1536-4096": None}


def test_the_decay_summary_is_a_at_the_mean_lambda_per_layer():
    # Two sequences, two layers, three heads. Mean lambda per head, then
    # a = exp(-mean lambda), then min / median / max over the heads.
    lam = np.array([[[0.1, 0.2, 0.3], [1.0, 1.0, 1.0]],
                    [[0.3, 0.4, 0.5], [3.0, 3.0, 3.0]]])
    rms = np.array([[1.0, 2.0], [3.0, 4.0]])
    acc = EvalAccumulator()
    acc.add(np.ones((2, 8)), {"decay_lambda": lam, "state_rms": rms})
    result = acc.result()
    np.testing.assert_allclose(result["decay"]["min"], np.exp(-np.array([0.4, 2.0])))
    np.testing.assert_allclose(result["decay"]["median"], np.exp(-np.array([0.3, 2.0])))
    np.testing.assert_allclose(result["decay"]["max"], np.exp(-np.array([0.2, 2.0])))
    np.testing.assert_allclose(result["state_rms"], [2.0, 3.0])


def test_arms_without_recurrent_layers_report_no_decay():
    acc = EvalAccumulator()
    acc.add(np.ones((1, 8)), {})
    assert "decay" not in acc.result() and "state_rms" not in acc.result()


def test_an_empty_eval_is_an_error_not_a_zero():
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="no held-out tokens"):
        EvalAccumulator().result()


def test_the_buckets_cover_the_training_sequence():
    assert BUCKETS[0][0] == 0 and BUCKETS[-1][1] == 4096
    assert all(a[1] == b[0] for a, b in zip(BUCKETS, BUCKETS[1:]))


def test_a_skip_is_a_drop_in_the_loss_scale():
    counter = SkipCounter()
    counter.observe(65536.0, 32768.0)      # overflow: step skipped, scale halved
    counter.observe(32768.0, 32768.0)      # a normal step
    counter.observe(32768.0, 65536.0)      # growth after 2000 clean steps
    assert counter.count == 1


def test_the_skip_count_round_trips_through_a_checkpoint():
    counter = SkipCounter()
    counter.observe(2.0, 1.0)
    restored = SkipCounter()
    restored.load_state_dict(counter.state_dict())
    assert restored.count == 1
```

```diff
+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
+# M1: the held-out evaluator, the skip counter, and a whole CLI run on CPU
+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
+
+import json                                                   # noqa: E402
+
+import yaml                                                   # noqa: E402
+
+from deluge.config import ModelConfig                          # noqa: E402
+from deluge.model import build                                 # noqa: E402
+from deluge.train.trainer import build_evaluator, main         # noqa: E402
+
+TINY_MODEL = dict(
+    d_model=64, n_heads=4, n_kv_heads=2, head_dim=16, window=16, chunk=8,
+    summary_sources=[], vocab_size=128, tie_embeddings=True, n_units=1, n_sink=2,
+    ffn={"type": "dense", "d_ff": 128}, mod=None, mtp=None,
+    mixer={"type": "cfc", "d_inner": 48, "n_decay_heads": 4, "conv_kernel": 4,
+           "delta_aware": True, "a_min": 0.9, "a_max": 0.999})
+
+
+def test_the_skip_count_is_checkpointed(tmp_path, model_cfg):
+    _, stateful = make_loop(tmp_path / "k", TrainConfig(**BUDGET), model_cfg)
+    assert stateful["skips"].state_dict() == {"count": 0}
+
+
+def heldout_pieces(n, piece_len, seed=0):
+    return np.random.default_rng(seed).integers(0, 128, size=n * piece_len, dtype=np.uint16)
+
+
+def test_the_evaluator_reads_every_piece_including_a_ragged_last_batch():
+    # Review focus: 5 pieces at micro_batch 2 leaves a last batch of one.
+    cfg = TrainConfig(**BUDGET)
+    model = build(ModelConfig.from_dict(TINY_MODEL))
+    evaluate = build_evaluator(model, cfg, "cpu", False, heldout_pieces(5, 33), 33)
+    full, subset = evaluate(None)
+    assert full["tokens"] == 5 * 32 and subset["tokens"] == 5 * 32
+    assert len(full["decay"]["min"]) == 3 and len(full["state_rms"]) == 3
+    assert model.training, "the evaluator must hand the model back in train mode"
+    first_two, _ = evaluate(2)
+    assert first_two["tokens"] == 2 * 32
+
+
+def test_the_evaluator_passes_nothing_by_keyword(monkeypatch):
+    # Review focus, on two GPUs: DataParallel splits tensors across the GPUs
+    # but copies keyword arguments to every one, so a last batch of one piece
+    # reaches the second GPU's replica as keywords with no inputs -- a
+    # TypeError in the final eval. Positional arguments split with the batch.
+    # No CPU test can run DataParallel's scatter, so this pins the call.
+    calls = []
+
+    class Recording(nn.Module):
+        def __init__(self, module):
+            super().__init__()
+            self.module = module
+
+        def forward(self, *args, **kwargs):
+            calls.append(kwargs)
+            return self.module(*args, **kwargs)
+
+    monkeypatch.setattr(torch.nn, "DataParallel", Recording)
+    model = build(ModelConfig.from_dict(TINY_MODEL))
+    evaluate = build_evaluator(model, TrainConfig(**BUDGET), "cpu", True,
+                               heldout_pieces(5, 33), 33)
+    full, _ = evaluate(None)
+    assert full["tokens"] == 5 * 32
+    assert calls == [{}, {}, {}]            # batches of 2, 2 and 1
+
+
+def test_evals_leave_training_bit_identical(tmp_path):
+    # Design 7.1: an eval touches neither the training RNG nor the data
+    # position, so a run that evaluates every step trains the same weights.
+    cfg = dataclasses.replace(TrainConfig(**BUDGET), total_tokens=256 * 4)
+    model_cfg = ModelConfig.from_dict(TINY_MODEL)
+
+    def train(directory, every):
+        torch.manual_seed(0)
+        step_fn, stateful = build_backend(model_cfg, cfg, "cpu", False, build)
+        evaluate = build_evaluator(stateful["model"], cfg, "cpu", False,
+                                   heldout_pieces(3, cfg.seq_len + 1, seed=1), cfg.seq_len + 1)
+        tokens = np.random.default_rng(0).integers(0, 128, size=20_000, dtype=np.uint16)
+        TrainLoop(cfg=dataclasses.replace(cfg, eval_every_tokens=every),
+                  stream=TokenStream(tokens, cfg.seq_len, cfg.seed),
+                  checkpointer=Checkpointer(directory), stateful=stateful,
+                  step_fn=step_fn, clock=FakeClock(),
+                  eval_fn=(lambda: evaluate(2)[0]) if every else None).run()
+        return stateful
+
+    plain = train(tmp_path / "plain", None)
+    evaluated = train(tmp_path / "evals", cfg.global_batch_tokens)
+    for name, tensor in plain["model"].state_dict().items():
+        assert torch.equal(tensor, evaluated["model"].state_dict()[name]), name
+    a = plain["optimizer"].state_dict()["state"]
+    b = evaluated["optimizer"].state_dict()["state"]
+    for index in a:
+        for key in ("exp_avg", "exp_avg_sq"):
+            assert torch.equal(a[index][key], b[index][key]), f"{index}.{key}"
+
+
+def write_run_inputs(tmp_path, train_overrides=None, piece_len=33, heldout=True):
+    """A tiny model config, a 4-step budget, and data in pieces of piece_len."""
+    data = tmp_path / "data"
+    data.mkdir()
+    heldout_pieces(40, piece_len).tofile(data / "train.bin")
+    if heldout:
+        heldout_pieces(3, piece_len, seed=1).tofile(data / "heldout.bin")
+    (data / "provenance.json").write_text(json.dumps({"piece_len": piece_len}))
+    (tmp_path / "model.yaml").write_text(yaml.safe_dump(TINY_MODEL))
+    budget = {**BUDGET, "total_tokens": 256 * 4, "checkpoint_every_tokens": 256 * 2,
+              "eval_every_tokens": 256 * 2, **(train_overrides or {})}
+    (tmp_path / "train.yaml").write_text(yaml.safe_dump(budget))
+    return ["--model", str(tmp_path / "model.yaml"), "--train", str(tmp_path / "train.yaml"),
+            "--data", str(data / "train.bin"), "--out", str(tmp_path / "run"),
+            "--device", "cpu"]
+
+
+def test_a_run_with_a_heldout_set_evaluates_and_finishes(tmp_path):
+    argv = write_run_inputs(tmp_path)
+    assert main(argv) == 0
+    run = tmp_path / "run"
+    final = json.loads((run / "final_eval.json").read_text())
+    assert final["step"] == 4 and final["skipped_steps"] == 0
+    assert final["full_tokens"] == 3 * 32 and final["subset_tokens"] == 3 * 32
+    assert set(final["buckets"]) == {"0-512", "512-1536", "1536-4096"}
+    assert len(final["decay"]["max"]) == 3
+    assert json.loads((run / "provenance.json").read_text()) == {"piece_len": 33}
+    records = [json.loads(line) for line in (run / "log.jsonl").read_text().splitlines()]
+    assert [r["step"] for r in records if r["event"] == "eval"] == [2, 4]
+    assert [r["event"] for r in records].count("final_eval") == 1
+    # A rerun finds the run complete and does nothing more.
+    assert main(argv) == 0
+    rerun = [json.loads(line) for line in (run / "log.jsonl").read_text().splitlines()]
+    assert [r["event"] for r in rerun].count("final_eval") == 1
+
+
+def test_a_heldout_set_without_a_piece_length_is_refused(tmp_path):
+    argv = write_run_inputs(tmp_path)
+    (tmp_path / "data" / "provenance.json").write_text("{}")
+    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="piece_len"):
+        main(argv)
+
+
+def test_pieces_that_do_not_fit_the_sequence_length_stop_the_run(tmp_path):
+    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="pieces of 65"):
+        main(write_run_inputs(tmp_path, piece_len=65))
```

**Create `deluge/train/monitors.py`:**

```python
"""Held-out eval accounting and the stability monitors (spec 7's watch-list,
M1 design 7.1-7.2).

Torch-free, like loop.py. The trainer's evaluator feeds EvalAccumulator numpy
arrays, so the arithmetic the "stable" criteria and the A1 verdict read --
position buckets, the decay summary, the skip count -- is testable without a
model.
"""

from typing import Any, Dict, Optional

import numpy as np

# The in-run evals read the first 488 pieces of heldout.bin: 488 x 4096 is
# ~2M tokens, and heldout.bin's hash order makes them a random document sample.
SUBSET_PIECES = 488
# Position ranges the A1 writeup reads: a CfC advantage from long-range state
# should show in the last bucket, beyond the 512-token windows' reach.
BUCKETS = ((0, 512), (512, 1536), (1536, 4096))


class EvalAccumulator:
    """Sums over held-out batches; result() turns them into an eval record."""

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.loss_sum, self.tokens, self.sequences = 0.0, 0, 0
        self.bucket_sum = {bucket: 0.0 for bucket in BUCKETS}
        self.bucket_tokens = {bucket: 0 for bucket in BUCKETS}
        self.lambda_sum: Optional[np.ndarray] = None    # [layers, heads]
        self.rms_sum: Optional[np.ndarray] = None       # [layers]

    def add(self, losses: np.ndarray, stats: Dict[str, np.ndarray]) -> None:
        """losses [batch, time] in nats; stats as the model returns them
        (decay_lambda [batch, layers, heads], state_rms [batch, layers]), or
        empty for an arm with no recurrent layer."""
        losses = np.asarray(losses, dtype=np.float64)
        self.loss_sum += losses.sum()
        self.tokens += losses.size
        self.sequences += losses.shape[0]
        for lo, hi in BUCKETS:
            part = losses[:, lo:hi]
            self.bucket_sum[(lo, hi)] += part.sum()
            self.bucket_tokens[(lo, hi)] += part.size
        if "decay_lambda" in stats:
            lam = np.asarray(stats["decay_lambda"], dtype=np.float64).sum(axis=0)
            rms = np.asarray(stats["state_rms"], dtype=np.float64).sum(axis=0)
            self.lambda_sum = lam if self.lambda_sum is None else self.lambda_sum + lam
            self.rms_sum = rms if self.rms_sum is None else self.rms_sum + rms

    def result(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        if not self.tokens:
            raise ValueError("no held-out tokens were evaluated")
        out: Dict[str, Any] = {
            "loss": self.loss_sum / self.tokens,
            "tokens": self.tokens,
            "buckets": {f"{lo}-{hi}": (self.bucket_sum[(lo, hi)] / self.bucket_tokens[(lo, hi)]
                                       if self.bucket_tokens[(lo, hi)] else None)
                        for lo, hi in BUCKETS},
        }
        if self.lambda_sum is not None:
            # a = exp(-mean lambda): the geometric mean of a at Delta = 1.
            a = np.exp(-self.lambda_sum / self.sequences)
            out["decay"] = {"min": a.min(axis=1).tolist(),
                            "median": np.median(a, axis=1).tolist(),
                            "max": a.max(axis=1).tolist()}
            out["state_rms"] = (self.rms_sum / self.sequences).tolist()
        return out


class SkipCounter:
    """Optimizer steps the fp16 loss scaler skipped, cumulative over the run.

    A skip shows as the scale dropping across scaler.update(). Checkpointed with
    the optimizer, so steps replayed after a kill are not counted twice;
    design 7.3's 1% bound is read off it.
    """

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.count = 0

    def observe(self, scale_before: float, scale_after: float) -> None:
        if scale_after < scale_before:
            self.count += 1

    def state_dict(self) -> Dict[str, int]:
        return {"count": self.count}

    def load_state_dict(self, state: Dict[str, int]) -> None:
        self.count = int(state["count"])
```

```diff
+
+def build_evaluator(model, cfg: TrainConfig, device: str, data_parallel: bool,
+                    heldout: np.ndarray, piece_len: int) -> Callable:
+    """The held-out eval: evaluate(n_pieces) -> (full, subset).
+
+    Reads pieces 0 .. n_pieces - 1 of heldout.bin (None: all of them) in
+    batches of micro_batch, under the training autocast, in eval mode with no
+    grad. full covers every piece read; subset the first SUBSET_PIECES of them,
+    or None if fewer were read. It touches neither the training RNG -- the
+    model draws none -- nor the data position, and it leaves the model in the
+    mode it found it in.
+    """
+    import torch
+
+    forward = torch.nn.DataParallel(model) if data_parallel else model
+    autocast = _autocast(cfg, device)
+
+    def evaluate(n_pieces: Optional[int]) -> Tuple[Dict[str, Any], Optional[Dict[str, Any]]]:
+        full, subset, seen = EvalAccumulator(), EvalAccumulator(), 0
+        was_training = model.training
+        model.eval()
+        try:
+            with torch.no_grad():
+                for inputs, targets in piece_batches(heldout, piece_len, n_pieces,
+                                                     cfg.micro_batch):
+                    x = torch.from_numpy(inputs).to(device, non_blocking=True)
+                    y = torch.from_numpy(targets).to(device, non_blocking=True)
+                    with autocast:
+                        # "none" positionally: DataParallel copies keyword
+                        # arguments to every GPU, and a last batch of one
+                        # piece would reach the second as keywords alone.
+                        losses, stats = forward(x, y, "none")
+                    losses = losses.float().cpu().numpy()
+                    stats = {k: v.float().cpu().numpy() for k, v in stats.items()}
+                    full.add(losses, stats)
+                    keep = min(len(losses), SUBSET_PIECES - seen)
+                    if keep > 0:
+                        subset.add(losses[:keep], {k: v[:keep] for k, v in stats.items()})
+                    seen += len(losses)
+        finally:
+            model.train(was_training)
+        return full.result(), (subset.result() if subset.tokens else None)
+
+    return evaluate
+
+
+def in_run_eval(evaluate: Callable) -> Dict[str, Any]:
+    """An "eval" record: the first SUBSET_PIECES pieces."""
+    result, _ = evaluate(SUBSET_PIECES)
+    return {"subset_loss": result["loss"], "subset_tokens": result["tokens"],
+            "buckets": result["buckets"], "decay": result.get("decay"),
+            "state_rms": result.get("state_rms")}
+
+
+def final_eval(evaluate: Callable, skips: SkipCounter) -> Dict[str, Any]:
+    """final_eval.json: the full set (the binding A1 metric), the subset (free:
+    it is inside the full set), buckets and monitors over the full set, and the
+    skip count design 7.3 bounds."""
+    full, subset = evaluate(None)
+    return {"full_loss": full["loss"], "full_tokens": full["tokens"],
+            "subset_loss": subset["loss"], "subset_tokens": subset["tokens"],
+            "buckets": full["buckets"], "decay": full.get("decay"),
+            "state_rms": full.get("state_rms"), "skipped_steps": skips.count}
 
 
 
@@ -187,19 +290,43 @@ def run(args: argparse.Namespace) -> Outcome:
           f"= {cfg.total_tokens/1e9:.2f}B, "
           f"{cfg.grad_accum} x micro_batch {cfg.micro_batch}, dtype {cfg.dtype}")
 
+    data = Path(args.data)
+    provenance = load_provenance(data)
+    # Before the model is built: a piece_len that does not fit seq_len stops
+    # the run here, in a second, not after the model is on the GPU.
+    stream = TokenStream.from_path(data, cfg.seq_len, cfg.seed)
+    if provenance is not None:
```

### Task 11: Long-document data prep

Design §6.1. The decisions live in `deluge/data/prep.py`, which is numpy-only and tested with a one-token-per-character fake tokenizer. Those decisions are:
- what qualifies;
- how a document becomes pieces;
- the blake2b split;
- heldout.bin's digest order;
- the yield estimate and the pre-filter calibration.

`scripts/prepare_data.py` becomes three subcommands that wire parquet and the tokenizer into them. `scripts/kaggle_prep.py` renders the Kaggle CPU kernel that runs `long`. Nothing here touches the network in tests. Task 16 runs it for real.

**Files:**
- Create: `deluge/data/prep.py`, `scripts/kaggle_prep.py`
- Modify: `scripts/prepare_data.py`
- Test: `tests/test_prep.py`, `tests/test_prep_scripts.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `TOKEN_DTYPE` and `PROVENANCE` (Task 5); in tests, `TokenStream` and `piece_batches`.
- Produces:
  - `prep.PIECE_LEN == 4097`, `HELDOUT_PERCENT == 1`, `TRAIN == "train.bin"`, `HELDOUT == "heldout.bin"` and `SPLIT_RULE`.
  - `text_digest(text) -> bytes` (blake2b, 16 bytes) and `is_heldout(digest) -> bool`.
  - `cut_pieces(ids, piece_len) -> uint16 [n, piece_len]`.
  - `yield_estimate(gpt2_counts, ratio, prefilter)` and `calibrate_ratio(gpt2_counts, counts, quantile=0.999)`, which returns `{"ratio", "prefilter", ...}`.
  - `prepare_long(batches, encode, prefilter, train_tokens, out, provenance, piece_len=PIECE_LEN)`, which writes `train.bin`, `heldout.bin` and `provenance.json` (with `piece_len`) and returns the provenance.
  - `prepare_data.py measure|long|stream`, and `kaggle_prep.render(dest, owner, commit, prep_args)`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

**Create `tests/test_prep.py`:**

```python
"""Long-document data prep (M1 design 6.1), with a fake tokenizer and no network."""

import hashlib
import json

import numpy as np
import pytest

from deluge.data import TokenStream, piece_batches
from deluge.data.prep import (PIECE_LEN, calibrate_ratio, cut_pieces, is_heldout,
                              prepare_long, text_digest, yield_estimate)

SMALL = 9          # a tiny piece length, so test documents stay short


def encode(texts):
    """One token per character: a document of n characters is n tokens."""
    return [[ord(c) % 1000 for c in text] for text in texts]


def documents(n, length, seed=0):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    return ["".join(chr(97 + x) for x in rng.integers(0, 26, size=length)) for _ in range(n)]


def run(tmp_path, texts, prefilter=0, train_tokens=10**9, counts=None):
    counts = counts if counts is not None else [len(t) for t in texts]
    batches = [(texts[i:i + 7], counts[i:i + 7]) for i in range(0, len(texts), 7)]
    return prepare_long(batches, encode, prefilter, train_tokens, tmp_path,
                        {"dataset": "fake"}, piece_len=SMALL)


def test_the_piece_is_one_sequence_of_4096_plus_its_target():
    assert PIECE_LEN == 4096 + 1


def test_the_split_hashes_the_text_with_blake2b():
    digest = text_digest("a document")
    assert digest == hashlib.blake2b(b"a document", digest_size=16).digest()
    assert is_heldout(digest) == (int.from_bytes(digest, "big") % 100 < 1)


def test_about_one_percent_is_held_out():
    held = sum(is_heldout(text_digest(f"doc {i}")) for i in range(20_000))
    assert 140 <= held <= 260


def test_cut_pieces_drops_the_remainder_and_short_documents():
    assert cut_pieces(list(range(20)), 9).tolist() == [list(range(9)), list(range(9, 18))]
    assert cut_pieces(list(range(8)), 9).shape == (0, 9)
    assert cut_pieces(list(range(9)), 9).shape == (1, 9)     # exactly one piece


def test_duplicates_land_on_the_same_side(tmp_path):
    texts = documents(300, 20) * 2
    record = run(tmp_path, texts)
    assert record["documents"]["heldout"] % 2 == 0
    assert record["documents"]["train"] % 2 == 0


def test_heldout_is_written_in_digest_order(tmp_path):
    texts = documents(3000, 20)
    run(tmp_path, texts)
    held = sorted((text_digest(t), t) for t in texts if is_heldout(text_digest(t)))
    expected = np.concatenate([cut_pieces(encode([t])[0], SMALL).ravel() for _, t in held])
    assert np.array_equal(np.fromfile(tmp_path / "heldout.bin", dtype=np.uint16), expected)


def test_the_output_reads_back_in_piece_mode(tmp_path):
    record = run(tmp_path, documents(500, 30))
    stream = TokenStream.from_path(tmp_path / "train.bin", seq_len=SMALL - 1, seed=0)
    assert stream.n_sequences * SMALL == record["tokens"]["train"]
    heldout = np.fromfile(tmp_path / "heldout.bin", dtype=np.uint16)
    assert sum(len(i) for i, _ in piece_batches(heldout, SMALL, None, 8)) * SMALL == len(heldout)
    provenance = json.loads((tmp_path / "provenance.json").read_text())
    assert provenance["piece_len"] == SMALL and provenance["dataset"] == "fake"
    assert provenance["bytes"]["train.bin"] == 2 * record["tokens"]["train"]


def test_documents_under_one_piece_are_counted_and_dropped(tmp_path):
    # Review focus: the GPT-2 pre-filter is loose, so a document can pass it
    # and still tokenize to less than one piece.
    texts = documents(10, 5) + documents(10, 9, seed=1)
    record = run(tmp_path, texts)
    assert record["documents"]["prefiltered"] == 20
    assert record["documents"]["qualifying"] == 10


def test_the_prefilter_keeps_short_documents_from_the_tokenizer(tmp_path):
    seen = []

    def spy(texts):
        seen.extend(texts)
        return encode(texts)

    texts = documents(10, 30)
    prepare_long([(texts, [100] * 5 + [10] * 5)], spy, 50, 10**9, tmp_path,
                 {}, piece_len=SMALL)
    assert seen == texts[:5]


def test_stops_once_train_holds_the_target(tmp_path):
    record = run(tmp_path, documents(1000, 30), train_tokens=900)
    assert 900 <= record["tokens"]["train"] < 900 + 30
    assert record["documents"]["read"] < 1000


def test_the_yield_estimate_counts_whole_converted_pieces():
    estimate = yield_estimate(np.array([1000, 4000, 9000]), ratio=1.1, prefilter=3000)
    # 4000 * 1.1 = 4400 -> 1 piece; 9000 * 1.1 = 9900 -> 2 pieces.
    assert estimate == {"documents": 3, "prefiltered": 2, "qualifying": 2,
                        "pieces": 3, "tokens": 3 * PIECE_LEN}


def test_calibration_sets_a_loose_prefilter():
    gpt2 = [1000, 2000, 3000, 4000]
    ours = [1100, 2200, 3300, 6000]           # the last document runs at 1.5
    result = calibrate_ratio(gpt2, ours, quantile=1.0)
    assert result["ratio"] == pytest.approx(12600 / 10000)
    assert result["prefilter"] == int(4097 / 1.5) // 100 * 100 == 2700
```

**Create `tests/test_prep_scripts.py`:**

```python
"""The data-prep command lines: argument wiring only, no network."""

import ast
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import pytest

REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
sys.path.insert(0, str(REPO / "scripts"))
import kaggle_prep  # noqa: E402
import prepare_data  # noqa: E402


def test_prepare_data_has_the_three_modes(capsys):
    with pytest.raises(SystemExit) as exit_:
        prepare_data.main(["--help"])
    assert exit_.value.code == 0
    out = capsys.readouterr().out
    assert all(mode in out for mode in ("measure", "long", "stream"))


def test_the_long_mode_needs_a_prefilter_and_a_target():
    with pytest.raises(SystemExit):
        prepare_data.main(["long", "--dataset", "d", "--files", "f", "--tokenizer", "t",
                           "--out", "o"])


def test_the_prep_kernel_is_a_private_cpu_kernel_with_internet(tmp_path):
    kaggle_prep.main(["--owner", "heylaine", "--commit", "abc123", "--dir", str(tmp_path),
                      "--", "--dataset", "HuggingFaceFW/fineweb-edu", "--prefilter", "2700"])
    meta = json.loads((tmp_path / "kernel-metadata.json").read_text())
    assert meta["id"] == "heylaine/deluge-prep-long4k" and meta["is_private"] is True
    assert meta["enable_gpu"] is False and meta["enable_internet"] is True
    code = (tmp_path / "prep_kernel.py").read_text()
    ast.parse(code)
    params = json.loads(ast.literal_eval(code.split("json.loads(")[1].split(")\n")[0]))
    assert params["commit"] == "abc123"
    assert params["args"] == ["--dataset", "HuggingFaceFW/fineweb-edu", "--prefilter", "2700"]
    assert '"--out", "/kaggle/working"' in code


def test_the_prep_kernel_owns_the_output_directory(tmp_path):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="--out"):
        kaggle_prep.render(tmp_path, "o", "c", ["--out", "/elsewhere"])
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the prep module and the scripts**

**Create `deluge/data/prep.py`:**

```python
"""Long-document data preparation, the decisions (M1 design 6.1).

scripts/prepare_data.py does the I/O: the parquet shards, the tokenizer, the
files. What decides the contents of a token file lives here and is testable
without a network: which documents qualify, how a document becomes pieces,
which side of the split it lands on, and the order heldout.bin is written in.

  - A piece is PIECE_LEN = 4097 tokens, one document's, never two:
    TokenStream.batch reads seq_len + 1 tokens, so a 4096-token piece would take
    each document's last target from the next document.
  - A document is cut into whole pieces and the remainder dropped.
  - The split hashes the document's text (blake2b, never Python's salted
    hash()): stable across re-runs, and exact duplicates -- FineWeb is not
    deduplicated across dumps -- land on the same side.
  - heldout.bin is written in digest order, so any prefix of it is a random
    sample of documents. The in-run evals read its first 488 pieces.
"""

import hashlib
import json
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, Iterable, List, Sequence, Tuple

import numpy as np

from .stream import PROVENANCE, TOKEN_DTYPE

PIECE_LEN = 4097
HELDOUT_PERCENT = 1
TRAIN, HELDOUT = "train.bin", "heldout.bin"
SPLIT_RULE = ("blake2b(text utf-8, digest_size=16) as a big-endian integer, "
              "mod 100 < 1: heldout, else train; heldout.bin in digest order")


def text_digest(text: str) -> bytes:
    return hashlib.blake2b(text.encode("utf-8"), digest_size=16).digest()


def is_heldout(digest: bytes) -> bool:
    return int.from_bytes(digest, "big") % 100 < HELDOUT_PERCENT


def cut_pieces(ids: Sequence[int], piece_len: int = PIECE_LEN) -> np.ndarray:
    """[n, piece_len] whole pieces, in order; the remainder is dropped."""
    n = len(ids) // piece_len
    return np.asarray(ids[: n * piece_len], dtype=TOKEN_DTYPE).reshape(n, piece_len)


def yield_estimate(gpt2_counts: np.ndarray, ratio: float, prefilter: int,
                   piece_len: int = PIECE_LEN) -> Dict[str, int]:
    """What the long mode would get from documents of these GPT-2 lengths.

    FineWeb-edu's token_count column is GPT-2 tokens; ratio converts them to
    the training tokenizer's (measured by calibrate_ratio). An estimate: the
    ratio varies by document. Pieces are counted before the 1% held out.
    """
    counts = np.asarray(gpt2_counts, dtype=np.float64)
    kept = counts[counts >= prefilter]
    pieces = np.floor(np.floor(kept * ratio) / piece_len)
    return {"documents": int(counts.size), "prefiltered": int(kept.size),
            "qualifying": int((pieces > 0).sum()), "pieces": int(pieces.sum()),
            "tokens": int(pieces.sum()) * piece_len}


def calibrate_ratio(gpt2_counts: Sequence[int], counts: Sequence[int],
                    piece_len: int = PIECE_LEN, quantile: float = 0.999) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """The training-tokenizer / GPT-2 token ratio over a sample of documents,
    and a loose pre-filter: the GPT-2 length below which a document cannot
    reach one piece unless its ratio is above the quantile-th of the sample.
    Rounded down to a multiple of 100."""
    gpt2 = np.asarray(gpt2_counts, dtype=np.float64)
    ours = np.asarray(counts, dtype=np.float64)
    per_document = ours / gpt2
    high = float(np.quantile(per_document, quantile))
    return {"documents": int(gpt2.size), "ratio": float(ours.sum() / gpt2.sum()),
            "ratio_quantile": quantile, "ratio_at_quantile": high,
            "prefilter": int(piece_len / high) // 100 * 100}


def prepare_long(batches: Iterable[Tuple[List[str], List[int]]],
                 encode: Callable[[List[str]], List[List[int]]],
                 prefilter: int, train_tokens: int, out: Path,
                 provenance: Dict[str, Any], piece_len: int = PIECE_LEN) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Write out/train.bin, out/heldout.bin and out/provenance.json.

    batches yields (texts, gpt2_counts) per parquet batch; encode tokenizes a
    list of texts without special tokens. Stops once train.bin holds at least
    train_tokens. provenance carries the source description and is completed
    with the counts. Returns it.
    """
    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    heldout: List[Tuple[bytes, np.ndarray]] = []
    docs = {"read": 0, "prefiltered": 0, "qualifying": 0, "train": 0, "heldout": 0}
    written = 0
    with open(out / TRAIN, "wb") as train:
        for texts, gpt2_counts in batches:
            docs["read"] += len(texts)
            chosen = [t for t, n in zip(texts, gpt2_counts) if n >= prefilter]
            docs["prefiltered"] += len(chosen)
            for text, ids in zip(chosen, encode(chosen) if chosen else []):
                pieces = cut_pieces(ids, piece_len)
                if not len(pieces):
                    continue
                docs["qualifying"] += 1
                digest = text_digest(text)
                if is_heldout(digest):
                    heldout.append((digest, pieces))
                    docs["heldout"] += 1
                    continue
                pieces.tofile(train)
                written += pieces.size
                docs["train"] += 1
                if written >= train_tokens:
                    break
            if written >= train_tokens:
                break
    held = 0
    with open(out / HELDOUT, "wb") as handle:
        for _, pieces in sorted(heldout, key=lambda item: item[0]):
            pieces.tofile(handle)
            held += pieces.size
    record = {**provenance, "piece_len": piece_len, "prefilter_gpt2_tokens": prefilter,
              "split": SPLIT_RULE, "documents": docs,
              "tokens": {"train": written, "heldout": held},
              "dtype": np.dtype(TOKEN_DTYPE).name,
              "bytes": {TRAIN: (out / TRAIN).stat().st_size,
                        HELDOUT: (out / HELDOUT).stat().st_size}}
    (out / PROVENANCE).write_text(json.dumps(record, indent=2) + "\n")
    return record
```

```diff
+        raise SystemExit(f"no files match {files!r} in {dataset}@{revision}")
+    return fs, revision, paths

+    import pyarrow.parquet as pq

+                         f"in deluge/data/stream.py")
```

**Create `scripts/kaggle_prep.py`:**

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python
"""Render the Kaggle CPU kernel that builds the M1 dataset (M1 design 6.1 step 6).

The kernel, deluge-prep-long4k, is private, CPU-only and has internet. It
clones this repo at a pinned commit and runs `scripts/prepare_data.py long`
into /kaggle/working, so the hours of download and tokenizing run on Kaggle
rather than on the home PC. Its output becomes the private dataset every M1 arm
attaches (docs/kaggle.md, "Data").

    python scripts/kaggle_prep.py --owner heylaine --commit <sha on GitHub> \\
        --dir /tmp/prep-kernel -- \\
        --dataset HuggingFaceFW/fineweb-edu --files 'sample/10BT/*.parquet' \\
        --revision <sha> --tokenizer mistralai/Mistral-7B-v0.1 \\
        --prefilter <n> --train-tokens 2_400_000_000
    KAGGLE_CONFIG_DIR=~/Downloads kaggle kernels push -p /tmp/prep-kernel

Everything after `--` is passed to `prepare_data.py long`; --out is the
kernel's.
"""

import argparse
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

REPO = "https://github.com/heylain/Deluge"
SLUG = "deluge-prep-long4k"

KERNEL = '''"""Builds the Deluge M1 dataset. Rendered by scripts/kaggle_prep.py."""
import json
import subprocess
import sys

PARAMS = json.loads({params!r})
SRC = "/tmp/deluge-src"
subprocess.run(["git", "clone", "--quiet", PARAMS["repo"], SRC], check=True)
subprocess.run(["git", "-C", SRC, "checkout", "--quiet", PARAMS["commit"]], check=True)
subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "pip", "install", "--quiet", "-e", SRC,
                "huggingface_hub", "pyarrow", "tokenizers"], check=True)
subprocess.run([sys.executable, SRC + "/scripts/prepare_data.py", "long",
                *PARAMS["args"], "--out", "/kaggle/working"], check=True)
'''


def render(dest: Path, owner: str, commit: str, prep_args: list) -> None:
    if "--out" in prep_args:
        raise ValueError("--out is the kernel's: /kaggle/working, whose files become the dataset")
    dest.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    params = json.dumps({"repo": REPO, "commit": commit, "args": prep_args})
    (dest / "prep_kernel.py").write_text(KERNEL.format(params=params))
    (dest / "kernel-metadata.json").write_text(json.dumps({
        "id": f"{owner}/{SLUG}", "title": SLUG, "code_file": "prep_kernel.py",
        "language": "python", "kernel_type": "script", "is_private": True,
        "enable_gpu": False, "enable_internet": True, "machine_shape": "",
        "dataset_sources": [], "competition_sources": [], "kernel_sources": [],
    }, indent=2) + "\n")


def main(argv=None) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.split("\n")[0])
    parser.add_argument("--owner", required=True, help="Kaggle username")
    parser.add_argument("--commit", required=True,
                        help="a commit the kernel can clone from GitHub")
    parser.add_argument("--dir", required=True, type=Path, help="where to render the kernel")
    parser.add_argument("prep_args", nargs=argparse.REMAINDER,
                        help="-- then the arguments for prepare_data.py long")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    prep_args = args.prep_args[1:] if args.prep_args[:1] == ["--"] else args.prep_args
    render(args.dir, args.owner, args.commit, prep_args)
    print(f"rendered {args.dir}; push it with: kaggle kernels push -p {args.dir}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Run them to see them pass**

Run: `$PY -m pytest tests/test_prep.py tests/test_prep_scripts.py -q`
Expected: `16 passed`. Then `$PY -m pytest -q`: `449 passed, 1 skipped`.

- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**

```bash
git add deluge/data/prep.py scripts/prepare_data.py scripts/kaggle_prep.py tests/test_prep.py tests/test_prep_scripts.py
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
data: long-document prep in 4,097-token pieces, split by a text hash

prepare_data.py gains measure (yield from the token_count column, a
calibrated GPT-2 ratio and pre-filter) and long (whole pieces, a 99/1
blake2b split, heldout.bin in digest order, provenance); the old mode is
the stream subcommand. kaggle_prep.py renders the CPU kernel that builds
the dataset on Kaggle.
```

### Task 12: Chain: the `export` flag and the done record

Design §9, "Changes to the chain". `runs.yaml` gains an optional `export` that defaults to false, and the done record gains `side` and `export`. Those two fields and nothing else: the existing rehearsal tests pass unchanged.

**Files:**
- Modify: `deluge/chain/queue.py`, `deluge/chain/step.py`, `configs/chain/runs.yaml` (header comment)
- Test: `tests/test_chain_queue.py`, `tests/test_chain_step.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing new.
- Produces:
  - Every loaded queue entry has `"export": bool`. A non-boolean raises `ConfigError` matching `export must be true or false`.
  - Every done record has `"side": "a" | "b" | None` and `"export": bool`.

### Task 13: The exporter and its workflow

Design §9. `python -m deluge.chain.export --step results|checkpoint` is idempotent. It reads the work off the done records, and each step is skipped once its marker exists. The workflow commits results before the checkpoint step starts, so a Hugging Face failure never holds up a verdict. A step that fails stays quiet until the run has been done for 3 h.

**Files:**
- Modify: `deluge/chain/kaggle.py` (adds `fetch_output`)
- Create: `deluge/chain/export.py`, `configs/chain/export.yaml`, `.github/workflows/export.yml`, `.github/export-commit.sh`
- Test: `tests/test_chain_export.py`; `tests/test_chain_kaggle.py` and `tests/test_chain_workflow.py` change

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: done records with `side` and `export` (Task 12), `kernel_slug` (step.py), `KaggleCLI._run`, and the training output `runs/<name>/{final_eval.json, log.jsonl, provenance.json, step-*.pt}` (Tasks 9–10).
- Produces:
  - `KaggleCLI.fetch_output(slug, dest, pattern)`, a wrapper for `kaggle kernels output <owner>/<slug> -p dest --file-pattern pattern`.
  - `export.due(state, step, root)`, `overdue(record, now)`, `read_log(path)`, `downsample(rows)`, `eval_row(record)` and `write_csv(path, rows, columns=None)`.
  - `export_results(record, kaggle, root)`, `export_checkpoint(record, kaggle, upload, root, hf_repo, entry, owner)`, `hf_upload(folder, repo, name)`, `queue_entry(queue, name)`, `run_step(step, state, kaggle, root, now, upload=hf_upload, hf_repo=None, queue=QUEUE, owner="")` and `main(argv)`.
  - The files it writes: `docs/results/runs/<name>/{final_eval.json, evals.csv, train.csv, export.json}`. `evals.csv` has the columns `step`, `tokens_seen`, `subset_loss`, `loss_<bucket>`, `a_min_i`, `a_median_i`, `a_max_i` and `h_rms_i`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Check what the real CLI reports (read-only)**

The exporter's file patterns are anchored on `(^|/)` so they match a nested path or a flat one. kaggle 2.2.4's source (`kernels_output`) `re.search()`es the pattern against each output file's name and writes the file under `-p` at that name, so nested is expected. Confirm it against the rehearsal's own output. This only reads from the user's account.

```bash
S=$(mktemp -d)
python -m venv $S/venv && $S/venv/bin/pip install -q "kaggle==2.2.4"
KAGGLE_CONFIG_DIR=~/Downloads $S/venv/bin/kaggle kernels output heylaine/deluge-smoke-b -p $S/out --file-pattern '(^|/)log\.jsonl$'
find $S/out -type f
```

Expected: `$S/out/runs/smoke/log.jsonl` (nested), plus `deluge-smoke-b.log`. If the file lands at `$S/out/log.jsonl` instead, nothing changes, because the tests cover both. If nothing matches at all, look at the names `find` printed and fix `RESULTS_FILES` and `CHECKPOINT_FILES` in Step 4 before going on.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing tests**

**Create `tests/test_chain_export.py`:**

```python
"""The exporter (M1 design 9) against a fake Kaggle and a fake Hugging Face."""

import json
from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
from pathlib import Path

import pytest

from deluge.chain.export import (GRACE, TRAIN_ROWS, downsample, due, eval_row,
                                 export_checkpoint, export_results, queue_entry,
                                 read_log, run_step)

NOW = datetime(2026, 10, 20, 12, 0, tzinfo=timezone.utc)
DONE = {"name": "m1-a0-cfc-s1", "run_id": "m1-a0-cfc-s1@t", "commit": "abc123",
        "sessions": 1, "finished": (NOW - timedelta(hours=1)).isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
        "side": "a", "export": True}
FINAL = {"step": 1907, "full_loss": 3.21, "subset_loss": 3.2, "skipped_steps": 2}


def log_lines(n_steps=1200):
    lines = [json.dumps({"event": "start", "step": 0})]
    for step in range(1, n_steps + 1):
        lines.append(json.dumps({"event": "step", "step": step, "loss": 10.0 / step,
                                 "lr": 1e-4, "tokens_seen": step * 262144,
                                 "loss_scale": 65536.0, "skipped_steps": 0,
                                 "grad_norm": 0.5, "tokens_per_second": 20000.0,
                                 "peak_alloc_gib": 9.0, "peak_reserved_gib": 11.0}))
    # A resumed session replays steps 1001-1002 with different losses.
    lines += [json.dumps({"event": "step", "step": s, "loss": 1.0, "lr": 1e-4})
              for s in (1001, 1002)]
    for step in (190, 380):
        lines.append(json.dumps({"event": "eval", "step": step, "tokens_seen": step * 262144,
                                 "subset_loss": 4.0, "buckets": {"0-512": 4.1,
                                 "512-1536": 4.0, "1536-4096": 3.9},
                                 "decay": {"min": [0.9, 0.8], "median": [0.95, 0.9],
                                           "max": [0.999, 0.99]},
                                 "state_rms": [1.0, 1.1]}))
    lines.append(json.dumps({"event": "eval", "step": 380, "subset_loss": 3.9}))
    lines.append('{"event": "step", "step": 1201, "lo')          # cut off by a kill
    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n"


class FakeKaggle:
    """Writes a kernel output under dest the way kaggle 2.2.4 does: at the
    file's path inside the output (runs/<name>/...), or flat if nested=False."""

    def __init__(self, files, nested=True, error=None):
        self.files, self.nested, self.error = files, nested, error
        self.calls = []

    def fetch_output(self, slug, dest, pattern):
        import re
        self.calls.append((slug, pattern))
        if self.error:
            raise self.error
        for name, content in self.files.items():
            path = f"runs/{DONE['name']}/{name}" if self.nested else name
            if re.search(pattern, path):
                target = Path(dest) / path
                target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
                target.write_bytes(content if isinstance(content, bytes) else content.encode())


def kernel_files():
    return {"final_eval.json": json.dumps(FINAL), "log.jsonl": log_lines(),
            "provenance.json": json.dumps({"piece_len": 4097}),
            "step-000001905.pt": b"older", "step-000001907.pt": b"newest",
            "session.json": "{}"}


def state(*records):
    return {"done": list(records)}


# ---- selection ----------------------------------------------------------------- #

def test_only_exportable_records_without_a_marker_are_due(tmp_path):
    records = [DONE,
               {**DONE, "name": "smoke", "export": False},
               {**DONE, "name": "old"} | {"side": None},
               {**DONE, "name": "skipped", "skipped": True},
               {**DONE, "name": "legacy"} | {"export": None}]
    assert [r["name"] for r in due(state(*records), "results", tmp_path)] == ["m1-a0-cfc-s1"]
    (tmp_path / "m1-a0-cfc-s1").mkdir()
    (tmp_path / "m1-a0-cfc-s1" / "final_eval.json").write_text("{}")
    assert due(state(DONE), "results", tmp_path) == []
    # Each step has its own marker: the checkpoint is still due.
    assert due(state(DONE), "checkpoint", tmp_path) == [DONE]


# ---- results ------------------------------------------------------------------- #

def test_the_log_is_deduplicated_last_write_wins(tmp_path):
    path = tmp_path / "log.jsonl"
    path.write_text(log_lines())
    steps, evals = read_log(path)
    assert [s["step"] for s in steps] == list(range(1, 1201))
    assert steps[1000]["loss"] == 1.0                      # step 1001, replayed
    assert [e["step"] for e in evals] == [190, 380] and evals[1]["subset_loss"] == 3.9


def test_downsampling_keeps_about_the_limit_and_the_last_row():
    rows = [{"step": i} for i in range(1, 1908)]
    kept = downsample(rows)
    assert len(kept) <= TRAIN_ROWS + 1 and kept[0]["step"] == 1 and kept[-1]["step"] == 1907
    assert downsample(rows[:10]) == rows[:10]


def test_an_eval_row_flattens_buckets_and_per_layer_monitors():
    row = eval_row({"step": 190, "tokens_seen": 5, "subset_loss": 4.0,
                    "buckets": {"0-512": 4.1}, "decay": {"min": [0.9], "median": [0.95],
                                                         "max": [0.99]}, "state_rms": [1.2]})
    assert row == {"step": 190, "tokens_seen": 5, "subset_loss": 4.0, "loss_0_512": 4.1,
                   "a_min_0": 0.9, "a_median_0": 0.95, "a_max_0": 0.99, "h_rms_0": 1.2}


@pytest.mark.parametrize("nested", [True, False])
def test_results_are_written_from_the_last_sessions_kernel(tmp_path, nested):
    # Review focus: whether the CLI reports runs/<name>/x or x, the files are found.
    kaggle = FakeKaggle(kernel_files(), nested=nested)
    out = export_results(DONE, kaggle, tmp_path)
    assert kaggle.calls[0][0] == "deluge-m1-a0-cfc-s1-a"
    assert json.loads((out / "final_eval.json").read_text()) == FINAL
    train = (out / "train.csv").read_text().splitlines()
    assert train[0].startswith("step,tokens_seen,loss,lr,loss_scale,skipped_steps")
    assert len(train) - 1 <= TRAIN_ROWS + 1
    evals = (out / "evals.csv").read_text().splitlines()
    assert len(evals) == 3 and "a_max_1" in evals[0]


def test_results_without_a_final_eval_write_nothing(tmp_path):
    files = kernel_files()
    del files["final_eval.json"]
    with pytest.raises(Exception, match="final_eval.json"):
        export_results(DONE, FakeKaggle(files), tmp_path)
    assert not (tmp_path / DONE["name"] / "final_eval.json").exists()


# ---- checkpoint ---------------------------------------------------------------- #

def test_the_newest_checkpoint_goes_to_hf_with_its_log_and_run_json(tmp_path):
    uploads = []

    def upload(folder, repo, name):
        uploads.append((repo, name, sorted(p.name for p in folder.iterdir()),
                        json.loads((folder / "run.json").read_text()),
                        (folder / "step-000001907.pt").read_bytes()))

    entry = {"name": DONE["name"], "model": "configs/ablations/A0_scan_only.yaml"}
    out = export_checkpoint(DONE, FakeKaggle(kernel_files()), upload, tmp_path,
                            "heylain/deluge-runs", entry, "heylaine")
    [(repo, name, files, run_json, content)] = uploads
    assert (repo, name) == ("heylain/deluge-runs", "m1-a0-cfc-s1")
    assert files == ["log.jsonl", "run.json", "step-000001907.pt"] and content == b"newest"
    assert run_json["run"] == entry and run_json["done"] == DONE
    assert run_json["provenance"] == {"piece_len": 4097}
    marker = json.loads((out / "export.json").read_text())
    assert marker["path"] == "m1-a0-cfc-s1/step-000001907.pt" and marker["size"] == 6
    assert marker["source_kernel"] == "heylaine/deluge-m1-a0-cfc-s1-a"
    assert len(marker["sha256"]) == 64


def test_no_hf_repo_fails_the_checkpoint_step_only(tmp_path):
    with pytest.raises(Exception, match="hf_repo"):
        export_checkpoint(DONE, FakeKaggle(kernel_files()), None, tmp_path, None, None, "o")
    assert not (tmp_path / DONE["name"] / "export.json").exists()


def test_a_failed_upload_writes_no_marker(tmp_path):
    def upload(*_):
        raise RuntimeError("HF is down")

    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError):
        export_checkpoint(DONE, FakeKaggle(kernel_files()), upload, tmp_path,
                          "heylain/deluge-runs", None, "o")
    assert not (tmp_path / DONE["name"] / "export.json").exists()


# ---- one workflow run -------------------------------------------------------------- #

def test_a_transient_failure_is_quiet_until_the_grace_runs_out(tmp_path):
    # Review focus: an expired HF token or a Kaggle outage must not email
    # every hour from the first; it must once the run has waited past GRACE.
    broken = FakeKaggle({}, error=RuntimeError("Kaggle is down"))
    assert run_step("results", state(DONE), broken, tmp_path, NOW) == 0
    late = {**DONE, "finished": (NOW - GRACE - timedelta(minutes=1)).isoformat()}
    assert run_step("results", state(late), broken, tmp_path, NOW) == 1


def test_a_step_already_done_is_not_repeated(tmp_path):
    kaggle = FakeKaggle(kernel_files())
    assert run_step("results", state(DONE), kaggle, tmp_path, NOW) == 0
    assert run_step("results", state(DONE), kaggle, tmp_path, NOW) == 0
    assert len(kaggle.calls) == 1


def test_results_do_not_wait_on_the_checkpoint(tmp_path):
    kaggle = FakeKaggle(kernel_files())
    late = {**DONE, "finished": (NOW - GRACE * 2).isoformat()}
    assert run_step("checkpoint", state(late), kaggle, tmp_path, NOW, hf_repo=None) == 1
    assert run_step("results", state(late), kaggle, tmp_path, NOW) == 0
    assert (tmp_path / DONE["name"] / "final_eval.json").exists()


def test_the_queue_entry_is_read_without_validating_other_entries(tmp_path):
    queue = tmp_path / "runs.yaml"
    queue.write_text("runs:\n  - name: m1-a0-cfc-s1\n    model: m.yaml\n"
                     "  - name: Bad_Name\n")
    assert queue_entry(queue, "m1-a0-cfc-s1") == {"name": "m1-a0-cfc-s1", "model": "m.yaml"}
    assert queue_entry(queue, "gone") is None
```

**Modify `tests/test_chain_workflow.py`:**

```diff
--- a/tests/test_chain_workflow.py
+++ b/tests/test_chain_workflow.py
@@ -1,7 +1,9 @@
 """The chain workflow's safety properties, read from the YAML itself."""
 
+import subprocess
 from pathlib import Path
 
+import pytest
 import yaml
 
 WORKFLOW = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / ".github/workflows/chain.yml"
@@ -60,3 +62,68 @@ def test_a_state_push_that_races_a_human_push_is_retried():
     workflow, _ = load()
     commit = next(s for s in workflow["jobs"]["tick"]["steps"] if s.get("name") == "Commit state")
     assert "for attempt in" in commit["run"] and "exit 1" in commit["run"]
+
+
+# ---- export.yml (M1 design 9) ------------------------------------------------- #
+
+EXPORT = WORKFLOW.with_name("export.yml")
+
+
+def load_export():
+    workflow = yaml.safe_load(EXPORT.read_text())
+    return workflow, workflow.get("on", workflow.get(True))

+def test_export_runs_hourly_and_by_hand_only():
+    workflow, triggers = load_export()
+    assert set(triggers) == {"schedule", "workflow_dispatch"}
+    assert triggers["schedule"] == [{"cron": "17 * * * *"}]
+    assert workflow["concurrency"] == {"group": "export", "cancel-in-progress": False}
+    assert workflow["permissions"] == {"contents": "write"}
+
+
+def test_export_runs_on_master_with_a_timeout():
+    workflow, _ = load_export()
+    job = workflow["jobs"]["export"]
+    assert job["if"] == "github.ref == 'refs/heads/master'"
+    assert job["timeout-minutes"] == 60
+    assert job["steps"][0]["with"]["ref"] == "master"
+
+
+def test_results_are_committed_before_the_checkpoint_step_starts():
+    workflow, _ = load_export()
+    names = [s.get("name") or s.get("id") for s in workflow["jobs"]["export"]["steps"]]
+    assert names.index("Commit results") < names.index("Checkpoints")
+
+
+def test_only_the_checkpoint_step_sees_the_hf_token():
+    workflow, _ = load_export()
+    steps = {s.get("id"): s for s in workflow["jobs"]["export"]["steps"] if s.get("id")}
+    assert "HF_TOKEN" in steps["checkpoint"]["env"]
+    assert "HF_TOKEN" not in steps["results"]["env"]
+
+
+def test_export_pins_the_clis_it_parses():
+    workflow, _ = load_export()
+    install = next(s for s in workflow["jobs"]["export"]["steps"] if "pip install" in s.get("run", ""))
+    assert '"kaggle==2.2.4"' in install["run"] and '"huggingface_hub==' in install["run"]
+
+
+def test_the_commit_script_retries_a_racing_push():
+    script = (EXPORT.parents[1] / "export-commit.sh").read_text()
+    assert "git add docs/results/runs" in script
+    assert "git pull --rebase" in script and "for attempt in" in script
+
+
+@pytest.mark.parametrize("path", ["docs/results/runs/m1-a0-cfc-s1/final_eval.json",
+                                  "docs/results/runs/m1-a0-cfc-s1/export.json",
+                                  "deluge/data/tokenizer.py"])
+def test_nothing_the_repo_commits_is_gitignored(path):
+    # An unanchored `runs/` in .gitignore matches docs/results/runs too: every
+    # export commit would come out empty while the workflow stayed green.
+    # `data/` would likewise hide new files in deluge/data. Anchor them: /runs/.
+    code = subprocess.run(["git", "check-ignore", "-q", path],
+                          cwd=EXPORT.parents[2]).returncode
+    if code == 128:
+        pytest.skip("not a git checkout")
+    assert code == 1, f"{path} is gitignored"
```
