**Create `deluge/chain/export.py`:**

```python
"""Export finished runs: results into the repo, checkpoints to Hugging Face.

Run hourly by .github/workflows/export.yml, apart from the chain's tick so a
tick never waits on a multi-GB transfer (M1 design 9). Idempotent, with no
ledger of its own: the work is read off the done records in
.github/chain/state.json, and each of a run's two steps is skipped once its
marker is in the repo.

  results     docs/results/runs/<name>/: final_eval.json (the marker),
              evals.csv and a downsampled train.csv, from the output of the
              kernel that ran the last session. The workflow commits them at
              once, so a verdict never waits on Hugging Face.
  checkpoint  the newest step-*.pt, the full log.jsonl and a run.json to
              <name>/ in the private HF repo configs/chain/export.yaml names;
              then docs/results/runs/<name>/export.json (the marker).

A failed step writes nothing and the next hourly run tries again. The exit
code is 1 -- the failed workflow emails you -- only for a run done more than
GRACE ago whose step still fails, so a transient Kaggle or HF error retries
quietly.
"""

import argparse
import csv
import hashlib
import json
import math
import os
import shutil
import sys
import tempfile
from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

import yaml

from .kaggle import KaggleCLI
from .step import kernel_slug

STATE = Path(".github/chain/state.json")
QUEUE = Path("configs/chain/runs.yaml")
CONFIG = Path("configs/chain/export.yaml")
RESULTS = Path("docs/results/runs")
GRACE = timedelta(hours=3)
STEPS = ("results", "checkpoint")
MARKERS = {"results": "final_eval.json", "checkpoint": "export.json"}
TRAIN_ROWS = 500
TRAIN_COLUMNS = ("step", "tokens_seen", "loss", "lr", "loss_scale", "skipped_steps",
                 "grad_norm", "tokens_per_second", "peak_alloc_gib", "peak_reserved_gib")
RESULTS_FILES = r"(^|/)(final_eval\.json|log\.jsonl)$"
CHECKPOINT_FILES = r"(^|/)(step-\d{9}\.pt|log\.jsonl|provenance\.json)$"


class ExportError(RuntimeError):
    """A step could not finish. It wrote nothing; the next run retries it."""


# ---- what to do ------------------------------------------------------------ #

def due(state: Dict[str, Any], step: str, root: Path) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    """Done records that asked for export and still lack this step's marker."""
    return [record for record in state["done"]
            if record.get("export") and not record.get("skipped") and record.get("side")
            and not (root / record["name"] / MARKERS[step]).exists()]


def overdue(record: Dict[str, Any], now: datetime) -> bool:
    return now - datetime.fromisoformat(record["finished"]) > GRACE


# ---- log.jsonl to csv -------------------------------------------------------- #

def read_log(path: Path) -> Tuple[List[Dict[str, Any]], List[Dict[str, Any]]]:
    """(step records, eval records), each deduplicated by step -- a resumed
    session replays steps since its checkpoint, and the last write wins --
    and sorted by step."""
    steps: Dict[int, Dict[str, Any]] = {}
    evals: Dict[int, Dict[str, Any]] = {}
    for line in path.read_text().splitlines():
        try:
            record = json.loads(line)
        except json.JSONDecodeError:
            continue                     # a line cut off by a kill
        if record.get("event") == "step":
            steps[record["step"]] = record
        elif record.get("event") == "eval":
            evals[record["step"]] = record
    return [steps[k] for k in sorted(steps)], [evals[k] for k in sorted(evals)]


def downsample(rows: List[Dict[str, Any]], limit: int = TRAIN_ROWS) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    """Every k-th row, so at most about `limit`, always keeping the last."""
    if len(rows) <= limit:
        return rows
    every = math.ceil(len(rows) / limit)
    kept = rows[::every]
    return kept if kept[-1] is rows[-1] else kept + [rows[-1]]


def eval_row(record: Dict[str, Any]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """One evals.csv row: the subset loss, the buckets, and per recurrent layer
    i the decay summary (a_min_i, a_median_i, a_max_i) and h_rms_i."""
    row = {"step": record["step"], "tokens_seen": record.get("tokens_seen"),
           "subset_loss": record["subset_loss"]}
    for bucket, loss in (record.get("buckets") or {}).items():
        row[f"loss_{bucket.replace('-', '_')}"] = loss
    decay = record.get("decay") or {}
    for i, (lo, mid, hi) in enumerate(zip(decay.get("min", []), decay.get("median", []),
                                          decay.get("max", []))):
        row.update({f"a_min_{i}": lo, f"a_median_{i}": mid, f"a_max_{i}": hi})
    for i, rms in enumerate(record.get("state_rms") or []):
        row[f"h_rms_{i}"] = rms
    return row


def write_csv(path: Path, rows: List[Dict[str, Any]], columns=None) -> None:
    columns = list(columns or (rows[0].keys() if rows else []))
    with open(path, "w", newline="") as handle:
        writer = csv.DictWriter(handle, fieldnames=columns, extrasaction="ignore")
        writer.writeheader()
        writer.writerows(rows)


# ---- the two steps ------------------------------------------------------------ #

def _find(directory: Path, name: str) -> Path:
    found = sorted(directory.rglob(name))
    if not found:
        raise ExportError(f"the kernel output has no {name}")
    return found[0]


def export_results(record: Dict[str, Any], kaggle, root: Path) -> Path:
    slug = kernel_slug(record["name"], record["side"])
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
        kaggle.fetch_output(slug, Path(tmp), RESULTS_FILES)
        final = _find(Path(tmp), "final_eval.json")
        steps, evals = read_log(_find(Path(tmp), "log.jsonl"))
        out = root / record["name"]
        out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        write_csv(out / "train.csv", downsample(steps), TRAIN_COLUMNS)
        write_csv(out / "evals.csv", [eval_row(r) for r in evals])
        shutil.copy2(final, out / "final_eval.json")        # last: the marker
    return out


def sha256(path: Path) -> str:
    digest = hashlib.sha256()
    with open(path, "rb") as handle:
        for block in iter(lambda: handle.read(1 << 20), b""):
            digest.update(block)
    return digest.hexdigest()


def hf_upload(folder: Path, repo: str, name: str) -> None:
    """Upload folder to <repo>/<name>/ in one commit, creating repo private."""
    from huggingface_hub import HfApi

    token = os.environ.get("HF_TOKEN")
    if not token:
        raise ExportError("HF_TOKEN is not set (a repo secret, in the workflow)")
    api = HfApi(token=token)
    api.create_repo(repo, repo_type="model", private=True, exist_ok=True)
    api.upload_folder(folder_path=str(folder), path_in_repo=name, repo_id=repo,
                      repo_type="model", commit_message=f"{name}: checkpoint, log, run.json")


def export_checkpoint(record: Dict[str, Any], kaggle, upload: Callable, root: Path,
                      hf_repo: Optional[str], entry: Optional[Dict[str, Any]],
                      owner: str) -> Path:
    if not hf_repo:
        raise ExportError(f"set hf_repo in {CONFIG}: the checkpoint has nowhere to go")
    name, slug = record["name"], kernel_slug(record["name"], record["side"])
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
        fetched = Path(tmp) / "fetched"
        kaggle.fetch_output(slug, fetched, CHECKPOINT_FILES)
        checkpoints = sorted(fetched.rglob("step-*.pt"), key=lambda p: p.name)
        if not checkpoints:
            raise ExportError(f"the output of {slug} has no step-*.pt")
        newest = checkpoints[-1]
        stage = Path(tmp) / "stage"
        stage.mkdir()
        shutil.move(str(newest), stage / newest.name)
        shutil.move(str(_find(fetched, "log.jsonl")), stage / "log.jsonl")
        provenance = sorted(fetched.rglob("provenance.json"))
        (stage / "run.json").write_text(json.dumps({
            "run": entry, "done": record,
            "provenance": json.loads(provenance[0].read_text()) if provenance else None,
        }, indent=2) + "\n")
        size, digest = (stage / newest.name).stat().st_size, sha256(stage / newest.name)
        upload(stage, hf_repo, name)
    out = root / name
    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    (out / "export.json").write_text(json.dumps({
        "hf_repo": hf_repo, "path": f"{name}/{newest.name}", "sha256": digest,
        "size": size, "source_kernel": f"{owner}/{slug}"}, indent=2) + "\n")
    return out


# ---- one run of the workflow step ------------------------------------------- #

def queue_entry(queue: Path, name: str) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
    """The run's entry in runs.yaml, unvalidated: a typo in a later entry must
    not block exporting an earlier run."""
    runs = (yaml.safe_load(queue.read_text()) or {}).get("runs") or []
    return next((run for run in runs if isinstance(run, dict) and run.get("name") == name), None)


def run_step(step: str, state: Dict[str, Any], kaggle, root: Path, now: datetime,
             upload: Callable = hf_upload, hf_repo: Optional[str] = None,
             queue: Path = QUEUE, owner: str = "") -> int:
    code = 0
    for record in due(state, step, root):
        name = record["name"]
        try:
            if step == "results":
                export_results(record, kaggle, root)
            else:
                export_checkpoint(record, kaggle, upload, root, hf_repo,
                                  queue_entry(queue, name), owner)
        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - any failure: retry next hour
            late = overdue(record, now)
            print(f"[export] {name}: {step} failed{' (overdue)' if late else ''}: "
                  f"{error!r}", file=sys.stderr)
            code = 1 if late else code
            continue
        print(f"[export] {name}: {step} exported")
    return code


def main(argv=None) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.split("\n")[0])
    parser.add_argument("--step", choices=STEPS, required=True)
    parser.add_argument("--state", type=Path, default=STATE)
    parser.add_argument("--queue", type=Path, default=QUEUE)
    parser.add_argument("--config", type=Path, default=CONFIG)
    parser.add_argument("--root", type=Path, default=RESULTS)
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)

    owner = os.environ.get("KAGGLE_USERNAME")
    if not owner:
        parser.error("KAGGLE_USERNAME is not set (a repo secret, in the workflow)")
    config = yaml.safe_load(args.config.read_text()) or {}
    state = json.loads(args.state.read_text())
    return run_step(args.step, state, KaggleCLI(owner), args.root,
                    datetime.now(timezone.utc), hf_repo=config.get("hf_repo"),
                    queue=args.queue, owner=owner)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

**Create `configs/chain/export.yaml`:**

```yaml
# Where .github/workflows/export.yml puts checkpoints (deluge/chain/export.py):
# a private Hugging Face model repo, "<hf-user>/<repo>", created private if it
# does not exist. The workflow also needs the HF_TOKEN repo secret, a token
# that can write to and create repos in that namespace (docs/kaggle.md,
# "Results and checkpoints").
#
# null until you set it. Checkpoint exports fail until then; results do not.
hf_repo: null
```

**Create `.github/workflows/export.yml`:**

```yaml
# Exports finished runs (deluge/chain/export.py): results into the repo, and
# checkpoints to the private Hugging Face repo in configs/chain/export.yaml.
# See docs/kaggle.md ("Results and checkpoints").
#
# Apart from chain.yml so a tick never waits on a multi-GB transfer. As there:
# no pull_request trigger, so the Kaggle and HF tokens are never reachable
# from a fork's code.
name: export

on:
  schedule:
    - cron: "17 * * * *"
  workflow_dispatch:

concurrency:
  group: export
  cancel-in-progress: false

permissions:
  contents: write

jobs:
  export:
    # Results are committed to master, where the chain's state lives.
    if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/master'
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 60
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          ref: master
          fetch-depth: 0
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with:
          python-version: "3.12"
      # kaggle pinned as in chain.yml; huggingface_hub to the 1.x API that
      # export.py calls (create_repo, upload_folder).
      - run: pip install -e . "kaggle==2.2.4" "huggingface_hub==1.33.0"
      - id: results
        name: Results
        env:
          KAGGLE_USERNAME: ${{ secrets.KAGGLE_USERNAME }}
          KAGGLE_KEY: ${{ secrets.KAGGLE_KEY }}
        run: |
          set +e
          python -m deluge.chain.export --step results
          echo "code=$?" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
      # Committed before the checkpoint step starts: a verdict never waits on HF.
      - name: Commit results
        if: always()
        run: bash .github/export-commit.sh results
      - id: checkpoint
        name: Checkpoints
        env:
          KAGGLE_USERNAME: ${{ secrets.KAGGLE_USERNAME }}
          KAGGLE_KEY: ${{ secrets.KAGGLE_KEY }}
          HF_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.HF_TOKEN }}
        run: |
          set +e
          python -m deluge.chain.export --step checkpoint
          echo "code=$?" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
      - name: Commit checkpoint markers
        if: always()
        run: bash .github/export-commit.sh checkpoints
      - name: Fail if an export is overdue
        if: steps.results.outputs.code != '0' || steps.checkpoint.outputs.code != '0'
        run: exit 1
```

**Create `.github/export-commit.sh`:**

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Commit what deluge/chain/export.py wrote under docs/results/runs, and push
# it. Called twice by .github/workflows/export.yml, once per step. The push
# retries because the chain's tick commits state.json to the same branch.
# -e: a failed `git add` (a path .gitignore matches) must fail the step, not
# leave the commit silently empty.
set -euo pipefail
what="$1"                       # results | checkpoints
[ -d docs/results/runs ] || exit 0
git add docs/results/runs
git diff --cached --quiet && exit 0
runs=$(git diff --cached --name-only | cut -d/ -f4 | sort -u | paste -sd, -)
git config user.name "github-actions[bot]"
git config user.email "41898282+github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com"
git commit --quiet -m "export: $what ($runs)"
for attempt in 1 2 3 4 5; do
  git pull --rebase --quiet && git push --quiet && exit 0
  sleep $((attempt * 5))
done
echo "::error::could not push the export after 5 attempts"
exit 1
```

chain: hourly export of results to the repo and checkpoints to HF

own marker: results (final_eval.json, evals.csv, a downsampled
train.csv) committed at once, then the newest checkpoint, the full log
and run.json uploaded to the private HF repo in configs/chain/export.yaml.
Failures retry quietly for 3 h before the workflow fails. The commit step

matching docs/results/runs.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

### Task 14: The verdict rules, the script, and the results page

Design §7.3, §8.3 and §8.4. The rules are pure functions over `Run` records:
- stability is judged first, with fixed thresholds only;
- noise is then computed from stable runs only;
- every boundary carries `EPS`, so a value exactly on it lands on the side the rule names.

The script reads only `docs/results/runs/` and writes only between the marker comments in `docs/results/m1.md`. A rerun under a new name can stand in for its role (`--run ROLE=RUN`), and the binding A1 verdict does not wait on the early runs. The page's caveats are known now, so they are written now.

**Files:**
- Create: `deluge/eval/__init__.py`, `deluge/eval/m1.py`, `scripts/m1_verdict.py`, `docs/results/m1.md`
- Test: `tests/test_m1_verdict.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the files Task 13 writes (it uses `export.eval_row` and `write_csv` to build test fixtures).
- Produces:
  - `m1.Run(name, full_loss, subset_loss, best_in_run_subset, decay_min, decay_max, steps, skipped_steps, buckets)` and `load_run(directory)`.
  - `stability(run) -> List[str]`, empty when the run is stable.
  - `a0_verdict(cfc_s1, cfc_s2, transformer) -> A0Verdict(outcome: "pass" | "behind" | "unstable", noise0, gap, unstable)`, with `.bar`.
  - `early_read(cfc_s1, other, noise0) -> float`.
  - `a1_verdict(cfc[3], conv[3]) -> A1Verdict(outcome: "GO" | "NO-GO" | "no verdict", noise, gap, bar, unstable)`.
  - `render_a0`, `render_early`, `render_a1` and `replace_block(text, key, body)`.
  - The run-name tuples `A0_RUNS`, `EARLY_RUNS`, `A1_CFC` and `A1_CONV`.
  - `scripts/m1_verdict.py`: `main(argv, root, page)`, with `a0`, `a1`, `--dry-run RUN` and `--run ROLE=RUN` (repeatable; RUN must be an `m1-*` run).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

**Create `tests/test_m1_verdict.py`:**

```python
"""The pre-registered M1 rules (design 7.3 and 8.3), at their boundaries."""

import json
import math
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import pytest

from deluge.chain.export import eval_row, write_csv
from deluge.eval.m1 import (A0_RUNS, A1_CFC, A1_CONV, EARLY_RUNS, Run, a0_verdict,
                            a1_verdict, early_read, load_run, replace_block, stability)

REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
sys.path.insert(0, str(REPO / "scripts"))
import m1_verdict  # noqa: E402


def run(name="r", loss=3.0, subset=None, best=None, low=(0.9,), high=(0.999,),
        steps=1000, skipped=0):
    return Run(name=name, full_loss=loss, subset_loss=loss if subset is None else subset,
               best_in_run_subset=best, decay_min=list(low), decay_max=list(high),
               steps=steps, skipped_steps=skipped)


# ---- stability (7.3) ------------------------------------------------------------ #

def test_a_healthy_run_is_stable():
    assert stability(run(best=3.0)) == []


@pytest.mark.parametrize("loss", [math.nan, math.inf])
def test_a_non_finite_loss_is_unstable(loss):
    assert stability(run(loss=loss))


def test_late_divergence_is_judged_at_exactly_two_hundredths():
    assert stability(run(subset=3.02, best=3.0)) == []
    assert stability(run(subset=3.0201, best=3.0))


def test_late_divergence_needs_in_run_evals():
    assert stability(run(subset=9.0, best=None)) == []


def test_a_layer_with_every_head_forgetting_is_unstable():
    assert stability(run(low=(0.9, 0.1), high=(0.999, 0.49)))


def test_a_layer_with_every_head_frozen_is_unstable():
    assert stability(run(low=(0.99991,), high=(0.99999,)))


def test_a_layer_with_heads_on_both_sides_is_stable():
    assert stability(run(low=(0.4,), high=(0.99995,))) == []


def test_one_percent_of_skipped_steps_is_the_limit():
    assert stability(run(steps=1000, skipped=10)) == []
    assert stability(run(steps=1000, skipped=11))


# ---- A0 ------------------------------------------------------------------------- #

def test_the_noise_floor_binds_for_a_lucky_close_pair():
    verdict = a0_verdict(run("s1", 3.0), run("s2", 3.001), run("x", 3.0))
    assert verdict.noise0 == 0.01


def test_a0_passes_at_exactly_twice_noise0():
    # noise0 = 0.01 (the floor), bar 0.02: 3.0 - 2.98 rounds to 0.020000000000000018.
    assert a0_verdict(run("s1", 3.0), run("s2", 3.0), run("x", 2.98)).outcome == "pass"
    assert a0_verdict(run("s1", 3.0), run("s2", 3.0), run("x", 2.98 - 1e-6)).outcome == "behind"


def test_noise0_is_the_seed_gap_when_it_beats_the_floor():
    verdict = a0_verdict(run("s1", 3.00), run("s2", 3.05), run("x", 2.91))
    assert verdict.noise0 == pytest.approx(0.05) and verdict.outcome == "pass"


def test_stable_but_behind_is_not_a_failure():
    verdict = a0_verdict(run("s1", 3.1), run("s2", 3.1), run("x", 3.0))
    assert verdict.outcome == "behind" and verdict.gap == pytest.approx(0.1)


def test_an_unstable_a0_run_settles_the_outcome_before_any_noise():
    verdict = a0_verdict(run("s1", 3.0), run("s2", math.nan), run("x", 3.0))
    assert verdict.outcome == "unstable" and verdict.noise0 is None
    assert list(verdict.unstable) == ["s2"]


def test_the_early_read_is_in_units_of_noise0():
    assert early_read(run(loss=3.0), run(loss=3.03), 0.01) == pytest.approx(3.0)


# ---- A1 ------------------------------------------------------------------------- #

def arms(cfc, conv):
    return ([run(f"cfc{i}", v) for i, v in enumerate(cfc)],
            [run(f"conv{i}", v) for i, v in enumerate(conv)])


def test_go_at_exactly_two_estimated_standard_errors():
    bar = 2 * 0.01 / math.sqrt(3)                       # floor-bound noise
    assert a1_verdict(*arms([3.0] * 3, [3.0 + bar] * 3)).outcome == "GO"
    assert a1_verdict(*arms([3.0] * 3, [3.0 + bar - 1e-6] * 3)).outcome == "NO-GO"


def test_the_noise_uses_the_n_minus_one_sample_sd():
    verdict = a1_verdict(*arms([3.00, 3.02, 3.04], [3.1] * 3))
    # stdev([3.00, 3.02, 3.04]) = 0.02 (n - 1); pstdev would give 0.0163.
    assert verdict.noise == pytest.approx(math.sqrt(2) * 0.02)
    assert verdict.gap == pytest.approx(0.08)
    assert verdict.bar == pytest.approx(2 * verdict.noise / math.sqrt(3))


def test_the_a1_floor_binds_when_the_seeds_agree():
    assert a1_verdict(*arms([3.0, 3.001, 3.002], [3.1] * 3)).noise == 0.01


def test_an_unstable_binding_run_is_no_verdict_not_a_no_go():
    cfc, conv = arms([3.0] * 3, [3.5] * 3)
    conv[2] = run("conv2", 3.5, skipped=500)
    verdict = a1_verdict(cfc, conv)
    assert verdict.outcome == "no verdict" and verdict.noise is None


def test_the_binding_verdict_needs_three_seeds():
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="three seeds"):
        a1_verdict(*arms([3.0] * 2, [3.1] * 2))


# ---- reading exports and writing m1.md --------------------------------------------- #

def export(root, name, loss, in_run=(3.5, 3.4)):
    """Write a run the way deluge/chain/export.py does."""
    out = root / name
    out.mkdir(parents=True)
    (out / "final_eval.json").write_text(json.dumps({
        "step": 1907, "full_loss": loss, "subset_loss": loss, "skipped_steps": 0,
        "buckets": {"0-512": loss + 0.2, "512-1536": loss, "1536-4096": loss - 0.1},
        "decay": {"min": [0.9], "median": [0.95], "max": [0.999]}, "state_rms": [1.0]}))
    write_csv(out / "evals.csv", [eval_row({"step": 190 * (i + 1), "subset_loss": v})
                                  for i, v in enumerate(in_run)])


def test_a_run_is_read_back_from_what_the_exporter_writes(tmp_path):
    export(tmp_path, "m1-a0-cfc-s1", 3.3)
    loaded = load_run(tmp_path / "m1-a0-cfc-s1")
    assert loaded.full_loss == 3.3 and loaded.best_in_run_subset == 3.4
    assert loaded.decay_max == [0.999] and loaded.steps == 1907
    assert loaded.buckets["1536-4096"] == pytest.approx(3.2)


def test_replace_block_writes_only_between_the_markers():
    text = "intro\n<!-- m1_verdict:a0 -->\nold\n<!-- /m1_verdict:a0 -->\noutro\n"
    assert replace_block(text, "a0", "new") == (
        "intro\n<!-- m1_verdict:a0 -->\nnew\n<!-- /m1_verdict:a0 -->\noutro\n")
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="a1"):
        replace_block(text, "a1", "new")


def test_the_results_page_has_every_marker():
    text = (REPO / "docs/results/m1.md").read_text()
    for key in ("a0", "early", "a1"):
        assert f"<!-- m1_verdict:{key} -->" in text and f"<!-- /m1_verdict:{key} -->" in text


def test_the_script_writes_the_a0_and_a1_tables(tmp_path):
    root = tmp_path / "runs"
    for name, loss in zip(A0_RUNS, (3.30, 3.31, 3.29)):
        export(root, name, loss)
    for name in EARLY_RUNS:
        export(root, name, 3.35)
    for name, loss in [*zip(A1_CFC, (3.00, 3.01, 3.02)), *zip(A1_CONV, (3.05, 3.06, 3.07))]:
        export(root, name, loss)
    page = tmp_path / "m1.md"
    page.write_text((REPO / "docs/results/m1.md").read_text())

    assert m1_verdict.main(["a0"], root=root, page=page) == 0
    assert "**A0: pass.**" in page.read_text()
    assert m1_verdict.main(["a1"], root=root, page=page) == 0
    text = page.read_text()
    assert "**A1: GO.**" in text and "`m1-a1s-lru` - CfC: +5.00 noise0" in text
    assert "## Caveats" in text                     # the prose is untouched


def test_the_dry_run_reads_any_name_and_writes_nothing(tmp_path, capsys):
    export(tmp_path, "rehearse-m1", 3.3)
    page = tmp_path / "m1.md"
    page.write_text("untouched")
    assert m1_verdict.main(["--dry-run", "rehearse-m1"], root=tmp_path, page=page) == 0
    assert "rehearse-m1" in capsys.readouterr().out and page.read_text() == "untouched"


def test_a_verdict_with_runs_missing_names_them(tmp_path):
    with pytest.raises(SystemExit, match="m1-a0-cfc-s1"):
        m1_verdict.main(["a0"], root=tmp_path, page=tmp_path / "m1.md")


def test_a_rerun_under_a_new_name_stands_in_for_its_role(tmp_path):
    # Design 8.2-8.3 rerun a broken run under a new name; the verdict must be
    # able to read it, and the table must say which run it read.
    root = tmp_path / "runs"
    for name, loss in zip(A0_RUNS, (3.30, 3.31, 3.29)):
        export(root, name, loss)
    export(root, "m1-a0-xfmr-2", 3.20)
    page = tmp_path / "m1.md"
    page.write_text((REPO / "docs/results/m1.md").read_text())
    assert m1_verdict.main(["a0", "--run", "m1-a0-xfmr=m1-a0-xfmr-2"],
                           root=root, page=page) == 0
    text = page.read_text()
    assert "| `m1-a0-xfmr-2` |" in text and "CfC s1 - transformer = +0.1000" in text
    export(root, "rehearse-m1", 3.0)
    for bad in ("m1-a0-xfrm=m1-a0-xfmr-2", "m1-a0-xfmr=rehearse-m1"):
        with pytest.raises(SystemExit):
            m1_verdict.main(["a0", "--run", bad], root=root, page=page)


def test_the_binding_verdict_does_not_wait_on_the_early_read(tmp_path):
    # The early runs bind nothing. One skipped after crashing never exports,
    # and it must not hold up the verdict the project turns on.
    root = tmp_path / "runs"
    for name, loss in [*zip(A0_RUNS, (3.30, 3.31, 3.29)), (EARLY_RUNS[0], 3.35),
                       *zip(A1_CFC, (3.00, 3.01, 3.02)), *zip(A1_CONV, (3.05, 3.06, 3.07))]:
        export(root, name, loss)
    page = tmp_path / "m1.md"
    page.write_text((REPO / "docs/results/m1.md").read_text())
    assert m1_verdict.main(["a1"], root=root, page=page) == 0
    text = page.read_text()
    assert "**A1: GO.**" in text and f"`{EARLY_RUNS[0]}` - CfC" in text
    assert f"`{EARLY_RUNS[1]}` - CfC" not in text
```

"""Evaluation and verdicts over finished runs. Importable without torch."""

**Create `deluge/eval/m1.py`:**

```python
"""The M1 verdicts: A0's gate and A1's go/no-go (M1 design 7.3 and 8.3).

Pre-registered: these rules were fixed before any result existed, and this
module is them and nothing more. Stability is judged first, with fixed
thresholds only; seed noise is then computed from stable runs only, so a
broken run can neither inflate the bar nor pass its own check.

Reads what deluge/chain/export.py committed to docs/results/runs/<name>/:
final_eval.json and evals.csv. scripts/m1_verdict.py is the command line.

Comparisons carry EPS, so a value exactly on a boundary lands on the side the
rule names (<= for A0, >= for A1) whatever the float rounding.
"""

import csv
import json
import math
import re
import statistics
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Sequence

EPS = 1e-12
NOISE_FLOOR = 0.01            # nats; stops a lucky close pair setting the bar
LATE_DIVERGENCE = 0.02        # nats; final subset <= best in-run subset + this
AMNESIA = 0.5                 # a layer with every head below: amnesia
FROZEN = 0.9999               # every head above: ~10k-token memory, b ~ 0.014
MAX_SKIPPED = 0.01            # of steps, skipped by the fp16 scaler

A0_RUNS = ("m1-a0-cfc-s1", "m1-a0-cfc-s2", "m1-a0-xfmr")
EARLY_RUNS = ("m1-a1s-conv", "m1-a1s-lru")
A1_CFC = ("m1-a1d-cfc-s1", "m1-a1d-cfc-s2", "m1-a1d-cfc-s3")
A1_CONV = ("m1-a1d-conv-s1", "m1-a1d-conv-s2", "m1-a1d-conv-s3")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Run:
    name: str
    full_loss: float
    subset_loss: float
    best_in_run_subset: Optional[float]      # None: no in-run evals
    decay_min: List[float]                   # per recurrent layer, over heads
    decay_max: List[float]
    steps: int
    skipped_steps: int
    buckets: Dict[str, Optional[float]] = field(default_factory=dict)


def load_run(directory: Path) -> Run:
    final = json.loads((directory / "final_eval.json").read_text())
    in_run: List[float] = []
    evals = directory / "evals.csv"
    if evals.exists():
        with open(evals, newline="") as handle:
            in_run = [float(row["subset_loss"]) for row in csv.DictReader(handle)]
    decay = final.get("decay") or {}
    return Run(name=directory.name, full_loss=float(final["full_loss"]),
               subset_loss=float(final["subset_loss"]),
               best_in_run_subset=min(in_run) if in_run else None,
               decay_min=list(decay.get("min", [])), decay_max=list(decay.get("max", [])),
               steps=int(final["step"]), skipped_steps=int(final["skipped_steps"]),
               buckets=dict(final.get("buckets") or {}))


def stability(run: Run) -> List[str]:
    """The criteria of design 7.3 a run fails; empty means stable."""
    failures = []
    if not math.isfinite(run.full_loss):
        failures.append(f"final loss is {run.full_loss}")
    if run.best_in_run_subset is not None and not (
            run.subset_loss <= run.best_in_run_subset + LATE_DIVERGENCE + EPS):
        failures.append(f"late divergence: final subset loss {run.subset_loss:.4f} > "
                        f"best in-run {run.best_in_run_subset:.4f} + {LATE_DIVERGENCE}")
    for layer, (low, high) in enumerate(zip(run.decay_min, run.decay_max)):
        if high < AMNESIA:
            failures.append(f"recurrent layer {layer}: every head below a = {AMNESIA}")
        if low > FROZEN:
            failures.append(f"recurrent layer {layer}: every head above a = {FROZEN}")
    if run.skipped_steps > MAX_SKIPPED * run.steps + EPS:
        failures.append(f"{run.skipped_steps} of {run.steps} steps skipped by the "
                        f"loss scaler, over {MAX_SKIPPED:.0%}")
    return failures


def _unstable(runs: Sequence[Run]) -> Dict[str, List[str]]:
    return {run.name: failures for run in runs if (failures := stability(run))}


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class A0Verdict:
    outcome: str                       # "pass" | "behind" | "unstable"
    noise0: Optional[float] = None
    gap: Optional[float] = None        # CfC s1 - transformer; > 0 means CfC is behind
    unstable: Dict[str, List[str]] = field(default_factory=dict)

    @property
    def bar(self) -> Optional[float]:
        return None if self.noise0 is None else 2 * self.noise0


def a0_verdict(cfc_s1: Run, cfc_s2: Run, transformer: Run) -> A0Verdict:
    """Pass iff all three are stable and CfC s1 - transformer <= 2 noise0.

    "behind" -- stable, but past the bar -- is not a fail: it is diagnosed,
    then either fixed and rerun or carried into A1 as a caveat (design 8.3).
    """
    unstable = _unstable((cfc_s1, cfc_s2, transformer))
    if unstable:
        return A0Verdict("unstable", unstable=unstable)
    noise0 = max(abs(cfc_s1.full_loss - cfc_s2.full_loss), NOISE_FLOOR)
    gap = cfc_s1.full_loss - transformer.full_loss
    return A0Verdict("pass" if gap <= 2 * noise0 + EPS else "behind", noise0, gap)


def early_read(cfc_s1: Run, other: Run, noise0: float) -> float:
    """other - CfC at seed 1234, in units of noise0. Reported; never acted on."""
    return (other.full_loss - cfc_s1.full_loss) / noise0


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class A1Verdict:
    outcome: str                       # "GO" | "NO-GO" | "no verdict"
    noise: Optional[float] = None
    gap: Optional[float] = None        # mean(conv) - mean(CfC); > 0 means CfC is better
    bar: Optional[float] = None
    unstable: Dict[str, List[str]] = field(default_factory=dict)


def a1_verdict(cfc: Sequence[Run], conv: Sequence[Run]) -> A1Verdict:
    """GO iff all six are stable and mean(conv) - mean(CfC) >= 2 noise / sqrt(3),
    noise = max(sqrt(2) s, NOISE_FLOOR) with s the n - 1 sample SD of the CfC
    losses. An unstable run means no verdict -- not a no-go."""
    if len(cfc) != 3 or len(conv) != 3:
        raise ValueError("the binding verdict needs three seeds per arm")
    unstable = _unstable((*cfc, *conv))
    if unstable:
        return A1Verdict("no verdict", unstable=unstable)
    s = statistics.stdev(run.full_loss for run in cfc)
    noise = max(math.sqrt(2) * s, NOISE_FLOOR)
    gap = (statistics.fmean(run.full_loss for run in conv)
           - statistics.fmean(run.full_loss for run in cfc))
    bar = 2 * noise / math.sqrt(3)
    return A1Verdict("GO" if gap >= bar - EPS else "NO-GO", noise, gap, bar)


# ---- docs/results/m1.md --------------------------------------------------------- #

def runs_table(runs: Sequence[Run]) -> str:
    buckets = sorted({b for run in runs for b in run.buckets},
                     key=lambda b: int(b.split("-")[0]))
    head = ["run", "full loss", "subset", "best in-run", *buckets, "skipped", "stable"]
    rows = ["| " + " | ".join(head) + " |", "|" + "---|" * len(head)]
    for run in runs:
        failures = stability(run)
        cells = [f"`{run.name}`", f"{run.full_loss:.4f}", f"{run.subset_loss:.4f}",
                 "-" if run.best_in_run_subset is None else f"{run.best_in_run_subset:.4f}",
                 *("-" if run.buckets.get(b) is None else f"{run.buckets[b]:.4f}"
                   for b in buckets),
                 f"{run.skipped_steps}/{run.steps}",
                 "yes" if not failures else "no: " + "; ".join(failures)]
        rows.append("| " + " | ".join(cells) + " |")
    return "\n".join(rows)


def render_a0(runs: Sequence[Run], verdict: A0Verdict) -> str:
    lines = [runs_table(runs), ""]
    if verdict.outcome == "unstable":
        lines.append("**A0: unstable.** Debug, fix, and rerun under new names.")
    else:
        lines += [f"noise0 = {verdict.noise0:.4f} nats; CfC s1 - transformer = "
                  f"{verdict.gap:+.4f}; bar 2 x noise0 = {verdict.bar:.4f}.", "",
                  "**A0: pass.**" if verdict.outcome == "pass" else
                  "**A0: stable but behind.** Diagnose before rerunning anything "
                  "(design 8.3)."]
    return "\n".join(lines)


def render_early(cfc_s1: Run, others: Sequence[Run], noise0: float) -> str:
    lines = [runs_table([cfc_s1, *others]), ""]
    lines += [f"- `{run.name}` - CfC: {early_read(cfc_s1, run, noise0):+.2f} noise0"
              for run in others]
    lines += ["", "Not binding: an early read triggers nothing."]
    return "\n".join(lines)


def render_a1(runs: Sequence[Run], verdict: A1Verdict) -> str:
    lines = [runs_table(runs), ""]
    if verdict.outcome == "no verdict":
        lines.append("**No verdict:** a binding run is unstable. Debug, fix, and "
                     "rerun under new names; instability is not a no-go.")
    else:
        lines += [f"noise = {verdict.noise:.4f} nats; gap = mean(conv) - mean(CfC) = "
                  f"{verdict.gap:+.4f}; bar 2 x noise / sqrt(3) = {verdict.bar:.4f}.", "",
                  f"**A1: {verdict.outcome}.**"]
    return "\n".join(lines)


def replace_block(text: str, key: str, body: str) -> str:
    """Put body between <!-- m1_verdict:key --> and <!-- /m1_verdict:key -->,
    leaving everything outside the markers alone."""
    pattern = re.compile(rf"(<!-- m1_verdict:{key} -->\n).*?(\n<!-- /m1_verdict:{key} -->)",
                         re.DOTALL)
    if not pattern.search(text):
        raise ValueError(f"no <!-- m1_verdict:{key} --> block to write into")
    return pattern.sub(lambda m: m.group(1) + body + m.group(2), text, count=1)
```

**Create `scripts/m1_verdict.py`:**

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python
"""Write the M1 verdict tables into docs/results/m1.md (M1 design 8.4).

    python scripts/m1_verdict.py a0                   # after batch 1
    python scripts/m1_verdict.py a1                   # after batch 2
    python scripts/m1_verdict.py --dry-run rehearse-m1
    python scripts/m1_verdict.py a0 --run m1-a0-xfmr=m1-a0-xfmr-2

Reads only docs/results/runs/<name>/, which the export workflow commits --
never Kaggle. Writes only between the marker comments in m1.md; the prose
around them is written by hand. --dry-run parses one run, under any name, and
prints what it read without judging or writing anything (batch 0).

--run ROLE=RUN reads a rerun in place of the run a role names. The design
reruns a broken run under a new name (8.2, 8.3), and the tables name every
run they read, so a substitution is on the record. The rerun must be an m1-*
run: the rehearsal never stands in for one.

The early read is not binding, so an early run that never exported is left
out of its table rather than holding up the binding verdict.
"""

import argparse
import sys
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]))

from deluge.eval.m1 import (A0_RUNS, A1_CFC, A1_CONV, EARLY_RUNS, a0_verdict,  # noqa: E402
                            a1_verdict, load_run, render_a0, render_a1,
                            render_early, replace_block, stability)

REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
RUNS = REPO / "docs/results/runs"
PAGE = REPO / "docs/results/m1.md"


def load(root: Path, names):
    missing = [name for name in names if not (root / name / "final_eval.json").exists()]
    if missing:
        raise SystemExit(f"not exported yet: {', '.join(missing)} (under {root})")
    return [load_run(root / name) for name in names]


def main(argv=None, root: Path = RUNS, page: Path = PAGE) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.split("\n")[0])
    parser.add_argument("verdict", nargs="?", choices=("a0", "a1"))
    parser.add_argument("--dry-run", metavar="RUN")
    parser.add_argument("--run", action="append", default=[], metavar="ROLE=RUN",
                        help="read RUN in place of ROLE's run, e.g. "
                             "m1-a0-xfmr=m1-a0-xfmr-2; repeatable")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    if args.dry_run:
        [run] = load(root, [args.dry_run])
        print(run)
        print("stability:", stability(run) or "stable")
        return 0
    if args.verdict is None:
        parser.error("name a verdict (a0, a1) or pass --dry-run RUN")

    roles = (*A0_RUNS, *EARLY_RUNS, *A1_CFC, *A1_CONV)
    reruns = dict(item.partition("=")[::2] for item in args.run)
    for role, name in reruns.items():
        if role not in roles:
            parser.error(f"--run {role}={name}: {role} is not one of {', '.join(roles)}")
        if not name.startswith("m1-"):
            parser.error(f"--run {role}={name}: a rerun must be an m1-* run")

    def named(names):
        return [reruns.get(name, name) for name in names]

    cfc_s1, cfc_s2, xfmr = load(root, named(A0_RUNS))
    a0 = a0_verdict(cfc_s1, cfc_s2, xfmr)
    blocks = {}
    if args.verdict == "a0":
        blocks["a0"] = render_a0([cfc_s1, cfc_s2, xfmr], a0)
        print(f"A0: {a0.outcome}")
    else:
        early = [name for name in named(EARLY_RUNS)
                 if (root / name / "final_eval.json").exists()]
        for name in sorted(set(named(EARLY_RUNS)) - set(early)):
            print(f"early read: {name} is not exported; left out")
        if a0.noise0 is not None and early:     # in units of A0's noise
            blocks["early"] = render_early(cfc_s1, load(root, early), a0.noise0)
        cfc, conv = load(root, named(A1_CFC)), load(root, named(A1_CONV))
        a1 = a1_verdict(cfc, conv)
        blocks["a1"] = render_a1([*cfc, *conv], a1)
        print(f"A1: {a1.outcome}")
    text = page.read_text()
    for key, body in blocks.items():
        text = replace_block(text, key, body)
    page.write_text(text)
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

**Create `docs/results/m1.md`:**

```markdown
# M1: A0 and the A1 go/no-go

The design and the pre-registered rules are in
`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-m1-design.md` (§7.3 stability, §8.3
verdicts). `scripts/m1_verdict.py` writes the tables between the marker
comments, from what the export workflow committed to `docs/results/runs/`.
The prose around them is written by hand once each verdict is in.

## A0: does CfC train stably to a matched transformer's loss?

Batch 1, screening budget (500M tokens): `m1-a0-cfc-s1`, `m1-a0-cfc-s2`,
`m1-a0-xfmr`.

<!-- m1_verdict:a0 -->
Not run yet.
<!-- /m1_verdict:a0 -->

## Caveats

Known before any result:

- **Parameter matching.** Every arm is matched to A0's 110.02M parameters to
  within 0.07%: conv 110.09M, LRU 109.95M, transformer 110.07M. Both the conv
  and the transformer come out slightly larger, so a mismatch works against
  CfC.
- **The conv's width differs.** The conv arm runs at `d_inner` 872 against
  CfC's 1152, because that is where its parameters match.
- **`b_g`.** CfC and the LRU carry a gate bias, initialised at 1.278, that
  spec §3 does not list. Without it the gate cannot start near 1 (design D7).
- **The λ grid.** The init reads spec §3's "log-uniform" as a geometric grid
  of λ over heads (memories of 999 to 9 tokens), not a linear one (design D6).
- **The LRU arm measures the conv only.** Δ is always 1 at A1, so CfC against
  the LRU is what the short conv is worth. Δ is A3's question.
- **The transformer is the hybrid's attention, not a tuned transformer's.**
  GQA 12/4, four learned sinks, RoPE θ = 1M, and the same init and schedule
  as the hybrid.
- **A0's gap, if it was carried forward.** If A0 was stable but behind and
  its diagnosis found no bug, the gap and the diagnosis are recorded here
  (design 8.3).
```

```bash

git commit -F - <<'EOF'
eval: the pre-registered M1 verdicts and the results page

Stability first, on fixed thresholds; noise from stable runs only. A0
passes within 2 x noise0, and stable-but-behind is its own outcome. A1
is GO iff the three-seed mean gap is at least 2 noise / sqrt(3). The
script writes tables between markers in docs/results/m1.md from the
exported runs, and --dry-run parses batch 0. --run reads a rerun in its
role's place, and a missing early run never blocks the binding verdict.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

EOF

@@ -77,14 +77,14 @@ v_t   = SiLU( DWConv_k4( W_v x_t ) )                 candidate, ℝ^{d_inner}
 a_t   = exp( −Δ_t · λ_t )                            decay ∈ (0,1)

 ```
 
 Correspondence to CfC: with h-independent heads, CfC's σ(−f·t) interpolation reduces to the exponential-decay form above; f ↔ λ, the "time" argument ↔ Δ_t.
 
 **Δ_t semantics.** Δ_t = 1 for consecutive processed tokens. When MoD skips this block for one or more tokens, Δ_t for the next processed token = 1 + number of skipped tokens (§6). For irregularly sampled inputs (audio/event streams, future work) Δ_t = real time gap.
 
-**Init.** λ initialised so that a_t at Δ=1 is log-uniform in [0.9, 0.999] across heads (λ ∈ [0.001, 0.105]); set b_f accordingly. W_g init so the gate starts near 1. DWConv init identity on the centre tap. This init is the single most important stability lever.
+**Init.** λ initialised so that a_t at Δ=1 is log-uniform in [0.9, 0.999] across heads (λ ∈ [0.001, 0.105]): a geometric grid of λ from −ln 0.999 to −ln 0.9, so dev's 12 heads remember 999, 655, 429, … 9 tokens (M1 design D6; a linear grid would give one long head). b_f = softplus⁻¹(λ) and W_f = 0, so the decays are exact at step 0. The gate starts near 1 through its bias: W_g = 0 and b_g = 1.278, so SiLU(b_g) = 0.9995 (D7; on zero-mean pre-normed input SiLU(W_g x) averages ~0.07 whatever W_g is, so only a bias can do it). DWConv init identity on the centre tap, read causally: the current token's. This init is the single most important stability lever.
 
 **Initial state h₀.** A learned parameter per layer (ℝ^{d_inner}, zero-init, weight-decay-free), not a zero constant. See §15 — this is the recurrent half of the baked-in system prompt. The conv buffer's initial contents are likewise learned (3 × d_inner per layer).

+A1 runs at 4k tokens on long documents: at 1k the stacked attention windows already reach the whole sequence, and A1 would test only local mixing (M1 design D5). Δ is always 1 at A1, so the LRU arm (B4) measures the short conv only; whether Δ does work is A3's question.
+
+Go/no-go after A1: if CfC does not beat the conv by the margin pre-registered in the M1 design (§8.3: three seeds per arm at the dev budget; GO iff the mean gap is at least 2·noise/√3), freeze text work and re-target the architecture to audio/event tokens.

-## Data
+With a `heldout.bin` beside the training data, the run evaluates on it every

+## Data
 
-```bash
-python scripts/prepare_data.py \
-    --dataset HuggingFaceFW/fineweb-edu --name sample-10BT \
-    --tokenizer mistralai/Mistral-7B-v0.1 \
-    --tokens 600_000_000 --out data/tokens.bin
-```
+Every M1 arm trains at `seq_len` 4096 on long documents, and every arm
+attaches the same private dataset, `heylaine/deluge-long4k`:
 
-600M tokens (1.2 GB) covers a screening arm with margin. A dev arm needs 2.26B,
-which is 4.5 GB — under Kaggle's dataset limit, and worth preparing once since
-every arm must see identical data for the ladder to mean anything.
+| file | what |
+|---|---|
+| `train.bin` | ~2.4B tokens: documents of at least 4,097 Mistral tokens, cut into whole 4,097-token pieces (the dev budget's 2.26B plus margin) |
+| `heldout.bin` | ~24M tokens: the 1% of documents whose text hashes there, in hash order |
+| `provenance.json` | source and revision, tokenizer, `piece_len`, pre-filter, split rule, counts |
+
+A piece is 4,097 tokens because a sequence reads 4,096 inputs plus one shifted
+target, and a piece never spans two documents. `deluge.train` reads the data in
+pieces whenever `provenance.json` names a `piece_len`, and refuses to start if
+it is not `seq_len + 1`. It picks up `heldout.bin` and `provenance.json` from
+beside `--data`, so the chain's `data:` field stays
+`heylaine/deluge-long4k:train.bin`.
+
+How the dataset is made:
+
+1. **Measure** the yield before fixing a size. This reads only FineWeb-edu's
+   `token_count` column (GPT-2 tokens), plus a few thousand documents to
+   calibrate the GPT-2 → Mistral ratio and suggest a pre-filter:
+
+   ```bash
+   pip install huggingface_hub pyarrow tokenizers
+   python scripts/prepare_data.py measure \
+       --dataset HuggingFaceFW/fineweb-edu --files 'sample/10BT/*.parquet' \
+       --tokenizer mistralai/Mistral-7B-v0.1
+   ```
+
+   If `yield.tokens` is under ~2.43B (2.4B to train.bin, plus the 1% held
+   out), use `--files 'sample/100BT/*.parquet'`: the long mode stops once
+   train.bin is full, so the larger sample costs only download time.
+2. **Build** it in a Kaggle CPU kernel with internet, so the download and
+   tokenizing never run on the home PC. `scripts/kaggle_prep.py` renders the
+   kernel, which clones this repo at a pinned commit (it must be on GitHub)
+   and runs `prepare_data.py long` into `/kaggle/working`:
+
+   ```bash
+   python scripts/kaggle_prep.py --owner heylaine --commit <sha> --dir /tmp/prep-kernel -- \
+       --dataset HuggingFaceFW/fineweb-edu --files 'sample/10BT/*.parquet' \
+       --revision <sha that measure printed> --tokenizer mistralai/Mistral-7B-v0.1 \
+       --prefilter <from measure> --train-tokens 2_400_000_000
+   KAGGLE_CONFIG_DIR=~/Downloads kaggle kernels push -p /tmp/prep-kernel
+   ```
+3. **Publish** the kernel's output as the private dataset `deluge-long4k`: on
+   Kaggle, open the kernel's output and create a new dataset from it. If that
+   route is not available, download the output and upload it with
+   `kaggle datasets create`.
+
+The pre-filter, the source revision and the split rule are in
+`provenance.json`, which the trainer copies into every run directory and the
+exporter puts in each run's Hugging Face `run.json`.
 
 ## Budgeting a session
 
@@ -80,15 +125,32 @@ every arm must see identical data for the ladder to mean anything.
 | dev arm (`train/dev.yaml`) | 2.26B | ~27 h | 3 |
 
 These assume ~16 TFLOP/s delivered across the pair, which is a guess until the
-first run measures it. `log.jsonl` records `tokens_per_second` every step —
-take the number from there and re-derive before planning a ladder around it.
+first run measures it, and were made at `seq_len` 1024. At 4096 the hybrid
+costs roughly 17% more and the all-attention transformer roughly 70% more.
+`log.jsonl` records `tokens_per_second` every step: re-derive this table from
+the first A0 session's number before planning around it.
+
+## Memory
+
+`micro_batch` in `configs/train/base.yaml` is split across the two T4s by
+`--data-parallel`, so it is twice the per-GPU batch. It is the largest power of
+two (so it divides a step's 64 sequences) whose peak *reserved* memory per GPU
+stays at or under ~13 GiB, on the largest M1 arm. A T4 has ~14.7 GiB usable;
+13 leaves margin, and on the 5070 Ti, where it is measured, the display uses
+some too. `log.jsonl` records `peak_alloc_gib` and `peak_reserved_gib` every
+step, the max over the GPUs in use.
+
+If even one sequence per GPU does not fit, the first lever is the loss: the
+fp32 cross-entropy over the 32k vocab is ~1–1.5 GB per 4,096-token sequence,
+and computing it in sequence chunks recovers most of that. Per-block activation
+checkpointing comes after.
 
 ## Checkpoint size
 
 The checkpoint holds fp32 weights, both Adam moments and the scaler. For the
-113M dev config that is roughly 1.8 GB, so `keep_last: 2` is ~3.6 GB of the
-20 GB working directory. Drop to `keep_last: 1` if that gets tight — rotation
-never deletes the only checkpoint.
+110M M1 arms that is ~1.3 GB, so `keep_last: 2` is ~2.6 GB of the 20 GB
+working directory. Drop to `keep_last: 1` if that gets tight — rotation never
+deletes the only checkpoint.
 
 ## If a session dies badly
 
@@ -104,7 +166,7 @@ session at a time, with no machine of yours involved. The design is in
 `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-kaggle-chain-design.md`.
 
 **Adding a run.** Append an entry to `configs/chain/runs.yaml` and push to
-`master`. The next idle tick (within 30 min) pins the run to that commit
+`master`. M1's runs set `export: true` (see "Results and checkpoints"). The next idle tick (within 30 min) pins the run to that commit
 and pushes its first session. Code pushed later affects only later runs.
 Names are identities: a name already in `done` never runs again, so rename
 to rerun.
@@ -140,3 +202,37 @@ doesn't email.
 **Sixty days.** GitHub disables scheduled workflows in a public repo after 60
 days without activity. The state commits should count as activity. If the
 schedule is disabled anyway, re-enable it under Actions → chain.
+
+## Results and checkpoints
+
+`.github/workflows/export.yml` runs hourly, apart from the chain's tick. For
+each done run with `export: true` it takes two independent steps, each skipped
+once its marker is in the repo:
+
+1. **Results.** It fetches `final_eval.json` and `log.jsonl` from the kernel
+   that ran the run's last session, and commits `final_eval.json`,
+   `evals.csv` and a downsampled `train.csv` to `docs/results/runs/<name>/`.
+   `scripts/m1_verdict.py` reads only these.
+2. **Checkpoint.** It uploads the newest `step-*.pt`, the full `log.jsonl` and
+   a `run.json` (the queue entry, the done record, the data provenance) to
+   `<name>/` in the private Hugging Face repo named in
+   `configs/chain/export.yaml`, then commits `docs/results/runs/<name>/export.json`
+   (repo, path, sha256, size).
+
+Setup, once, before the first exported run:
+- Create a Hugging Face token that can write to and create repos in your
+  namespace, and add it as the repo secret `HF_TOKEN`
+  (`gh secret set HF_TOKEN`).
+- Set `hf_repo: <hf-user>/deluge-runs` in `configs/chain/export.yaml`. The
+  repo is created private if it does not exist.
+
+A step that fails writes nothing, and the next hourly run retries it. The
+workflow fails, and so emails you, only for a run done more than 3 h without
+that step succeeding; then it fails every hour until fixed. A Hugging Face
+failure never holds up the results.
+
+Each checkpoint is ~1.3 GB; M1's twelve exported runs come to ~16 GB of the
+free account's 100 GB of private storage. If the export cannot be fixed in
+time, the checkpoint can still be fetched by hand while Kaggle keeps the
+kernel's output:
+`kaggle kernels output heylaine/deluge-<run>-<side> -p out/`.
````

+   `kernels/head_scan.py`; there is no registry until a second implementation exists.)
 4. `infer/engine.py` in plain PyTorch + `test_train_infer_equivalence` — before any speed work.
-5. Triton scan + parity test. Only now start caring about tok/s.
+5. Triton scan + parity test, and the registry (M3). Only now start caring about tok/s.
```

### Task 16: Measure the yield, build the dataset on Kaggle, publish it

Design §6.1, §12.2 and §13 ("data yield is measured first"). Each step marked **(user)** waits for the user's yes or the user's hands.

Set up once per shell, for this task and Task 17:
```bash
S=<your scratchpad directory, or the output of mktemp -d>
python -m venv "$S/venv" && "$S/venv/bin/pip" install -q "kaggle==2.2.4"
K="$S/venv/bin/kaggle"
export KAGGLE_CONFIG_DIR=~/Downloads
```

**Files:**
- Modify: `.gitignore`
- Modify (only if something differs from what Task 15 wrote): `docs/kaggle.md` ("Data")

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `scripts/prepare_data.py measure|long` and `scripts/kaggle_prep.py` (Task 11).
- Produces: the private Kaggle dataset `heylaine/deluge-long4k`, holding `train.bin` (≥ 2.4B tokens), `heldout.bin` (~24M tokens) and `provenance.json` (`piece_len` 4097). Every M1 run and Task 17 read it.

- [ ] **Step 1: Keep the local artefacts out of git**

From here on, data, runs and a CUDA venv land in the checkout. Step 9's fallback downloads ~4.9 GB into `data/long4k`, and Task 17 adds `.venv-cuda`, `data/smoke` and `runs/`. Append to `.gitignore`, anchored at the repo root:
```
/.venv-*/
/data/
/runs/
```
The leading slashes matter. An unanchored `runs/` also matches `docs/results/runs/`, so every export commit would come out empty. An unanchored `data/` would hide new files in `deluge/data/`.

Run: `$PY -m pytest tests/test_chain_workflow.py -q`
Expected: `15 passed`. `test_nothing_the_repo_commits_is_gitignored` checks the anchoring.

```bash

git commit -F - <<'EOF'
data: keep local datasets, runs and the CUDA venv out of git

Anchored at the repo root: an unanchored runs/ would also hide
docs/results/runs/, where the exporter commits.

Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_014aUsWk2mR3EEzk211iLJE2
EOF
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Install the prep dependencies into `.venv`**

Run: `$PY -m pip install -q huggingface_hub pyarrow tokenizers`
Expected: no error. `$PY -c "import huggingface_hub, pyarrow, tokenizers"` prints nothing.

- [ ] **Step 3: Measure the 10BT sample**

```bash
$PY scripts/prepare_data.py measure --dataset HuggingFaceFW/fineweb-edu \
    --files 'sample/10BT/*.parquet' --tokenizer mistralai/Mistral-7B-v0.1 | tee "$S/measure-10BT.json"
```

Expected: JSON with a `revision` (a 40-hex sha) and `shards` = 14 (the 10BT sample is 14 parquet files, 28.5 GB). It should show `documents` at about 9.7M, `gpt2_tokens` at about 10B, and `calibration.ratio` at about 1.1–1.25. `calibration.prefilter` is a multiple of 100 below 4097, and `yield.tokens` is the estimate. This reads tens of MB plus a few thousand documents, and takes minutes.

If it prints `no files match`, list the layout with `$PY -c "from huggingface_hub import HfFileSystem as F; print(F().ls('datasets/HuggingFaceFW/fineweb-edu/sample', detail=False))"` and fix `--files`. If that changes the glob, fix it in `docs/kaggle.md` too.

- [ ] **Step 4: Choose the sample**

Train needs ≥ 2.4B tokens, and the 1% held out comes on top, so the bar is `yield.tokens` ≥ 2.43e9.
- At or above the bar, use `--files 'sample/10BT/*.parquet'`.
- Below it, use `--files 'sample/100BT/*.parquet'`. Use the same revision, ratio and pre-filter: the samples share a distribution, and `long` stops once train.bin is full, so the larger sample costs only download time.

Tell the user the numbers and the choice.

- [ ] **Step 5 (user): Push `m1` to GitHub**

The prep kernel clones a pinned commit from `https://github.com/heylain/Deluge`, so that commit has to be there. Only the chain reads `master`, so pushing `m1` changes nothing that runs. Ask the user, and on a yes run `git push origin m1`.

- [ ] **Step 6 (user): Check the route with a 20M-token run**

The fill-ins in this step and Step 8 come from earlier steps. `<files>` is the glob Step 4 chose. `<revision>` and `<prefilter>` are `revision` and `calibration.prefilter` from Step 3's JSON.

Render the kernel:
```bash
$PY scripts/kaggle_prep.py --owner heylaine --commit "$(git rev-parse HEAD)" --dir "$S/prep-kernel" -- \
    --dataset HuggingFaceFW/fineweb-edu --files '<files>' --revision <revision> \
    --tokenizer mistralai/Mistral-7B-v0.1 --prefilter <prefilter> --train-tokens 20_000_000
```
Ask the user before pushing: it creates the private kernel `heylaine/deluge-prep-long4k`. On a yes, run `$K kernels push -p "$S/prep-kernel"`. Then poll `$K kernels status heylaine/deluge-prep-long4k` about every 5 minutes until it reports `complete`. Use the Monitor tool with an until-loop, not a sleep. If it reports `error`, fetch the log (`$K kernels output heylaine/deluge-prep-long4k -p "$S/prep-out"`, then read `deluge-prep-long4k.log`), fix, and re-push.

Then fetch the provenance and check it:
```bash
$K kernels output heylaine/deluge-prep-long4k -p "$S/prep-out" --file-pattern '(^|/)provenance\.json$'
cat "$S"/prep-out/provenance.json
```
Expected:
- `piece_len` 4097;
- `tokens.train` ≥ 20,000,000;
- `tokens.heldout` > 0;
- `bytes.train.bin` = 2 × `tokens.train`;
- `documents.qualifying` ≤ `documents.prefiltered` ≤ `documents.read`.

Now the route. Ask the user to open the kernel on kaggle.com, go to its Output, and create a new private dataset from it, named something throwaway like `deluge-long4k-test`. Ask them to confirm that the dataset lists `train.bin`, `heldout.bin` and `provenance.json` at its top level, then delete it. If Kaggle offers no such route, Step 9 uses the fallback instead.

- [ ] **Step 7: Note the elapsed time**

The 20M run's log shows when each shard started. Scale its elapsed time to 2.4B tokens (×120). If the estimate is over ~10 h, tell the user before Step 8: Kaggle stops a session at 12 h. The options are to run two kernels over disjoint `--files` and concatenate, which needs a small change to `prepare_long`, or to build locally with the fallback.

- [ ] **Step 8 (user): The full run**

Re-render with `--train-tokens 2_400_000_000`, everything else unchanged. Ask the user, then push. This makes a new version of the same kernel. Poll every 30–60 minutes until it is complete. Then fetch and check `provenance.json` again:
- `tokens.train` ≥ 2,400,000,000;
- `tokens.heldout` between 0.8% and 1.2% of `tokens.train`;
- `piece_len` 4097;
- the byte counts twice the token counts.

If `tokens.train` is short of 2.4B, the sample ran out of long documents before train.bin filled (`yield.tokens` is an estimate). Re-render with `--files 'sample/100BT/*.parquet'`, ask the user, and push again. The dev runs need at least 551,744 pieces (2.2605B tokens) to finish inside one epoch.

- [ ] **Step 9 (user): Publish `heylaine/deluge-long4k`**

Ask the user to create the private dataset `deluge-long4k` from the kernel's output, by the route checked in Step 6.

Fallback, only if that route does not exist: download the output and upload it as a dataset. This is ~4.9 GB each way over the home connection, so say so first.
```bash
mkdir -p data/long4k
$K kernels output heylaine/deluge-prep-long4k -p data/long4k --file-pattern '^(train|heldout)\.bin$|^provenance\.json$'
printf '{"title": "deluge-long4k", "id": "heylaine/deluge-long4k", "licenses": [{"name": "other"}]}\n' > data/long4k/dataset-metadata.json
$K datasets create -p data/long4k
```
(`kaggle datasets create` makes a private dataset unless it is given `--public`.)

Confirm: `$K datasets files heylaine/deluge-long4k` lists `train.bin` (~4.9 GB), `heldout.bin` (~48 MB) and `provenance.json`.

- [ ] **Step 10: Record the build**

If the route, the glob or the commands differ from `docs/kaggle.md` "Data", fix that section. In either case, add one line at the end of the section with what was built, in this form:
`The M1 dataset: <files>, revision <first 12 of the sha>, <tokens.train> train / <tokens.heldout> held-out tokens, built <date>.`
Then commit:
```bash

git commit -F - <<'EOF'
docs: the M1 dataset as built

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_014aUsWk2mR3EEzk211iLJE2

### Task 17: Local smoke on the 5070 Ti; set `micro_batch`

Design §8.1 and §11 ("Local smoke"). Run all four arms at `seq_len` 4096, in fp16 as on the T4, on the held-out files. This settles `micro_batch` and logs peak memory before anything is queued. Tell the user before starting: it is roughly two hours of GPU time on the shared power bill, a guess until the first arm logs its tokens/s. That is about 20 minutes of probes, then per arm 80 steps (21M tokens) plus a full held-out eval (~24M tokens, forward only), with the transformer the slowest.

**Files:**
- Modify: `configs/train/base.yaml` (`micro_batch`), `docs/kaggle.md` ("Memory")

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: everything from Tasks 1–10; `heylaine/deluge-long4k` (Task 16); `$K` and `KAGGLE_CONFIG_DIR` as Task 16 sets them; the `.gitignore` entries from Task 16 Step 1.
- Produces: the measured `micro_batch` in `configs/train/base.yaml`, with per-arm peak memory and tokens/s in `docs/kaggle.md`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Make a CUDA venv**

```bash
python -m venv .venv-cuda
.venv-cuda/bin/pip install -q torch --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu130
.venv-cuda/bin/pip install -q -e '.[dev]'
.venv-cuda/bin/python -c "
import torch
print(torch.__version__, torch.cuda.get_device_name(0), torch.cuda.get_device_capability(0))
x = torch.randn(1024, 1024, device='cuda', dtype=torch.float16)
print((x @ x).float().abs().mean().item())"
```
Expected: `(12, 0)` and a finite number. The 5070 Ti is sm_120, and the wheel must be built for it. If you see `no kernel image is available`, install from the newest `cuXXX` index that pytorch.org lists for this torch version.

- [ ] **Step 2: Run the suite on the GPU**

Run: `.venv-cuda/bin/python -m pytest -q`
Expected: `508 passed`. The GPU-only scaler test runs here.

- [ ] **Step 3: Fetch the smoke data**

```bash
mkdir -p data/smoke
$K datasets download heylaine/deluge-long4k -f heldout.bin -p data/smoke --unzip
$K datasets download heylaine/deluge-long4k -f provenance.json -p data/smoke
ls -la data/smoke
```
Expected: `heldout.bin` (~48 MB; unzip it if it arrived as `heldout.bin.zip`) and `provenance.json` with `"piece_len": 4097`. With `--data data/smoke/heldout.bin`, the trainer trains on the held-out file and also evaluates on it, which is fine for a smoke.

- [ ] **Step 4: Probe the per-GPU micro-batch**

For each arm, try per-GPU sizes 1, 2, 4 and 8, each for 90 seconds. Read the peak reserved memory from the log, delete each probe's checkpoint, and stop an arm at its first OOM. `runs/` must exist first, because each probe's console output is redirected into it before the trainer creates anything:
```bash
mkdir -p runs
for arm in A0_scan_only A1_conv_baseline A1_lru_baseline A0_transformer_baseline; do
  for mb in 1 2 4 8; do
    out=runs/probe-$arm-$mb; rm -rf "$out"
    .venv-cuda/bin/python -m deluge.train --model configs/ablations/$arm.yaml \
        --train configs/train/rehearse.yaml --data data/smoke/heldout.bin --out "$out" \
        --device cuda --micro-batch $mb --deadline-minutes 1.5 > "$out.txt" 2>&1
    code=$?
    echo "$arm mb=$mb exit=$code $(grep -o '"peak_reserved_gib": [0-9.]*' "$out/log.jsonl" 2>/dev/null | tail -1) $(grep -o '"tokens_per_second": [0-9.]*' "$out/log.jsonl" 2>/dev/null | tail -1)"
    rm -rf "$out"
    grep -qi "out of memory" "$out.txt" && break
  done
done
```
Expected: exit 2 (the deadline) with a peak for each size that fits, and an OOM where one does not. An exit other than 2 with no OOM in `runs/probe-<arm>-<mb>.txt` is a crash: read that file before going on. For each arm, take the largest `mb` whose `peak_reserved_gib` is ≤ 13.0. The per-GPU size is the smallest of those four, because one `base.yaml` serves every arm. `micro_batch` is twice that.

If an arm fails before its first step with an SDPA error ("No available kernel" or an alignment message), apply contingency (a) in Step 6 and re-probe. If even `mb=1` runs out of memory on some arm, apply contingency (b) and re-probe.

- [ ] **Step 5: Run each arm to completion at that size**

```bash
MB=<per-GPU size from Step 4>
for arm in A0_scan_only A1_conv_baseline A1_lru_baseline A0_transformer_baseline; do
  .venv-cuda/bin/python -m deluge.train --model configs/ablations/$arm.yaml \
      --train configs/train/rehearse.yaml --data data/smoke/heldout.bin --out runs/smoke-$arm \
      --device cuda --micro-batch $MB --deadline-minutes 0; echo "$arm exit $?"
  rm -f runs/smoke-$arm/step-*.pt
done
```
Expected, for each arm:
- `exit 0`;
- `runs/smoke-<arm>/final_eval.json` exists, with a finite `full_loss` well below the initial ~10.5;
- `log.jsonl` has `eval` records at steps 40 and 80 and one `final_eval`;
- the console shows `[model] attention: SDPA pinned to the memory-efficient backend` once.

Note each arm's `tokens_per_second`, `peak_reserved_gib`, `skipped_steps` and final loss. Early fp16 steps can be skipped while the scale settles, so a few skips in 80 steps is normal. NaN losses, or skips that keep coming, are not: stop and report them to the user. That is an A0-level finding, not something to tune away here.

- [ ] **Step 6: Contingencies (only if Step 4 or 5 needed them)**

**(a) SDPA refuses the mask or the key length.** Pad the keys to a multiple of 8 with zero keys the mask hides. In `deluge/model/mixers/attention.py`, `Attention.forward`:
```diff
         mask = attention_mask(time, self.n_sink, self.window, x.device)
+        # The memory-efficient kernel wants the key length a multiple of 8:
+        # pad with zero keys that the mask hides.
+        pad = -k.shape[2] % 8
+        if pad:
+            k = F.pad(k, (0, 0, 0, pad))
+            v = F.pad(v, (0, 0, 0, pad))
+            mask = F.pad(mask, (0, pad), value=False)
         y = F.scaled_dot_product_attention(q, k, v, attn_mask=mask)
```
`tests/test_attention.py` must still pass unchanged (checked against the brute-force reference for windows 8, 16, 24 and 32). Commit it as `model: pad attention keys to a multiple of 8 for the memory-efficient kernel`, with the trailers.

**(b) One sequence per GPU does not fit.** Compute the loss in sequence chunks, recomputing each chunk's logits in backward. In `deluge/model/model.py`, add after the imports:
```python
from torch.utils.checkpoint import checkpoint

LOSS_CHUNK = 1024   # tokens per cross-entropy chunk: memory, not architecture


def token_losses(hidden, weight, targets, chunk=LOSS_CHUNK):
    """Per-token cross-entropy without holding [B, T, V] fp32 logits: each
    chunk's logits are recomputed in backward (docs/kaggle.md, "Memory")."""
    def piece(h, t):
        return F.cross_entropy(F.linear(h, weight).float().flatten(0, 1), t.flatten(),
                               reduction="none").view_as(t)
    return torch.cat([checkpoint(piece, hidden[:, i:i + chunk], targets[:, i:i + chunk],
                                 use_reentrant=False)
                      for i in range(0, hidden.shape[1], chunk)], dim=1)
```
and in `Model.forward` replace the logits and loss lines with:
```python
        losses = token_losses(self.norm(x), self.embed.weight, targets)
```
Add this test to `tests/test_model.py`:
```python
def test_chunked_losses_equal_the_whole_sequence_ones():
    from deluge.model.model import token_losses
    torch.manual_seed(0)
    hidden = torch.randn(2, 100, 16, requires_grad=True)
    weight = torch.randn(50, 16, requires_grad=True)
    targets = torch.randint(0, 50, (2, 100))
    whole = torch.nn.functional.cross_entropy(
        torch.nn.functional.linear(hidden, weight).flatten(0, 1), targets.flatten(),
        reduction="none").view_as(targets)
    chunked = token_losses(hidden, weight, targets, chunk=32)
    torch.testing.assert_close(chunked, whole)
    for a, b in zip(torch.autograd.grad(whole.mean(), (hidden, weight)),
                    torch.autograd.grad(chunked.mean(), (hidden, weight))):
        torch.testing.assert_close(a, b)
```
Run `$PY -m pytest tests/test_model.py -q`, which must be all passed. Commit it as `model: cross-entropy in sequence chunks, to fit 4096 tokens on a T4`, with the trailers.

- [ ] **Step 7: Set `micro_batch` and record the measurements**

In `configs/train/base.yaml`, set `micro_batch:` to twice the per-GPU size. Update its trailing comment to the new number of accumulation steps (64 / `micro_batch`). In `docs/kaggle.md` "Memory", add a table after the first paragraph:

```markdown

(M1 local smoke, <date>):

| arm | peak reserved GiB | tokens/s | final loss at 80 steps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `A0_scan_only` | … | … | … |
| `A1_conv_baseline` | … | … | … |
| `A1_lru_baseline` | … | … | … |
| `A0_transformer_baseline` | … | … | … |
```
Fill each `…` with the numbers Step 5 printed. These are measurements, not placeholders.

Run: `$PY -m pytest -q`
Expected: `507 passed, 1 skipped` (`test_a_step_is_sixty_four_sequences_of_4096` checks the new value is a power of two).

- [ ] **Step 8: Commit**

```bash

git commit -F - <<'EOF'

**Files:** none, unless the review finds something.

- [ ] **Step 1: Both suites**

Run: `$PY -m pytest -q` and `.venv-cuda/bin/python -m pytest -q`
Expected: `507 passed, 1 skipped` (plus one more if Task 17's contingency (b) added a test) and `508 passed` (likewise).

- [ ] **Step 2: Whole-branch review**

Use superpowers:requesting-code-review on `master..m1` against the spec and this plan. Fix what it finds, with a test for each correctness fix, and commit.

- [ ] **Step 3: Hand off**

Tell the user that M1's code is done. Give them the steps that are theirs (design §12), in the order of the next section. Do not merge, set secrets or queue runs yourself.

---

## After the plan: the user's steps (design §12)

1. **The Hugging Face token, before batch 0.** Create a token that can write to and create repos in your namespace, then run `gh secret set HF_TOKEN`. Set `hf_repo: <your-hf-user>/deluge-runs` in `configs/chain/export.yaml` and commit it.
2. **The data:** done in Task 16. **The smoke files:** done in Task 17.
3. **Merge `m1` into `master`**, once the suites and the smoke pass. The chain's bot commits state to `master`, so pull first:
   ```bash
   git checkout master && git pull --rebase && git merge --no-ff m1 && git push
   ```
4. **Queue batch 0, the rehearsal.** Append to `configs/chain/runs.yaml` on `master`, then push:
   ```yaml
     - name: rehearse-m1
       model: configs/ablations/A0_scan_only.yaml
       train: configs/train/rehearse.yaml
       accelerator: gpu
       data: heylaine/deluge-long4k:train.bin
       export: true
   ```
   It passes when all of these hold:
   - `.github/chain/state.json` lists it in `done` (not skipped), with `"export": true`;
   - `docs/results/runs/rehearse-m1/` holds `final_eval.json`, `evals.csv` (rows at steps 40 and 80) and `train.csv`;
   - `train.csv`'s `peak_reserved_gib` stays under the 13 GiB the smoke used;
   - `export.json` exists and the checkpoint is on Hugging Face;
   - `python scripts/m1_verdict.py --dry-run rehearse-m1` prints the run.

   If any of these fails, fix it and rerun as `rehearse-m1-2`.
5. **Queue batch 1, the A0 gate** (about one week of quota):
   ```yaml
     - name: m1-a0-cfc-s1
       model: configs/ablations/A0_scan_only.yaml
       train: configs/train/screen.yaml
       accelerator: gpu
       data: heylaine/deluge-long4k:train.bin
       export: true
     - name: m1-a0-xfmr
       model: configs/ablations/A0_transformer_baseline.yaml
       train: configs/train/screen.yaml
       accelerator: gpu
       data: heylaine/deluge-long4k:train.bin
       export: true
     - name: m1-a0-cfc-s2
       model: configs/ablations/A0_scan_only.yaml
       train: configs/train/screen_seed2.yaml
       accelerator: gpu
       data: heylaine/deluge-long4k:train.bin
       export: true
   ```
   Once `m1-a0-cfc-s1` has exported, re-derive the budgeting table in `docs/kaggle.md` from its `train.csv` `tokens_per_second`. When all three have exported, run `python scripts/m1_verdict.py a0`, look at the curves (`train.csv`, `evals.csv`), and write the A0 prose in `docs/results/m1.md`. If A0 is "stable but behind", diagnose it as design §8.3 says before rerunning anything. Queue batch 2 if A0 passes, or if it is carried forward. A run rerun under a new name (say `m1-a0-xfmr-2`) stands in for its role with `--run m1-a0-xfmr=m1-a0-xfmr-2`, on `a0` and again on `a1`, whose early read is in units of A0's noise.
6. **Queue batch 2, A1** (about seven weeks):
   ```yaml
     - name: m1-a1s-conv
       model: configs/ablations/A1_conv_baseline.yaml
       train: configs/train/screen.yaml
       accelerator: gpu
       data: heylaine/deluge-long4k:train.bin
       export: true
     - name: m1-a1s-lru
       model: configs/ablations/A1_lru_baseline.yaml
       train: configs/train/screen.yaml
       accelerator: gpu
       data: heylaine/deluge-long4k:train.bin
       export: true
     - name: m1-a1d-cfc-s1
       model: configs/ablations/A0_scan_only.yaml
       train: configs/train/dev.yaml
       accelerator: gpu
       data: heylaine/deluge-long4k:train.bin
       export: true
     - name: m1-a1d-cfc-s2
       model: configs/ablations/A0_scan_only.yaml
       train: configs/train/dev_seed2.yaml
       accelerator: gpu
       data: heylaine/deluge-long4k:train.bin
       export: true
     - name: m1-a1d-cfc-s3
       model: configs/ablations/A0_scan_only.yaml
       train: configs/train/dev_seed3.yaml
       accelerator: gpu
       data: heylaine/deluge-long4k:train.bin
       export: true
     - name: m1-a1d-conv-s1
       model: configs/ablations/A1_conv_baseline.yaml
       train: configs/train/dev.yaml
       accelerator: gpu
       data: heylaine/deluge-long4k:train.bin
       export: true
     - name: m1-a1d-conv-s2
       model: configs/ablations/A1_conv_baseline.yaml
       train: configs/train/dev_seed2.yaml
       accelerator: gpu
       data: heylaine/deluge-long4k:train.bin
       export: true
     - name: m1-a1d-conv-s3
       model: configs/ablations/A1_conv_baseline.yaml
       train: configs/train/dev_seed3.yaml
       accelerator: gpu
       data: heylaine/deluge-long4k:train.bin
       export: true
   ```
   When all six binding runs have exported, run `python scripts/m1_verdict.py a1`, passing the same `--run` substitutions as for `a0`, plus any for reruns in batch 2. An early run that never exported is left out of the early table and does not hold up the verdict. Write the binding verdict's prose and the caveats in `docs/results/m1.md`. M1 is done (design §1) when `docs/results/m1.md` states both verdicts and `docs/cfc-hybrid-spec.md` matches what M1 built. GO leads to M2; NO-GO leads to spec §11's pivot.

---

## Execution notes

What was built departs from the task blocks above in the places below. Each
was ruled during execution, and the blocks are left as they were approved:
the commits are the record of what changed and why.

- **Task 2, the inter-chunk loop** (cb32daa). The loop body indexed
  `within[:, :, j, -1]` and `carry[:, :, j]` per chunk, and each select's
  backward allocated a full-size zero gradient, so the scan's backward was
  O(N·|within|). The chunk ends are unbound before the loop. Outputs and
  gradients are bit-identical.
- **Task 6, `GatedScanBase`** (44fb953). The CfC and LRU mixers held two
  copies of their six shared parameters, their init and their decay. A1 reads
  CfC − LRU as what the conv is worth, which holds only if both arms start
  from the same decay, gate and readout. One base class in `cfc.py` owns
  them: CfC adds the conv, and neither arm subclasses the other. Names,
  `state_dict` keys, the seeded init and the outputs are unchanged, and a test
  pins W_f, W_g and h0 at zero on both arms.
- **Task 8, the output-projection std** (0a05fd7, 501e4ed). The uniform-loss
  test passed with depth scaling broken. `tests/test_model.py` also pins every
  residual output projection (mixer `W_out`/`W_o`, FFN `W_down`) at spec
  §4.4's STD/√(2·n_blocks), written from the formula rather than from
  `init.output_std`, and its comment says what the loss check does and does
  not detect.
- **Task 10a, the seeded init** (6b04c38), a task this plan does not have.
  `build_backend` built the model before anything seeded torch, so the
  initial weights followed neither the run nor its seed, against design §8.1.
  It seeds with `cfg.seed` before the build.
- **Task 14, a run with no in-run evals, and the dry run** (aae4211). A run
  with no in-run evals is unstable, with that as the named cause: §7.3 needs
  all four criteria to hold. `--dry-run` prints the run without judging it
  (§8.4).
- **Task 16's commands, after the final review** (cf59435, 339b0a7).
  - Step 2: install the versions the prep kernel pins,
    `huggingface_hub==1.33.0 pyarrow==25.0.1 tokenizers==0.23.2`
    (`PIP_PINS` in `scripts/kaggle_prep.py`).
  - Step 3: `measure` takes `--tokenizer-revision` and prints a
    `tokenizer_revision` sha beside `revision`. Its token_count pass reads
    about 80 MiB in about 10,000 range requests, roughly 40 min on a slow
    link, not "tens of MB" in "minutes".
  - Steps 6 and 8: `long` also takes `--tokenizer-revision <the
    tokenizer_revision Step 3 printed>`, and `--revision` is always resolved
    to a sha. The kernel passes `--builder-commit` itself (the `--commit` it
    checks out). The provenance adds `tokenizer_revision`,
    `tokenizers_version`, `builder_commit`, `train_tokens_target` and
    `documents.short`; check that `revision` and `tokenizer_revision` are
    40-hex shas and `builder_commit` is the rendered `--commit`.
  - Step 8: a build that runs out of documents before `--train-tokens` exits
    non-zero, so the kernel version errors, and moves nothing into place: no
    `train.bin`, `heldout.bin` or `provenance.json`, only `.tmp` files. The
    "if `tokens.train` is short" case is that failure; the fix is unchanged.

The final review's other fixes, the commits after 95c9ef1, also change code
that the blocks of Tasks 9 to 14 show. Their commit messages say what changed
and why.
